\begin{abstract}
\noindent\textbf{Paper type:} Conditional theorem closure paper. Establishes the substrate-level chirality matrix element $|M| = \chi/6 = \phig^{-3}/6 \approx 0.0394$ as a \emph{cross-sector substrate-physics handle} shared across three observable manifestations of substrate chirality --- mass-mixing (K3-doublet), electroweak V--A (W-bracelet), and electromagnetic handedness (qDP/eDP polarization) --- under a conditional-closure framework on twelve foundational inputs and four CPP axioms.

Conscious Point Physics (CPP) registers chirality as a substrate-wide phenomenon~\cite{abshier_axiom_registry,abshier_theorem_registry,abshier_research_frontier}: a single substrate primitive --- a preferred 4D direction $\hat{n}$ in the ambient $\mathbb{R}^4$ where the 600-cell substrate sits (FI-C-RC-1) --- sources the chirality content of every parity-sensitive observable in the framework, with the vertex-aligned reading $\hat{n} = v_{\text{host}}$ (FI-C-RC-2) identifying the substrate's local geometric structure as the icosahedral first-shell of a 600-cell host vertex. The substrate residual symmetry is $\Stab_{\Hfour}(v_{\text{host}}) = H_3 = I_h$ of order 120; the magnitude $|\chi| = \phig^{-3}$ is derived from the perturbative-distance-ratio constraint on $\hat{n}$-induced edge perturbations~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch}, replacing v1.0's free magnitude postulate. v2.0 derives the chirality matrix element on three substrate objects on the host vertex's first-shell icosahedron --- the K3-doublet of charged-lepton substrate states (3-vertex triangular face); the W-bracelet hosting the V--A coupling (6-vertex Petrie hexagon); the qDP/eDP linear-vs-orbital ZBW configuration of Dipole Points (1-vertex / 0-vertex first-shell subsets per SM-2~\cite{abshier_sm2}) --- with identical numerical content:
\[
|M^{\Kthree}| \;=\; |M^W| \;=\; |M^{qDP}| \;=\; \frac{\chi}{6} \;=\; \frac{\phig^{-3}}{6} \;\approx\; 0.0394.
\]
The three-way unification at substrate level is mediated by a four-step proof chain --- local-$I_h$-preservation at $v_{\text{host}}$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation}); Substrate-Locality Unification on first-shell substrate objects (Corollary~\ref{cor:substrate_locality_unification}); sector-specific cage-shell factor $d_\Gamma/V_{\text{cage}} = 2/12 = 1/6$ on the shared icosahedral cage; sector-specific pairing-convention identification via sector-specific $\mathbb{Z}_2$ generator and matter-doublet structure --- producing structurally distinct mechanisms with identical numerical magnitude. The three sectors are registered as programme-level theorems THEO-CAP-1 (K3-doublet; Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we}, registered Patch 0397 Session 103 ahead of paper publication), THEO-SD-CHIR-1 (K3-doublet $\leftrightarrow$ W-bracelet cross-sector unification; Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_1}, registered Patch 0434 Session 132), and THEO-SD-CHIR-2 (qDP/eDP closure; Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_2}, registered Patch 0440 Session 133)~\cite{abshier_theorem_registry}. End-to-end numerical verification matches to machine precision $10^{-17}$ at every checkpoint of the K3-doublet derivation chain (Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we}); cross-sector calculations are rigorous via standard character-table group theory.

The single primary empirical prediction is the parity-violation asymmetry $\DeltapLR = \chi/6 \approx 0.0394$, validated within $2\%$ of the empirical anchor $\DeltapLR \approx 0.04$ inferred from the baryon asymmetry $\eta_B$ via leptogenesis. The cross-sector predictions for the W-bracelet V--A coupling and qDP/eDP chiral-polarity-bias at substrate level are substrate-physics handles feeding sector-specific Layer 4 continuum-EFT kinematic projections to observable scales (registered as open work in the OPEN-FP-SF-2-CHIR and SM-2 v2.0+ programmes~\cite{abshier_research_frontier}). The paper makes explicit at theorem level: the substrate-locality theorem (Local-$I_h$-Preservation at the host vertex); the Substrate-Locality Unification corollary across first-shell substrate objects; the chirality-eigenvalue matching principle yielding amplitude factor $\chi$ in each sector; the cage-shell averaging principle yielding $1/6$ on the shared icosahedral cage; the pairing-convention identifications via icosahedral-center inversion for the W-bracelet and combined-$CP$ for the qDP/eDP sector; the empirical prediction $\DeltapLR$. The paper makes explicit as out of scope at v2.0: observable manifestations (iv) thermodynamic causal arrow and (v) cosmological-vacuum asymmetry of the OPEN-SD-CHIR-PRIMITIVE umbrella (future-window work); the Capotauro nucleation event (OPEN-SM-4 sub-claim (a) and Q7 cosmological-timing sub-questions of the Reading C closure trajectory); the first-principles derivation of $\hat{n}$ itself from CPP primitive axioms (Q1$'$+Q1$'$.A Layer 3 promotion; future-window dynamical/causal/energetic derivation); the PMNS reactor mixing angle $\sin^2\theta_{13}$ (re-scoped to SF-2 v2.0+ flagship per v1.0); the CP-violating phase $\delta_{CP}$ and the baryon asymmetry $\eta_B$ (downstream sub-claim work); sector-specific Layer 4 continuum-EFT kinematic projections of the substrate handle $\chi/6$ to observable scales (SF-2 V--A coupling at the massless helicity limit; SM-2 chiral-polarity-bias EFT continuum-limit calculation). The paper's conditional-theorem-closure framework registers two new Reading C foundational inputs --- FI-C-RC-1 (primitive 4D direction $\hat{n}$) and FI-C-RC-2 (vertex-aligned reading $\hat{n} = v_{\text{host}}$ at Layer 2 via three converging arguments) --- in addition to the ten foundational inputs of v1.0 (FI-C-1 through FI-C-10).
\end{abstract}

\medskip\noindent
\textbf{Keywords:} chirality matrix element, K3-doublet, W-bracelet, qDP/eDP, three-way cross-sector unification, Wigner-Eckart theorem, tribimaximal mixing, $\Dsix$ stabilizer, $D_{5d}$ stabilizer, combined $CP$ operation, icosahedral-center inversion, primitive 4D direction, vertex-aligned Reading C, 600-cell substrate, substrate-locality theorem, local-$I_h$-preservation, Substrate-Locality Unification, Conscious Point Physics, substrate primitive chirality, OPEN-SD-CHIR-PRIMITIVE umbrella, parity violation, $\Delta p_{LR}$, OPEN-SM-4, Capotauro mechanism, conditional theorem closure, flagship paper.

\bigskip\noindent
\textbf{Plain Language Summary:}
This paper shows that one specific number computed from the geometry of a four-dimensional substrate --- $|M| = \chi/6 \approx 0.0394$ --- governs three apparently different physical phenomena at the deepest level of the framework: the parity-violation asymmetry that distinguishes left-handed and right-handed behavior at the electroweak scale, the chirality of the V--A coupling that mediates weak-force interactions through $W^\pm$ bosons, and the electromagnetic handedness that produces the up/down asymmetry of quark charges. The unification works as follows. The substrate is postulated to have a primitive 4D direction $\hat{n}$ --- a single arrow built into the four-dimensional space where the substrate sits, on the same epistemic footing as the existence of the substrate itself. The magnitude $|\chi| = \phig^{-3} \approx 0.236$ is derived from a structural constraint: the substrate's chirality must be small enough not to disturb the geometric structure of the 600-cell, and the natural distance ratios of the 600-cell (powers of the golden ratio) provide a discrete set of candidate magnitudes; $\phig^{-3}$ is the first that satisfies the constraint. The number $\chi$ is then transmitted from the substrate level to three different observable contexts through a common four-step machinery: a structural theorem showing that the local geometry around any 600-cell vertex is preserved at first order under the substrate's chirality perturbation; an averaging operation on the icosahedral cage of 12 first-shell vertices around the host; and a sector-specific pairing operation that selects how each sector's matter states couple to the chirality content. Despite using structurally distinct pairing operations in the three sectors (3D inversion for the K3-doublet of charged-lepton states; 4D icosahedral-center inversion for the W-bracelet; combined $CP$ operation for the qDP/eDP sector), all three sectors produce the same number $\chi/6 \approx 0.0394$ at the substrate level. The primary empirical match is the parity-violation asymmetry $\DeltapLR \approx 0.04$, matched within $2\%$; the substrate handle for the W-bracelet V--A coupling and qDP/eDP chiral-polarity-bias feed two further Layer 4 calculations registered as future work. The paper is explicit about what is and is not derived: it derives the magnitude of the chirality and its propagation to three observable contexts, but does not derive the substrate-physics mechanism that produces the primitive 4D direction itself (that is the deepest open work of the programme). Two further manifestations of the substrate chirality --- the thermodynamic causal arrow and the cosmological-vacuum asymmetry --- are predicted to follow the same pattern with sector-specific pairing operations yet to be identified.

\section{Introduction and Strategic Frame}\label{sec:introduction}

\subsection{The Capotauro problem in CPP}\label{sec:cap_problem}

\textbf{This is a conditional theorem closure paper.}\footnote{The conditional-closure framework is established by SF-4 v4.x and SF-2 v1.0 in the CPP corpus~\cite{abshier_conditional_closure}; the discipline distinguishes theorem-level claims that hold rigorously under explicit foundational-input assumptions from claims at structural-argument or empirical-anchor level.} Its primary deliverable at v2.0 is a closed-form derivation of the substrate-level chirality matrix element $|M| = \chi/6 = \phig^{-3}/6 \approx 0.0394$ on three substrate objects --- the K3-doublet (mass-mixing sector), the W-bracelet (electroweak V--A sector), and the qDP/eDP polarization (electromagnetic-handedness sector) --- with identical numerical magnitude despite structurally distinct sector-specific mechanisms. The derivation is theorem-level under a foundational-input stack (FI-C-1 through FI-C-10 from v1.0 plus FI-C-RC-1 and FI-C-RC-2 newly registered at v2.0) plus four CPP axioms (AXIM-1, AXIM-2, AXIM-4, AXIM-7); first-principles closure of the foundational inputs themselves is registered as open work in \S\ref{sec:open_work}. The paper's empirical content is one primary prediction, $\DeltapLR = |M^{\Kthree}|$, validated within $2\%$ of the leptogenesis back-derived empirical anchor $\sim 0.04$ (\S\ref{sec:dplr}). Sector-specific Layer 4 continuum-EFT projections of the W-bracelet and qDP/eDP substrate handles are registered as open work pending the SF-2 v2.0+ and SM-2 v2.0+ flagships respectively.

The Standard Model treats parity violation as an empirical input: the $\sin^2\theta_W$ Weinberg angle, the V--A coupling structure, the CP-violating phase $\delta_{CP}$ in the PMNS matrix, and the cosmological baryon asymmetry $\eta_B$ all originate in observation and are inserted into the Lagrangian by hand. Conscious Point Physics (CPP)~\cite{abshier_axiom_registry,abshier_theorem_registry} proposes a substrate-level mechanism --- the \emph{Capotauro mechanism} --- whereby a chirality-activation phenomenon at the 600-cell substrate level produces these parity-violation signatures structurally rather than empirically. The mechanism was proposed in the foundational Capotauro nucleation paper~\cite{abshier_grok_capotauro_2025} but with the chirality magnitude $\chi$ left empirically anchored ($\chi \approx 0.04$ from leptogenesis back-derivation) and the chirality matrix element $|M|$ on the substrate-level K3-doublet of charged-lepton states left underived. The CPP programme registered the mechanism's theorem-level closure as OPEN-SM-4 in the Research Frontier~\cite{abshier_research_frontier}.

The v1.0 paper closed OPEN-SM-4 sub-claim (c) --- the chirality matrix element $|M^{\Kthree}|$ on the K3-doublet --- at theorem level via the eight-step closure trajectory documented in the working sketches.\footnote{v1.0 closure trajectory Sessions 87--102; closure declared Session 102; programme-level registration as theorem THEO-CAP-1 at Patch 0397 Session 103~\cite{abshier_theorem_registry}, the first programme-level theorem registered ahead of its own flagship paper publication in the CPP corpus; v1.0 SHIPPED at Patch 0415 Session 110.} The v2.0 paper extends this closure in two structurally substantive directions. First, it grounds the substrate primitive chirality at a sharper epistemic position: $\chi$ is no longer postulated with free magnitude, but \emph{derived} from a primitive 4D direction $\hat{n}$ in the ambient $\mathbb{R}^4$ where the 600-cell substrate sits (FI-C-RC-1, on the same epistemic footing as the existence of the substrate itself) under the vertex-aligned reading $\hat{n} = v_{\text{host}}$ (FI-C-RC-2, established at Layer 2 via three converging arguments: kinematic, energetic, and substrate-physics; \S\ref{sec:h4_i4}). The magnitude $|\chi| = \phig^{-3}$ is then derived from the perturbative-distance-ratio constraint on $\hat{n}$-induced edge perturbations (\S\ref{sec:order_parameter}). Second, the v2.0 paper extends the K3-only matrix element $|M^{\Kthree}| = \chi/6$ to a three-way cross-sector unification at substrate level: the same substrate chirality $\chi$ feeds the K3-doublet (mass-mixing sector, THEO-CAP-1), the W-bracelet (electroweak V--A sector, THEO-SD-CHIR-1), and the qDP/eDP polarization (electromagnetic-handedness sector, THEO-SD-CHIR-2) with identical numerical magnitude $\chi/6 \approx 0.0394$ in each sector, via structurally distinct sector-specific cage-shell averaging operations on the shared icosahedral cage at the host vertex's first shell. The cross-sector unification is registered under the OPEN-SD-CHIR-PRIMITIVE umbrella in the Research Frontier~\cite{abshier_research_frontier} (umbrella registered Patch 0422 Session 130).\footnote{v2.0 cross-sector extension trajectory Sessions 87--133 (Reading C closure trajectory; six Q-questions Q1--Q7 with Q1, Q1$'$, Q1$'$.A, Q2, Q3, Q4, Q5, Q5-PAIRING, Q6, Q6-PAIRING resolved at Layer 3 or Layer 2 conservative; Q7 cosmological-timing remains open at sketch level); programme-level theorem registrations: THEO-SD-CHIR-1 at Patch 0434 Session 132 (K3-doublet $\leftrightarrow$ W-bracelet unification); THEO-SD-CHIR-2 at Patch 0440 Session 133 (qDP/eDP closure); v2.0 paper drafting completed Session 134 via single-window collapse of multi-session planned budget into Patches 0444--0448 plus 0450 (the 0449 slot was assigned to a parallel organizational workstream).}

\subsection{What this paper delivers}\label{sec:what_delivers}

At conditional theorem closure level (per the conditional-closure framework~\cite{abshier_conditional_closure} established by SF-4 v4.x and SF-2 v1.0):

\begin{itemize}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item \textbf{Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation} (Substrate-Locality Theorem: Local-$I_h$-Preservation at the Host Vertex)} (\S\ref{sec:substrate_locality}): under vertex-aligned Reading C with $\hat{n} = v_{\text{host}}$, the local $I_h$ symmetry of the first-shell icosahedron at the host vertex is preserved at $\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)$ in 4D ambient under the substrate edge-perturbation mechanism. Corollary~\ref{cor:substrate_locality_unification} (Substrate-Locality Unification on first-shell substrate objects) and Lemma~\ref{lem:chi_epsilon} (substrate-level identification $\chi \equiv \epsilon = \phig^{-3}$) provide the structural foundation for the three-way cross-sector unification.
    \item \textbf{Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we} (Composite Capotauro Wigner-Eckart Theorem; THEO-CAP-1)} (\S\ref{sec:composite_we}): the chirality matrix element on the tribimaximal-aligned K3-doublet of charged-lepton substrate states is $|M^{\Kthree}| = \chi/6 = \phig^{-3}/6 \approx 0.0394$. Inherited verbatim from v1.0; underlying derivation chain (\S\ref{sec:k3_doublet}--\S\ref{sec:composite_we}) preserved at content level.
    \item \textbf{Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_1} (Cross-Sector Substrate Chirality Unification; THEO-SD-CHIR-1)} (\S\ref{sec:w_bracelet_sector}): the K3-doublet $\leftrightarrow$ W-bracelet cross-sector unification at substrate level, $|M^{\Kthree}| = |M^W| = \chi/6 \approx 0.0394$, via Substrate-Locality Unification on the shared icosahedral cage and pairing-convention identification via the icosahedral-center inversion $\zeta^W$ in 4D (Definition~\ref{def:zeta_w}).
    \item \textbf{Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_2} (qDP/eDP Sector Substrate Chirality Closure; THEO-SD-CHIR-2)} (\S\ref{sec:qdp_edp_sector}): the qDP/eDP electromagnetic-handedness manifestation at substrate level, $|M^{qDP}| = \chi/6 \approx 0.0394$, via antipodal-pair refinement to $D_{5d}$ stabilizer and pairing-convention identification via the combined-$CP$ operation $\zeta^{qDP}$ (Definition~\ref{def:zeta_qdp}).
    \item \textbf{Three-way cross-sector unification at substrate level} (\S\ref{sec:three_way_unification}): $|M^{\Kthree}| = |M^W| = |M^{qDP}| = \chi/6 \approx 0.0394$ across three structurally distinct sector-specific mechanisms with distinct stabilizer types ($D_{3d}$ / $\Dsix$ Petrie / $D_{5d}$) and distinct $\zeta$ generators (3D inversion / 4D icosahedral-center inversion / combined $CP$). Single substrate parameter $\chi = \phig^{-3}$ feeds all three sectors.
    \item \textbf{Primary empirical prediction}: the parity-violation asymmetry $\DeltapLR = |M^{\Kthree}| \approx 0.0394$, validated within $2\%$ of the observed $\DeltapLR \approx 0.04$ (\S\ref{sec:dplr}). Cross-sector predictions for W-bracelet V--A coupling magnitude and qDP/eDP chiral-polarity-bias magnitude at substrate level feed sector-specific Layer 4 continuum-EFT projections registered as open work.
    \item \textbf{Two new foundational inputs}: FI-C-RC-1 (primitive 4D direction $\hat{n}$ in ambient $\mathbb{R}^4$; postulated on same epistemic footing as the existence of the 600-cell substrate) and FI-C-RC-2 (vertex-aligned reading $\hat{n} = v_{\text{host}}$; established at Layer 2 via three converging arguments). Registered in addition to v1.0's FI-C-1 through FI-C-10. The cage-shell averaging factor FI-C-10 inherited from v1.0 is now seen to apply uniformly across all three sectors at the shared icosahedral cage.
    \item \textbf{Honest scope-limitation framing extended to v2.0}: explicit re-scoping of $\sin^2\theta_{13}$ derivation to SF-2 v2.0+ flagship (preserved from v1.0); future-window enumeration of OPEN-SD-CHIR-PRIMITIVE umbrella manifestations (iv) thermodynamic causal arrow and (v) cosmological-vacuum asymmetry; Q5-PAIRING and Q6-PAIRING register-then-resolve patterns documented as templates for cross-sector closure work (\S\ref{sec:w_zeta_inversion}, \S\ref{sec:qdp_zeta_definition}).
\end{itemize}

The $\chi/6 \approx 0.0394$ result is not the outcome of parameter selection to match the empirical anchor $\sim 0.04$. The framework's structural inputs --- the substrate primitive chirality $\phig^{-3}$ (derived from the perturbative-distance-ratio constraint on $\hat{n}$-induced edge perturbations); the cage-shell averaging factor $1/6 = d_\Gamma/V_{\text{cage}}$ on the shared icosahedral cage; the substrate-physics parameter $b = \chi/\sqrt{3}$ from chirality-eigenvalue matching --- are independently fixed by foundational inputs and structural facts with zero free parameters across all three sectors. The structural-constraint argument is developed in \S\ref{sec:nontriviality}; the visual derivation map for the K3-doublet derivation chain appears in \S\ref{sec:roadmap} (Figure~\ref{fig:derivation_flow}); the empirical match within $2\%$ on the primary prediction confirms the structural prediction rather than motivating parameter fitting. The cross-sector identity of magnitude across structurally distinct $\zeta$ generators and stabilizer types is the v2.0 substantive prediction beyond v1.0's K3-only $|M| = \chi/6$.

\subsection{What this paper does not deliver}\label{sec:what_not}

The conditional closure at v2.0 is partial closure of OPEN-SM-4 plus the first-three-manifestation Layer 3 closure of OPEN-SD-CHIR-PRIMITIVE umbrella. The following are explicitly out of scope at v2.0 and registered as open work in \S\ref{sec:open_work}:

\begin{itemize}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item \textbf{OPEN-SD-CHIR-PRIMITIVE umbrella manifestations (iv) and (v)} (\S\ref{sec:three_way_unification}): (iv) thermodynamic causal arrow and (v) cosmological-vacuum asymmetry as substrate-level chirality manifestations. Future-window work; sector-specific $\zeta$ generators and matter-doublet structures yet to be identified. The umbrella's full prediction is $|M^{\text{(iv)}}| = |M^{\text{(v)}}| = \chi/6$ at substrate level, extending the v2.0 three-way unification to five-way, but the explicit closures await sector-specific identification work.
    \item \textbf{Sub-claim (a) Capotauro nucleation event}: the substrate-dynamical event by which the universe localizes into one specific enantiomorph of the substrate's primitive chirality. Q7 cosmological-timing sub-questions of the Reading C closure trajectory (sketch \S21~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch}; the legacy $\sim 120$ Myr post-Big Bang figure is downstream) remain open at sketch level.
    \item \textbf{First-principles derivation of $\hat{n}$ from CPP primitive axioms}: Q1$'$+Q1$'$.A Layer 3 promotion --- the dynamical, causal, or energetic derivation of FI-C-RC-1 (the primitive 4D direction $\hat{n}$ itself) from primitive CPP axioms (A1, A2, A4, A7) rather than postulating $\hat{n}$ as substrate-foundational. The deepest open work of the v2.0 programme; future-window substrate-physics territory. The v2.0 paper accepts FI-C-RC-1 as substrate-foundational at the same epistemic footing as the existence of the 600-cell substrate itself; Layer 3 promotion of $\hat{n}$ would close the postulate at primitive-axiom level.
    \item \textbf{Q11 $\sin^2\theta_{13}$ derivation}: re-scoped to SF-2 v2.0+ flagship per v1.0 (preserved at v2.0); the linear-vs-quadratic scaling tension that surfaced during the closure trajectory remains unresolved within Capotauro's direct scope. Candidate $\gamma$ numerical conjecture ($\sin^2\theta_{13} = b \cdot m_\perp = \chi/(6\sqrt{3}) \approx 0.0227$) remains a guiding structural observation.
    \item \textbf{$\delta_{CP}$ and $\eta_B$}: downstream observables to be derived in future Capotauro sub-claim work; not v1.0 or v2.0 falsifiers.
    \item \textbf{Sector-specific Layer 4 continuum-EFT kinematic projections}: the substrate handle $\chi/6$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_1} for the W-bracelet V--A coupling feeds (but does not close) SF-2's Layer 4 projection from the substrate-level chirality magnitude to the 75\%-finite-mass-to-100\%-massless-helicity-limit kinematic structure of the V--A current (OPEN-FP-SF-2-CHIR); the substrate handle $\chi/6$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_2} for the qDP/eDP sector feeds the SM-2 v2.0+ chiral-polarity-bias EFT continuum-limit calculation closing to numerical Linear-ZBW-on-$-$qCP stabilization energy at observable scales. Both are registered as open Layer 4 work in the SF-2 and SM-2 v2.0+ programmes.
    \item \textbf{FI-C-10 first-principles verification}: derivation from primitive CPP axioms (A3 DI-bit propagation + A4 Nexus connectivity) of the cage-shell averaging factor's structural foundation; SS-corpus territory; inherited from v1.0 open work.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Position in the SF-line and broader programme}\label{sec:position}

The Capotauro paper is the third flagship paper to ship at v1.0 after SF-4~\cite{abshier_sf4} and SF-2~\cite{abshier_sf2}.\footnote{SF-4 v1.0 shipped at Session 54; SF-2 v1.0 shipped at Session 83; Capotauro v1.0 shipped at Session 110 Patch 0415.} v2.0 makes it the first flagship paper in the CPP corpus to undergo a substantive v2.0 extension --- not a polish or reviewer-feedback revision, but a structural extension that grounds an existing foundational input ($\chi$ at FI-C-9, v1.0 free magnitude postulate) at a sharper epistemic position (FI-C-RC-1 + FI-C-RC-2, derived magnitude) and extends the v1.0 single-sector closure to a multi-sector unification under an umbrella registration. The paper is also the first to use the THEO-CAP-N + THEO-SD-CHIR-N theorem-naming convention, distinguishing sector-specific theorems (CAP-N) from cross-sector unification theorems (SD-CHIR-N) under a single flagship paper's umbrella; the convention templates future cross-sector flagship work in the CPP corpus.

The paper continues to be the first flagship paper outside the SF-N numerical convention --- the THEO-CAP-N and THEO-SD-CHIR-N naming for the Capotauro theorem chain reflects its flagship-paper-pending status across the closure trajectory and is preserved at v2.0 to maintain the programme's archival traceability. The paper is also the first to register a programme-level theorem (THEO-CAP-1) ahead of its own paper outline (Session 103 Patch 0397; paper v1.0 SHIPPED at Patch 0415); v2.0 extends this pattern with THEO-SD-CHIR-1 (Patch 0434) and THEO-SD-CHIR-2 (Patch 0440) registered programme-level Sessions 132+133 before this v2.0 paper drafting Session 134. The ``registered ahead of paper'' pattern is now established as the CPP corpus's standard for substrate-physics theorem work prior to flagship-paper venue.

\subsection{Cross-sector unification as front-line structural claim}\label{sec:cross_sector}

v1.0 included a sidebar note on the closure's compounding value across five CPP sectors --- SM-2, SM-4, SM-5, SF-2, SF-4 --- as a future-work pointer. v2.0 elevates the cross-sector content to the paper's \emph{front-line structural claim}: the same substrate-vacuum chirality magnitude $\chi = \phig^{-3}$ feeds every parity-sensitive observable in the framework, with substantive flagship-paper-level closure now achieved for three observable manifestations (mass-mixing K3-doublet, electroweak V--A W-bracelet, electromagnetic-handedness qDP/eDP) under the three-way unification of Eq.~\eqref{eq:three_way_unification}. Two further manifestations (thermodynamic causal arrow, cosmological-vacuum asymmetry) are predicted under the OPEN-SD-CHIR-PRIMITIVE umbrella to follow the same three-step closure pattern with sector-specific $\zeta$ generators yet to be identified, completing a five-way unification at full programme closure.

The structural mechanism for cross-sector unification is the Substrate-Locality Theorem (\S\ref{sec:substrate_locality}, Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation}): under vertex-aligned Reading C with $\hat{n} = v_{\text{host}}$, the substrate's local $I_h$ symmetry at the first-shell icosahedron of the host vertex is preserved at $\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)$ in 4D ambient, with isotropic radial scaling on host-to-first-shell edges and zero perturbation on first-shell-to-first-shell edges. Any substrate object built from first-shell vertices of the host (the K3-base, the W-bracelet, the qDP 1-vertex subset, the eDP trivial subset, and substrate objects relevant to future-window manifestations (iv) and (v)) inherits the substrate-level chirality magnitude $\chi \equiv \epsilon = \phig^{-3}$ from the same identification (Corollary~\ref{cor:substrate_locality_unification}, Lemma~\ref{lem:chi_epsilon}). The three-way unification of Eq.~\eqref{eq:three_way_unification} is the first concrete demonstration that this structural unification at the substrate level translates to identical numerical magnitude at the matrix-element-content level across structurally distinct sector-specific mechanisms.

\subsection{Strict-C inheritance discipline}\label{sec:strict_c}

This paper distinguishes claims at three rigor levels per the strict-C inheritance discipline established by SS-7~\cite{abshier_ss7}, SS-8, SS-9~\cite{abshier_ss9}, SF-4, and SF-2. \emph{Theorem level}: derived rigorously from the foundational input stack plus CPP axioms with explicit Wigner-Eckart factorization (Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation}; Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we}; Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_1}; Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_2}). \emph{FI-inherited level}: accepted as foundational inputs to the closure (FI-C-1 through FI-C-10 from v1.0; FI-C-RC-1 + FI-C-RC-2 added at v2.0), flagged explicitly in \S\ref{sec:fi_summary}. \emph{Structural-argument level}: supported by structural observations but not yet at theorem-level rigor (the $\sin^2\theta_{13}$ candidate $\gamma$ structural observation in \S\ref{sec:sin2theta13}). The conditional-closure framework~\cite{abshier_conditional_closure} routes all three into a single closure declaration: theorem-level claims hold rigorously under the FI-inherited assumptions; structural-argument-level claims are reviewer-visible and registered as future work.

v2.0 introduces an additional epistemic discipline pattern: the \emph{register-then-resolve} cycle for open structural sub-questions surfaced during cross-sector extension. When the cross-sector pattern surfaces a structural question that the original sector's analytical scope did not require resolving (e.g., Q5-PAIRING for the W-bracelet's pairing-convention $\mathbb{Z}_2$ generator analog of K3's $\zeta$; Q6-PAIRING for the qDP/eDP sector's analog), the responsible move is to register the question explicitly with candidate resolutions enumerated and to close at Layer 2 conservative composite (full Layer 3 promotion deferred) until the structural question is resolved in a subsequent patch. The pattern was applied for Q5-PAIRING (registered Patch 0428 Session 131; resolved Patch 0429 Session 131 via icosahedral-center inversion $\zeta^W$, \S\ref{sec:w_zeta_inversion}) and Q6-PAIRING (registered Patch 0438 Session 133; resolved Patch 0439 Session 133 via combined-$CP$ operation $\zeta^{qDP}$, \S\ref{sec:qdp_zeta_definition}) and is templated in v2.0 for future cross-sector closure work under the OPEN-SD-CHIR-PRIMITIVE umbrella.

Figure~\ref{fig:derivation_flow} summarizes the structural inputs and the eight-step composition that produces the K3-doublet chirality matrix element $|M^{\Kthree}| = \chi/6$ and the primary empirical prediction $\DeltapLR$. The figure preserves v1.0's derivation map structure --- the K3-doublet derivation chain is the historical-priority sector and remains the load-bearing path for the primary empirical prediction. Each arrow represents a structurally fixed transition (foundational input, derivation principle, or composition step) with zero free parameters at any stage; readers tracing the K3-doublet proof at the level of structural ingredients can follow the figure top-to-bottom and match each transition to its corresponding section. The v2.0 cross-sector extensions of the K3-doublet derivation chain to the W-bracelet sector (\S\ref{sec:w_bracelet_sector}, THEO-SD-CHIR-1) and the qDP/eDP sector (\S\ref{sec:qdp_edp_sector}, THEO-SD-CHIR-2) are not redrawn in figure form --- the underlying structural pattern is the four-step proof chain (local-$I_h$-preservation, Substrate-Locality Unification, cage-shell factor identity on shared icosahedral cage, sector-specific pairing-convention identification) detailed textually in the cross-sector sections; a future v2.0+ revision may add cross-sector derivation maps if reviewer feedback recommends them.

\section{Substrate-Vacuum Chirality as Primitive Feature}\label{sec:substrate_vacuum}

This section establishes the substrate-vacuum chirality magnitude $|\chi| = \phig^{-3} \approx 0.236$ from the substrate's primitive 4D direction $\hat{n}$. v2.0 (this section) registers $\hat{n}$ as the substrate's chirality-breaking primitive (foundational input FI-C-RC-1, \S\ref{sec:h4_i4}) and identifies the vertex-aligned reading $\hat{n} = v_{\text{host}}$ at Layer 2 (FI-C-RC-2, \S\ref{sec:h4_i4}) per the Q1$'$ resolution of the Reading C closure trajectory.\footnote{Q1$'$ resolved at Session 126 Patch 0419 (Finding C-W37) by three converging arguments developed in \S\ref{sec:h4_i4}; see~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch} \S11.4 for the full Reading C trajectory derivation.} The chirality magnitude $|\chi| = \phig^{-3}$ is derived from the perturbative-distance-ratio constraint on $\hat{n}$-induced edge perturbations (\S\ref{sec:chi_resolution}); under the local-$I_h$-preservation theorem developed in the working sketch~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch} (sketch \S13, Patch 0424, Finding C-W39; formal theorem statement deferred to a subsequent v2.0 patch), the substrate-level identification $\chi \equiv \epsilon$ (where $\epsilon$ is the $\hat{n}$-perturbation amplitude) is established directly with no intermediate geometric or irrep-theoretic prefactor. FI-C-9, retained from v1.0, registers the magnitude $|\chi| = \phig^{-3}$ at the substrate-physics input level.\footnote{FI-C-9 originally registered at Session 87 Patch 0381. v2.0 retains FI-C-9 as a foundational input; the magnitude is now derived via the chain FI-C-RC-1 $\to$ \S\ref{sec:chi_resolution} (perturbative-distance-ratio constraint) $\to$ Finding C-W39 substrate-level identification $\chi \equiv \epsilon$, rather than postulated as a free magnitude.} The chirality serves as the substrate-physics input to the Capotauro chirality matrix element derivation in \S\ref{sec:composite_we}.

\textbf{Framing choice and the CPP core principle.} A conventional-physics presentation would describe the substrate's chirality via \emph{spontaneous symmetry breaking}: a prior $\Hfour$-symmetric phase, a symmetry-breaking dynamics, an order parameter $\chi$ that emerges in the broken phase. We adopt the alternative framing instead: \emph{the substrate's chirality is the structural consequence of a primitive 4D direction $\hat{n}$ in the substrate's existence-state, not the outcome of a dynamics acting on a prior symmetric state}. The two framings are mathematically equivalent at the level of substrate-physics observables (Remark~\ref{rem:ssb_vs_initial}), but they differ in physical commitment. The CPP programme's core methodological principle is that mathematical descriptions are not themselves physical mechanisms: a derivation that calls something ``spontaneous symmetry breaking'' without specifying the substrate-dynamical primitive that selects one phase over the other is a mathematical description without a physical mechanism. The primitive-feature framing is preserved in v2.0 with one substantive update relative to v1.0: the primitive is the substrate's 4D direction $\hat{n}$ (FI-C-RC-1), not the chirality magnitude $|\chi|$ directly. Under the Reading C framing developed in this section, $|\chi| = \phig^{-3}$ is derived from $\hat{n}$ via the perturbative-distance-ratio constraint (\S\ref{sec:chi_resolution})~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch}. The geometric-chirality candidate mechanism registered as sub-claim (b) future work at v1.0 (\S\ref{sec:open_subclaim_b}) is the operative framework in v2.0: the substrate primitive is the 4D direction $\hat{n}$, and the chirality magnitude is its perturbation amplitude.

The closure derivation in \S\ref{sec:composite_we} uses FI-C-9 ($|\chi| = \phig^{-3}$) as the substrate-physics input. Under v2.0, FI-C-9 is no longer a free postulate at the magnitude level: $|\chi| = \phig^{-3}$ follows from FI-C-RC-1 (primitive 4D direction $\hat{n}$) + FI-C-RC-2 (vertex-aligned reading) + the perturbative-distance-ratio constraint on $\hat{n}$-induced edge perturbations (\S\ref{sec:chi_resolution}). The remaining open work is the first-principles derivation of $\hat{n}$ itself (Q1$'$+Q1$'$.A Layer 2 $\to$ Layer 3 promotion: a dynamical/causal/energetic derivation of vertex-alignment from CPP primitives) and the cosmological-nucleation event that fixes the sign of $\hat{n}$ universe-wide (Q7 sub-questions Q7.1--Q7.4 of the Reading C closure trajectory~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch} sketch \S21). v2.0's deepest open work is therefore not ``what produces the chirality?'' but ``what produces the primitive 4D direction?'' --- a question registered at the same epistemic footing as ``what produces the substrate?'' and tracked at \S\ref{sec:open_subclaim_b}.

\subsection{The $\Hfour \to H_3 = I_h$ vertex-aligned reduction}\label{sec:h4_i4}

The 600-cell is the regular four-dimensional polytope with 120 vertices, 720 edges, 1200 faces, and 600 tetrahedral cells, with full symmetry group $\Hfour$ (the $H_4$ Coxeter group) of order 14{,}400. The rotational subgroup $\Ifour = H_4^+$ has order 7{,}200, an index-2 normal subgroup with quotient $\Hfour / \Ifour = \mathbb{Z}_2$. The two enantiomorphic forms $[\text{600-cell}]_L$ and $[\text{600-cell}]_R$ are related by a reflection element of $\Hfour$ that is not contained in $\Ifour$~\cite{coxeter_1973}. The CPP substrate as an \emph{idealized geometric structure} respects the full $\Hfour$: the polytope itself is racemic with no inherent left/right bias. The substrate's \emph{actual} symmetry, however, is strictly smaller than $\Hfour$, by virtue of the substrate's primitive 4D direction $\hat{n}$.

\textbf{The substrate primitive direction $\hat{n}$ (FI-C-RC-1).} v2.0 frames the substrate's chirality as the structural consequence of a primitive 4D direction $\hat{n}$ in the ambient $\mathbb{R}^4$ where the 600-cell sits.\footnote{FI-C-RC-1 registered Session 134 Patch 0445 (this patch); the Reading C primitive-direction framing traces to the Patch 0414 working sketch ahead of v1.0 SHIP and was developed across Sessions 124--133 of the Reading C closure trajectory~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch}.} The primitive 4D direction is a foundational feature of the substrate's existence-state, on the same epistemic footing as the existence of Conscious Points and Grid Points and the rules governing their interactions. The primitive $\hat{n}$ is a unit vector in $\mathbb{R}^4$; its specific orientation in 4D is not derivable within the framework (any global rotation of the substrate is unobservable from inside the substrate), but the fact that \emph{one} direction is preferred over all others is what produces the substrate's chirality. The sign of $\hat{n}$ (i.e., $\hat{n}$ vs $-\hat{n}$) determines which of the two enantiomorphs $[\text{600-cell}]_{L,R}$ is the actual substrate.

\textbf{The vertex-aligned reading: $\hat{n} = v_{\text{host}}$ (FI-C-RC-2).} The $\Hfour$ action on $\mathbb{R}^4$ has four orbit types corresponding to the four vertex-figure classes of the 600-cell: the 120-vertex orbit, the 720-edge-midpoint orbit, the 1200-face-centroid orbit, and the 600-cell-centroid orbit. The stabilizer of any unit vector in $\Hfour$ depends on which orbit it lies in: $H_3 = I_h$ (order 120) for vertex-aligned $\hat{n}$, $\Dsix$ (order 12) for face-centroid-aligned, $D_5 \times \mathbb{Z}_2$ (order 20) for edge-midpoint-aligned, $S_4$ (order 24) for cell-centroid-aligned.\footnote{Stabilizer derivation via Coxeter parabolic-subgroup theory; see~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch} \S9.3 (Session 124 Patch 0417) for the Q1+Q2 closure analysis establishing these stabilizers.} Among these, only the vertex-aligned and face-centroid-aligned cases produce a substrate residual symmetry consistent with the downstream $\Dsix$-stabilizer machinery at the K3-doublet's location (\S\ref{sec:k3_stabilizer}). The selection between vertex-aligned and face-aligned closes at Layer 2 (structural-argument level) toward \emph{vertex-aligned} by three converging arguments:

\begin{enumerate}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item \textbf{Paper-internal hosting argument.} The Capotauro derivation requires a unique icosahedral cage of $V_{\text{cage}} = 12$ first-shell vertices hosting the K3-doublet (\S\ref{sec:cage_shell_factor}). Under vertex-aligned, $\hat{n}$ uniquely identifies a 600-cell vertex (the \emph{host vertex} $v_{\text{host}}$), and the K3-doublet's icosahedral cage is the first-shell icosahedron of $v_{\text{host}}$ (the 12 nearest-neighbor vertices at vertex-to-vertex distance $1/\phig$ from $v_{\text{host}}$). Under face-aligned, by contrast, each 600-cell face has \emph{two} candidate host vertices --- equivalently, exactly 2 common-neighbor vertices of its 3 face-vertices in the 600-cell edge graph~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch} (sketch \S11.6 combinatorial accounting: 120 hosts $\times$ 20 first-shell triangles each $= 2400$ (host, face) pairs; $2400 / 1200$ distinct faces $= 2.0$ hosts per face, verified to be exactly 2 in all 1200 cases). The face-aligned reading introduces a 2-fold host ambiguity that the paper's framing does not accommodate.
    \item \textbf{Cross-sector unification with SF-2 W-bracelet.} Under vertex-aligned, the SF-2 W-bracelet --- the substrate object hosting the V--A coupling in $W^\pm$-mediated weak interactions --- sits on-axis with $\hat{n}$ at radius $\phig/2$ from the host vertex, with 10 W-bracelets per host vertex corresponding to the 10 Petrie-polygon hexagons of the icosahedral $H_3 = I_h$~\cite{abshier_sf2}.\footnote{Finding C-W36 (Session 125 Patch 0418): W-bracelet centroid coincidence with 600-cell vertex directions under vertex-aligned Reading C; see~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch} \S10.5.} The W-bracelet's $\Dsix$ stabilizer (SF-2 v1.0 Theorem 4.2~\cite{abshier_sf2}) is one of 10 Petrie-hexagon sub-stabilizers of the substrate residual $H_3 = I_h$. The K3-doublet's $\Dsix$ stabilizer (\S\ref{sec:k3_stabilizer}) is one of 10 $D_{3d}$ sub-stabilizers of the same substrate residual $H_3$, corresponding to the 10 3-fold rotation axes of the icosahedron. Both substrate objects emerge as sub-stabilizers of substrate $H_3$ at distinct geometric positions within the host vertex's first shell --- the cross-sector substrate-level unification of K3-doublet chirality and W-bracelet V--A coupling registered under the \emph{OPEN-SD-CHIR-PRIMITIVE} umbrella~\cite{abshier_research_frontier} (umbrella registered Session 127 Patch 0422).
    \item \textbf{SM-1 first-shell icosahedron preserved as substrate local structure.} Under vertex-aligned, the substrate's local geometric structure at $v_{\text{host}}$ retains the SM-1 first-shell icosahedron as its fundamental structure ($H_3 = I_h$ residual symmetry around each 600-cell vertex direction, $z = 12$ coordination); this is the standard CPP geometric framework~\cite{abshier_sm1}. Preserving the SM-1 first-shell icosahedron under Reading C is a consistency argument with the rest of the CPP corpus; face-aligned would break the local-$I_h$ structure non-trivially.
\end{enumerate}

The three arguments converge: \emph{$\hat{n} = v_{\text{host}}$ for some 600-cell vertex $v_{\text{host}}$} (FI-C-RC-2).\footnote{FI-C-RC-2 registered Session 134 Patch 0445 (this patch) at Layer 2 (structural-argument level). Layer 3 closure --- a dynamical/causal/energetic derivation of vertex-alignment from CPP primitives, or an observable falsifier distinguishing vertex-aligned and face-aligned predictions at empirical level --- remains future-window work, registered as Q1$'$+Q1$'$.A in the Reading C closure trajectory~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch} (sketch \S11.4, Patch 0419 Session 126).} The substrate's actual residual symmetry under FI-C-RC-1 + FI-C-RC-2 is therefore
\[
\Stab_{\Hfour}(v_{\text{host}}) \;=\; H_3 \;=\; I_h \quad \text{(order 120)},
\]
where $I_h$ is the full icosahedral group (60 rotations + 60 rotoreflections), the stabilizer of any 600-cell vertex in the $\Hfour$ Coxeter action. The $\Hfour \to H_3 = I_h$ reduction is Operation B in the v2.0 framing.

\textbf{Operation B implies Operation A: the v1.0 $\Hfour \to \Ifour$ reduction as chirality-only coarsening.} The v1.0 paper presented the substrate's symmetry reduction as $\Hfour \to \Ifour = H_4^+$ of order 7{,}200, the algebraic chirality-only projection that retains all rotations and drops all reflections (Operation A). This is recovered under v2.0 as the chirality-only coarsening of the vertex-aligned reduction:
\[
\underbrace{\Hfour \to H_3 = I_h}_{\text{Operation B (v2.0)}} \;\Longrightarrow\; \underbrace{\Hfour \to \Ifour}_{\text{Operation A (v1.0)}}
\]
The implication runs by projection: the chirality content (left vs right enantiomorph distinction) is captured by the $\Hfour / \Ifour = \mathbb{Z}_2$ quotient, which Operation B's $\Hfour \to H_3 = I_h$ also produces (since $H_3 = I_h$ acts on the orthogonal hyperplane preserving $\hat{n}$'s sign, while reflections that send $\hat{n} \to -\hat{n}$ are excluded from $\Stab_{\Hfour}(v_{\text{host}})$). The v1.0 algebraic reduction is a valid \emph{chirality-only} statement; v2.0's vertex-aligned reduction carries additional structural information --- the K3-doublet's geometric realization as a triangular face of the host vertex's first-shell icosahedron, the W-bracelet's on-axis position, the local $I_h$ residual symmetry at $v_{\text{host}}$, the 10-fold $D_{3d}$ / Petrie-hexagon multiplicity at substrate level --- that the v1.0 algebraic framing did not expose.\footnote{The v1.0 paper's claim that $\Stab_{\Hfour}(\hat{n}) \cong \Ifour$ as a direct algebraic identity was geometrically incomplete: the $\Hfour$ stabilizer of a unit vector depends on the vector's $\Hfour$-orbit (vertex, edge, face, or cell), as enumerated above. This was corrected in the Q1+Q2 closure analysis~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch} (sketch \S9, Patch 0417). The v1.0 paper's downstream $\Dsix$ machinery at the K3-doublet's location (\S\ref{sec:k3_stabilizer}) is \emph{preserved unchanged} under vertex-aligned Reading C: the $\Dsix$ stabilizer is one of 10 $D_{3d}$ sub-stabilizers of substrate $H_3 = I_h$, not the substrate's full residual symmetry. The substrate's racemic-idealized $\Hfour$ description, the substrate's actual residual $H_3 = I_h$ under FI-C-RC-2, and the K3-doublet's location-specific $\Dsix$ sub-stabilizer are three distinct levels of symmetry that v2.0 distinguishes explicitly.}

\subsection{The primitive chirality magnitude $|\chi| = \phig^{-3}$}\label{sec:order_parameter}

\begin{definition}[Substrate primitive chirality magnitude]\label{def:chi_order_parameter}
The substrate chirality magnitude $|\chi|$ is the dimensionless quantity that characterizes the magnitude of the substrate's primitive chirality bias. Its value is
\[
|\chi| = \phig^{-3} \approx 0.236,
\]
where $\phig = (1+\sqrt{5})/2$ is the golden ratio. The sign of $\chi$ (which enantiomorph is selected) and the magnitude of $\chi$ are both fixed by the substrate's primitive 4D direction $\hat{n}$ (FI-C-RC-1, \S\ref{sec:h4_i4}): the sign follows from the sign of $\hat{n}$, the magnitude from the perturbative-distance-ratio constraint on $\hat{n}$-induced edge perturbations (\S\ref{sec:chi_resolution}). FI-C-9 records the magnitude $|\chi| = \phig^{-3}$ at substrate-physics input level for use in the chirality matrix element derivation of \S\ref{sec:composite_we}.
\end{definition}

The value $|\chi| = \phig^{-3}$ is the natural distance-ratio bias of the substrate's chirality primitive, registered as foundational input FI-C-9~\cite{abshier_capotauro_closure_sketch}.\footnote{FI-C-9 registered at Session 87 Patch 0381.} Several features motivate the registration:

\begin{enumerate}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item \textbf{Distance-ratio appearance}. The 600-cell distance-shell structure features $\phig$-graded distances $d^2 \in \{1/\phig^2, 1, \phig^2, \ldots\}$. The $\phig^{-3}$ scale appears as the natural third-tier distance-ratio bias when the substrate's primitive chirality direction selects between enantiomorphic distance-shell assignments at the perturbative scale required by the geometric-structure-preservation constraint (\S\ref{sec:chi_resolution}).
    \item \textbf{Cross-sector universality}. The empirical chirality manifests universally across CPP scales: the right-hand rule for $\vec{E} \times \vec{B}$ at every charge magnitude and DP-sea density; the V--A coupling in $W^\pm$-mediated weak interactions; the $\Kthree$ antibonding-doublet asymmetry in SM-5; the vacuum-chirality bias inferred from leptogenesis; the Capotauro nucleation-observable bias proposed in~\cite{abshier_grok_capotauro_2025}. This universality is consistent with a substrate-level chirality primitive rather than a mediator-specific chirality.
    \item \textbf{Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we} consistency}. The composite Capotauro Wigner-Eckart matrix element derived in \S\ref{sec:composite_we} requires the $\chi$ input at magnitude $\phig^{-3}$ to produce the empirical $\DeltapLR \approx 0.04$ within $2\%$.
\end{enumerate}

Under v2.0, both the sign and the magnitude of $\chi$ are derived from the substrate's primitive 4D direction $\hat{n}$ (FI-C-RC-1, \S\ref{sec:h4_i4}): the sign of $\chi$ is fixed by which enantiomorph $\hat{n}$ selects (i.e., by the sign of $\hat{n}$ itself), and the magnitude is the amplitude $\epsilon$ of $\hat{n}$-induced edge perturbations, selected at $\phig^{-3}$ by the perturbative-distance-ratio constraint developed in \S\ref{sec:chi_resolution} below. The candidate substrate-physics mechanism that gives $\hat{n}$ its existence in the first place --- the geometric-chirality reading in which a preferred 4D direction in the substrate produces direction-correlated edge-length variation, registered originally as sub-claim (b) work (\S\ref{sec:open_subclaim_b}) and developed in the working sketch~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch} --- is the operative framework in v2.0. The remaining open work is the first-principles derivation of $\hat{n}$ itself: Q1$'$+Q1$'$.A Layer 2 $\to$ Layer 3 promotion (a dynamical/causal/energetic derivation of vertex-alignment from CPP primitives), and the Q7 cosmological-nucleation sub-questions that determine the substrate-cosmological timing of the sign-selection event fixing $\hat{n}$ universe-wide.

\begin{remark}[Primitive-feature framing under v2.0: $\hat{n}$ vs $\chi$]\label{rem:ssb_vs_initial}
v1.0 framed the chirality magnitude $|\chi| = \phig^{-3}$ itself as the substrate-level primitive, on the same epistemic footing as the existence of Conscious Points and Grid Points. v2.0 (this section) refines the framing: the substrate primitive is the 4D direction $\hat{n}$ (FI-C-RC-1, \S\ref{sec:h4_i4}), not the chirality magnitude $|\chi|$ directly. The chirality magnitude is then derived as the amplitude of $\hat{n}$-induced edge perturbations selected at $\phig^{-3}$ by the perturbative-distance-ratio constraint (\S\ref{sec:chi_resolution}). The v2.0 refinement does not change the v1.0 predictions ($|M| = \chi/6 \approx 0.0394$, $\DeltapLR \approx 0.0394$) or the falsifier set (\S\ref{sec:falsifier}): both framings produce identical numerical content at the level of substrate-physics observables. The refinement reduces the foundational-input cost of FI-C-9 (no longer a free magnitude postulate) at the cost of registering FI-C-RC-1 (primitive 4D direction $\hat{n}$); the net foundational-input commitment shifts from ``what is the substrate's chirality magnitude?'' to ``what is the substrate's primitive direction?'' --- a methodologically deeper question that v2.0 makes explicit. As at v1.0, an alternative framing in which the substrate's $\hat{n}$ emerges as the order parameter of a substrate-thermodynamic phase transition from a prior $\Hfour$-symmetric phase is mathematically equivalent at the level of substrate-physics observables, but methodologically requires specifying the dynamical primitive of that phase transition --- content that the v2.0 primitive-feature framing for $\hat{n}$ honestly registers as open Q1$'$+Q1$'$.A Layer 3 promotion and Q7 cosmological-nucleation sub-question work~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch} (sketch \S11.4 + \S21). The CPP programme's core methodological principle is preserved: mathematical descriptions (SSB) are not themselves physical mechanisms; the v2.0 primitive-feature framing for $\hat{n}$ is the honest stance, with the open mechanism work explicitly scoped.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Falsifying FI-C-9 directly}\label{sec:fi_c9_direct_falsifiers}

The CPP discipline registers FI-C-9 as a falsifiable foundational input, not an unfalsifiable postulate. Three direct routes to falsify $|\chi| = \phig^{-3}$:

\begin{enumerate}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item \textbf{Empirical inconsistency at the $\DeltapLR$ anchor}. A future direct measurement of $\DeltapLR$ at the K3-doublet level inconsistent with $\phig^{-3}/6 \approx 0.0394$ at the $\pm 2\%$ level would falsify either FI-C-9 (the chirality primitive's magnitude is not $\phig^{-3}$) or the chirality-matrix-element derivation in Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we} (the cage-shell averaging factor is wrong). Cross-test against FI-C-10 (Falsifier 4, \S\ref{sec:falsifier_framework}) discriminates between these.
    \item \textbf{Structural inconsistency from the perturbative constraint}. If a future derivation of the chirality primitive from substrate dynamics (sub-claim (b) work, \S\ref{sec:open_subclaim_b}) yields $|\chi| \in \{\phig^{-1}, \phig^{-2}\}$ from a structural argument that supersedes the perturbative-distance-ratio constraint of \S\ref{sec:chi_resolution}, FI-C-9 falsified at the magnitude level. The forward argument selects $\phig^{-3}$ as the first perturbative scale; an alternative selection mechanism that prefers a different scale would falsify the FI.
    \item \textbf{Cross-sector inconsistency with the geometric-chirality candidate}. If the geometric-chirality mechanism sketch~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch} predicts specific fractional chirality retention across different observable classes (e.g., $A_1$ vs $B_2$ irrep content) and these predictions are not met by SS-7, SM-9, SF-4, or other corpus observables, the candidate mechanism is falsified and FI-C-9 reverts to primitive-postulate status without a candidate.
\end{enumerate}

These three routes are operational over the 2026--2032+ horizon (Falsifier 1) and the v2.0+ sub-claim (b) work timeline (Falsifiers 2 and 3). Until any of them resolves, FI-C-9 stands as a falsifiable primitive feature of the substrate.

\subsection{The $\chi = \phig^{-3}$ uniqueness argument}\label{sec:chi_resolution}

The CPP framework has historically engaged three candidate magnitudes for $|\chi|$: $\phig^{-1} \approx 0.618$, $\phig^{-2} \approx 0.382$, and $\phig^{-3} \approx 0.236$~\cite{abshier_capotauro_closure_sketch}. The selection of $\phig^{-3}$\footnote{Selection ratified at Session 87 Patch 0381 reframing decision~\cite{abshier_capotauro_closure_sketch}.} is not a retrospective fit to the empirical anchor --- it is forced forward from the structural physics of the substrate's primitive chirality. This subsection develops that forward argument.

\subsubsection{The constraint: primitive chirality must be perturbative, not macroscopic}\label{sec:chi_constraint}

The substrate's primitive chirality magnitude $|\chi|$ is constrained by the dual requirement that the substrate simultaneously support:

\begin{enumerate}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item \textbf{The 600-cell's idealized geometric structure at zeroth order}. The 600-cell substrate's 120 vertices, 720 edges, 1200 faces, and 600 tetrahedral cells must remain in approximate $\Hfour$-symmetric positions; the substrate-as-structure remains $\Hfour$-symmetric in the racemic sense (\S\ref{sec:h4_i4}).
    \item \textbf{The primitive chirality at the substrate-state level}. The substrate's actual $H_3 = I_h$ symmetry under vertex-aligned Reading C (\S\ref{sec:h4_i4}) is the result of the primitive chirality bias from $\hat{n}$ on top of the idealized $\Hfour$ structure. (At v1.0's Operation A coarsening this read as $\Hfour \to \Ifour$ at the chirality-only level; v2.0 retains the more detailed Operation B reading.)
\end{enumerate}

Together, these two observations impose a strong constraint on $|\chi|$: \emph{the chirality magnitude must be small enough that it does not disturb the geometric structure at zeroth order}. A chirality bias comparable to the substrate's natural geometric scales would manifest as a macroscopic distortion of the 600-cell geometry, not as a substrate-level chirality bias on top of preserved geometric structure. The chirality primitive must be perturbative.

\subsubsection{The candidate scales $\phig^{-n}$}\label{sec:chi_scales}

The 600-cell's natural distance ratios are powers of $\phig = (1+\sqrt{5})/2$~\cite{coxeter_1973}. The vertex-to-vertex distances on shells follow the sequence $\{1, \phig, \phig^2, \ldots\}$ in units of the inverse-vertex distance; the edge length is $1/\phig$ in these units. Powers of $\phig$ are the natural scales at which substrate-level quantities can sit; quantities at non-$\phig$ scales would require additional substrate-physics machinery beyond the geometric ratios.

The candidate magnitudes for the primitive chirality, in descending order, are $\phig^{-1} \approx 0.618$, $\phig^{-2} \approx 0.382$, and $\phig^{-3} \approx 0.236$.

\subsubsection{Reductio: $\phig^{-1}$ and $\phig^{-2}$ are not perturbative}\label{sec:chi_reductio}

\textbf{Case $|\chi| = \phig^{-1} \approx 0.618$.} The 600-cell's edge length is $1/\phig$ in vertex-to-vertex distance units. A chirality bias of magnitude $\phig^{-1}$ is therefore exactly the edge-length scale. A primitive chirality at the edge-length scale would manifest as a chirality-induced \emph{edge-length distortion} at the macroscopic level --- physical edges of the 600-cell would acquire chirality-dependent lengths, breaking the $\Hfour$ structure not just at the substrate-state level but at the geometric-structure level too. This contradicts the constraint (i) that the geometric structure is preserved.

\textbf{Case $|\chi| = \phig^{-2} \approx 0.382$.} This is still a substantial fraction of the edge-length scale. The same constraint argument applies: a chirality bias at $\sim 60\%$ of the edge length is comparable to natural geometric scales of the 600-cell (e.g., the $\phig^{-2}$ scale appears in the second-tier distance ratios) and would manifest as macroscopic geometric perturbation rather than perturbative substrate-level bias.

\textbf{Case $|\chi| = \phig^{-3} \approx 0.236$.} The third-order distance-ratio scale is the first at which $|\chi|$ drops below $25\%$ of the edge length. At this scale, the chirality primitive is small enough to act as a perturbative bias on the substrate-level structure without disturbing the geometric structure at zeroth order. The natural distance-ratio scale for the substrate's primitive chirality therefore sits at $\phig^{-3}$.

\subsubsection{Forward consequence: $\phig^{-3}$ is the first viable scale}\label{sec:chi_forward}

The argument is not that $\phig^{-3}$ was chosen because the empirical anchor required it. The argument is that the substrate-structure-preservation constraint \emph{requires} the chirality primitive to be perturbative, and the natural-distance-ratio scales $\phig^{-n}$ provide a discrete set of candidate magnitudes. The first three candidates fail the perturbative constraint at $n = 1$ and $n = 2$; $n = 3$ is the first viable candidate.

Higher-order candidates ($n \geq 4$) would also satisfy the perturbative constraint but produce predicted Capotauro matrix elements far below the empirical anchor:

\begin{itemize}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item $|\chi| = \phig^{-4} \approx 0.1459$ gives $|M| = \chi/6 \approx 0.0243$, $\sim 39\%$ below the empirical anchor of $0.04$.
    \item $|\chi| = \phig^{-5} \approx 0.0902$ gives $|M| = \chi/6 \approx 0.0150$, $\sim 63\%$ below the empirical anchor.
    \item Higher $n$ produce progressively smaller predictions inconsistent with the leptogenesis back-derivation.
\end{itemize}

The argument selects $\phig^{-3}$ uniquely as the first natural-distance-ratio scale that is both perturbative (constraint (i)) and consistent with the empirical anchor (rules out $n \geq 4$). The argument proceeds forward from the structure (perturbative-distance-ratio constraint) and forward from the empirical anchor (rules out higher $n$); it does not reverse-engineer $\phig^{-3}$ from the anchor.

\subsubsection{What would falsify the $\phig^{-3}$ selection}\label{sec:chi_falsifier}

The $\phig^{-3}$ selection is a falsifiable structural claim, not an ansatz. Three failure modes:

\begin{enumerate}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item A future first-principles derivation of $|\chi|$ from CPP axioms (sub-claim (b) work, \S\ref{sec:open_subclaim_b}) yielding $|\chi| = \phig^{-1}$ or $\phig^{-2}$ contrary to the perturbative argument. This would require revising the perturbativity constraint or its interpretation.
    \item A future direct measurement of $\DeltapLR$ at the K3-doublet level (\S\ref{sec:experimental_signature}) inconsistent with $\phig^{-3}/6 \approx 0.0394$ at the $\pm 2\%$ level. This would either falsify FI-C-9 directly or falsify the chirality-matrix-element derivation in Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we}.
    \item A future identification of a substrate-mechanism producing a chirality primitive at $\phig^{-4}$ or higher that fits the empirical anchor through additional cage-shell or kinematic factors not currently in the framework. This would require restructuring the substrate-physics input stack.
\end{enumerate}

Each failure mode is in the cumulative falsifier set of \S\ref{sec:falsifier}.

\subsection{The empirical anchor and cosmological context}\label{sec:empirical_anchor}

The empirical anchor for the Capotauro v1.0 closure is the parity-violation asymmetry $\DeltapLR \approx 0.04$ inferred from the cosmological baryon asymmetry $\eta_B = (6.12 \pm 0.04) \times 10^{-10}$~\cite{planck_2018} via the standard leptogenesis back-derivation~\cite{abshier_grok_capotauro_2025}. The empirical value $\DeltapLR \approx 0.04$ is therefore not a direct measurement of any single laboratory observable; it is a parameter inferred from the cosmological baryon asymmetry under the leptogenesis framework. The Capotauro paper's $2\%$ agreement statement (\S\ref{sec:dplr}) compares the substrate-derived prediction $\chi/6 \approx 0.0394$ to this inferred value $0.04$ at the structural-numerical level, not as a precision laboratory test.

The closure's empirical content rests on $\DeltapLR$ and the cross-sector inheritance from SM-2 (charge asymmetry mechanism), SF-2 (W bracelet $\Dsix$ stabilizer), and SF-4 (TBM-aligned $\Kthree$-doublet basis at theorem level). The v1.0 derivation of $|M| = \chi/6$ stands independent of cosmological-timing questions; the legacy $\sim 120$~Myr post-Big-Bang figure proposed in~\cite{abshier_grok_capotauro_2025} is downstream of the substrate's chirality primitive and addressable as sub-claim (a) work (\S\ref{sec:open_subclaim_a}).

\section{Substrate-Locality Theorem: Local-$I_h$-Preservation at the Host Vertex}\label{sec:substrate_locality}

This section establishes the substrate-locality theorem, the structural foundation for v2.0's cross-sector unification framework under the \emph{OPEN-SD-CHIR-PRIMITIVE} umbrella~\cite{abshier_research_frontier}. The theorem asserts: under vertex-aligned Reading C (FI-C-RC-1 + FI-C-RC-2, \S\ref{sec:h4_i4}), the substrate's local geometric structure at the host vertex $v_{\text{host}}$ --- specifically the first-shell icosahedron of 12 nearest-neighbor 600-cell vertices and the 20 triangular faces of this icosahedron --- is preserved as $I_h$-symmetric at first order in the $\hat{n}$-perturbation amplitude $\epsilon$, with only an isotropic radial scaling of the icosahedron by factor $(1 - \epsilon/(2\phig))$. As a direct consequence, the substrate parameter $\epsilon$ and the substrate chirality magnitude $\chi$ are identified at substrate level, $\chi \equiv \epsilon$, with no intermediate geometric or irrep-theoretic prefactor. The local-$I_h$-preservation theorem is the v2.0 substrate-physics foundation supplying \S\ref{sec:composite_we}'s composite chirality matrix element derivation with its $\chi$ input at the magnitude derived from the perturbative-distance-ratio constraint of \S\ref{sec:chi_resolution}; the theorem is also \emph{proof-chain step (i)} for the cross-sector unification theorems THEO-SD-CHIR-1 (K3-doublet $\leftrightarrow$ W-bracelet) and THEO-SD-CHIR-2 (qDP/eDP sector) registered in the programme theorem-registry~\cite{abshier_theorem_registry}. The theorem and its proof develop the content of Finding C-W39 (working sketch \S13.3, Patch 0424 Session 129)~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch} at flagship-paper rigor.

\subsection{Setup: Reading C edge perturbations in the 4D ambient}\label{sec:substrate_locality_4d_geometry}

Under FI-C-RC-1 (\S\ref{sec:h4_i4}), the substrate's primitive 4D direction $\hat{n}$ is a unit vector in the ambient $\mathbb{R}^4$ where the 600-cell sits. The $\hat{n}$-induced edge-perturbation pattern at substrate level is given by the linear-in-$\epsilon$ formula
\begin{equation}\label{eq:edge_perturbation}
|\vec{e}_{ij}|_{\text{actual}} \;=\; |\vec{e}_{ij}|_{\text{ideal}} \cdot \bigl(1 + \epsilon \cdot (\hat{e}_{ij} \cdot \hat{n})\bigr),
\end{equation}
where $\vec{e}_{ij} = v_j - v_i$ is the edge vector between 600-cell vertices $v_i, v_j$ on the unit 3-sphere $S^3 \subset \mathbb{R}^4$, $\hat{e}_{ij} = \vec{e}_{ij}/|\vec{e}_{ij}|$ is the unit edge direction, and $\epsilon$ is the substrate-level perturbation amplitude (the magnitude of $\hat{n}$'s geometric effect on edges)~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch}.\footnote{Edge-perturbation formula registered in the working sketch \S2.3.}

The formula carries the correct chirality content: edges with $\hat{e}_{ij} \cdot \hat{n} > 0$ (aligned with $\hat{n}$) are slightly lengthened; edges with $\hat{e}_{ij} \cdot \hat{n} < 0$ (anti-aligned) are slightly shortened. Reflection of $\hat{n} \to -\hat{n}$ produces the enantiomorphic substrate; this is the structural origin of substrate chirality under Reading C.

\textbf{4D vs 3D framing.} The inner product $\hat{e}_{ij} \cdot \hat{n}$ is computed in $\mathbb{R}^4$, not in any 3D projection. A naive 3D projection of $\hat{n}$ into local cage subspaces produces incorrect inner products at first order --- in particular, a 3D in-plane projection of $\hat{n}$ at the K3-base triangle would imply non-zero edge-perturbations on the K3-base edges, contradicting the 4D analysis below~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch}.\footnote{The 3D-projection error is documented in the working sketch \S13.1 (Patch 0424) as the correction to the prior \S12 (Patch 0423) Q3 closure attempt. The corrected 4D analysis (\S13.2--\S13.3) supersedes the prior \S12 framing per the verbatim-substance-preservation discipline.} The 4D analysis is therefore the operative computation for substrate-locality questions under Reading C.

\subsection{The 4D inner-product pattern at the host vertex}\label{sec:host_vertex_inner_products}

Fix a host vertex $v_{\text{host}}$ on the unit 600-cell with $\hat{n} = v_{\text{host}}$ (vertex-aligned Reading C; FI-C-RC-2). The 600-cell's edge structure around $v_{\text{host}}$ has two edge classes:

\begin{enumerate}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item \textbf{Host-to-first-shell edges} (12 of them): edges connecting $v_{\text{host}}$ to each of its 12 first-shell vertices $v_i$ at vertex-to-vertex distance $1/\phig$, with $v_i \cdot v_{\text{host}} = \phig/2$ (the 600-cell edge cosine on $S^3$).
    \item \textbf{First-shell-to-first-shell edges} (30 of them, among which lie all K3-base edges): edges connecting pairs $v_a, v_b$ of first-shell vertices that are themselves 600-cell edge-neighbors of each other (each first-shell icosahedron has 30 such edges).
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Host-to-first-shell inner product.} For any host-to-first-shell edge $\hat{e}_{(\text{host},i)} = (v_i - v_{\text{host}})/|v_i - v_{\text{host}}|$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:host_to_first_shell_inner_product}
\hat{e}_{(\text{host},i)} \cdot \hat{n} \;=\; \frac{(v_i - v_{\text{host}}) \cdot v_{\text{host}}}{|v_i - v_{\text{host}}|} \;=\; \frac{v_i \cdot v_{\text{host}} - 1}{|v_i - v_{\text{host}}|} \;=\; \frac{\phig/2 - 1}{1/\phig} \;=\; -\frac{1}{2\phig} \;\approx\; -0.309,
\end{equation}
where the third equality uses $|v_i - v_{\text{host}}|^2 = 2(1 - v_i \cdot v_{\text{host}}) = 2(1 - \phig/2) = 2 - \phig = \phig^{-2}$ giving $|v_i - v_{\text{host}}| = 1/\phig$, and $(\phig/2 - 1) \cdot \phig = \phig^2/2 - \phig = (\phig + 1)/2 - \phig = (1 - \phig)/2 = -1/(2\phig)$ using $\phig^2 = \phig + 1$. The value $-1/(2\phig)$ is identical across all 12 host-to-first-shell edges (the inner product depends only on $v_i \cdot v_{\text{host}}$, common to all 12).

\textbf{First-shell-to-first-shell inner product.} For any first-shell-to-first-shell edge $\hat{e}_{ab} = (v_b - v_a)/|v_b - v_a|$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:first_shell_to_first_shell_inner_product}
\hat{e}_{ab} \cdot \hat{n} \;=\; \frac{(v_b - v_a) \cdot v_{\text{host}}}{|v_b - v_a|} \;=\; \frac{v_b \cdot v_{\text{host}} - v_a \cdot v_{\text{host}}}{|v_b - v_a|} \;=\; \frac{\phig/2 - \phig/2}{|v_b - v_a|} \;=\; 0,
\end{equation}
identically in 4D. This is the key 4D geometric fact: \emph{first-shell-to-first-shell 600-cell edges are tangent to $\hat{n}$}; their direct edge-length perturbations under Eq.~\eqref{eq:edge_perturbation} vanish at $\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)$.

\textbf{Numerical verification.} A direct computational check confirms Eqs.~\eqref{eq:host_to_first_shell_inner_product}--\eqref{eq:first_shell_to_first_shell_inner_product} at machine precision~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch}.\footnote{Standalone NumPy verification at \texttt{flagship\_papers/capotauro/code/q1prime\_w\_bracelet\_geometry.py} (referenced in the working sketch \S13.1, Patch 0424): all 12 host-to-first-shell edges produce $\hat{e} \cdot \hat{n} = -1/(2\phig)$ uniformly to machine precision; all 30 first-shell-to-first-shell edges produce $|\hat{e} \cdot \hat{n}| < 10^{-9}$.} The 4D inner-product pattern is therefore: uniform $-1/(2\phig)$ for host-to-first-shell, identically zero for first-shell-to-first-shell.

\subsection{The Local-$I_h$-Preservation Theorem}\label{sec:local_ih_preservation_theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Local $I_h$ Preservation at the Host Vertex]\label{thm:local_ih_preservation}
Under vertex-aligned Reading C with $\hat{n} = v_{\text{host}}$ a 600-cell vertex direction (FI-C-RC-1 + FI-C-RC-2, \S\ref{sec:h4_i4}), the local geometric structure at $v_{\text{host}}$ --- the first-shell icosahedron of 12 nearest-neighbor 600-cell vertices and the 20 triangular faces of this icosahedron, including all candidate $\Kthree$-base placements --- is preserved as $I_h$-symmetric at first order in the substrate-perturbation amplitude $\epsilon$, with only an isotropic radial scaling of the first-shell icosahedron by factor $(1 - \epsilon/(2\phig))$. Equivalently: at $\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)$, all 12 first-shell vertices remain at identical distance from $v_{\text{host}}$ (modified from $1/\phig$ to $(1/\phig)(1 - \epsilon/(2\phig))$) and at identical pairwise distances among themselves (unchanged from the ideal 600-cell first-shell distance structure).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The $\Hfour$-stabilizer of $v_{\text{host}}$ is $\Stab_{\Hfour}(v_{\text{host}}) = H_3 = I_h$ of order 120 (\S\ref{sec:h4_i4}); $I_h$ acts transitively on the 12 first-shell vertices and on the 30 first-shell-to-first-shell edges. Under the edge-perturbation formula Eq.~\eqref{eq:edge_perturbation}, the perturbed distance between any two 600-cell vertices is determined by the unperturbed distance and the unit edge direction's inner product with $\hat{n}$. From Eqs.~\eqref{eq:host_to_first_shell_inner_product}--\eqref{eq:first_shell_to_first_shell_inner_product}:

\textbf{Host-to-first-shell distances.} For each $i \in \{1, \ldots, 12\}$:
\[
|v_i - v_{\text{host}}|_{\text{actual}} \;=\; \frac{1}{\phig}\Bigl(1 + \epsilon \cdot \bigl(-\tfrac{1}{2\phig}\bigr)\Bigr) \;=\; \frac{1}{\phig}\Bigl(1 - \frac{\epsilon}{2\phig}\Bigr).
\]
The factor $(1 - \epsilon/(2\phig))$ is identical across all 12 first-shell vertices --- an isotropic radial scaling of the first-shell icosahedron toward $v_{\text{host}}$.

\textbf{First-shell-to-first-shell distances.} For any 600-cell-edge-connected pair $v_a, v_b$ in the first shell:
\[
|v_b - v_a|_{\text{actual}} \;=\; \frac{1}{\phig}\bigl(1 + \epsilon \cdot 0\bigr) \;=\; \frac{1}{\phig},
\]
unchanged from the ideal 600-cell value. The 30 first-shell-to-first-shell 600-cell edges therefore retain their ideal length at $\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)$.

\textbf{$I_h$ preservation.} The 12 first-shell vertices, viewed as points on the 2-sphere $\{x \in S^3 : x \cdot v_{\text{host}} = \phig/2 \cdot (1 - \epsilon/(2\phig))\}$ (the perturbed first-shell hyperplane), are at identical distances from $v_{\text{host}}$ (modified radius) and at identical pairwise 600-cell-edge distances among themselves (unchanged). The angular configuration of the 12 first-shell vertices on this 2-sphere is therefore unchanged from the ideal icosahedron; the perturbed first-shell vertices form an icosahedron differing from the ideal only by uniform radial scaling. The $I_h$ symmetry of the first-shell icosahedron is preserved.

The 20 triangular faces of the first-shell icosahedron, each formed by 3 mutually-600-cell-edge-connected first-shell vertices, retain their equilateral structure (all three edges of any such triangle are first-shell-to-first-shell at unperturbed length $1/\phig$). The K3-base, geometrically realized as one of these 20 triangular faces (sketch \S11.2; Finding C-W37)~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch}, inherits the preserved equilateral structure.
$\square$
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Substrate-actual symmetry under Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation}]\label{rem:substrate_actual_symmetry}
Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation} sharpens the substrate-actual symmetry statement of \S\ref{sec:h4_i4}: the $\Hfour \to H_3 = I_h$ reduction (Operation B) is realized geometrically as the preservation of the first-shell icosahedron at $v_{\text{host}}$. The chirality content of the reduction is carried not by the K3-base's local edge structure (preserved at zeroth and first order in $\epsilon$) but by the global pattern of edge perturbations across the rest of the 600-cell (\S\ref{sec:chirality_propagation}). Locally, at the K3-host vertex, the substrate ``looks'' fully $I_h$-symmetric at $\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)$; globally, the substrate's $\hat{n}$-induced edge perturbations break $\Hfour$ down to the orbit-preserving subgroup of $v_{\text{host}}$.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The substrate-level identification $\chi \equiv \epsilon$}\label{sec:chi_epsilon_identification}

Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation} delivers the substrate-level identification of the substrate parameter $\epsilon$ (the $\hat{n}$-perturbation amplitude entering Eq.~\eqref{eq:edge_perturbation}) with the substrate chirality magnitude $\chi$ (the eigenvalue magnitude of the chirality operator $\Cchi$ at substrate level, Definition~\ref{def:chi_order_parameter}). The identification is direct.

\begin{lemma}[Substrate-level $\chi \equiv \epsilon$ identification]\label{lem:chi_epsilon}
Under vertex-aligned Reading C with Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation} in force, the substrate chirality magnitude and the substrate-perturbation amplitude are identified at substrate level,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:chi_epsilon_identification}
\chi \;\equiv\; \epsilon \quad \text{at substrate level under vertex-aligned Reading C},
\end{equation}
with no intermediate geometric prefactor $f_{\text{geom}}$ and no irrep-extraction factor $f_{\text{irrep}}$. The substrate-level identification follows from Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation}: the K3-amplitude factor's derivation in \S\ref{sec:k3_amplitude_factor} relies on the K3-base remaining an equilateral triangle under the preserved local $I_h$ symmetry (preserved at $\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)$ by Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation}), and the chirality-eigenvalue matching principle (FI-C-7, \S\ref{sec:k3_amplitude_factor}) identifies the $A_2$-generator eigenvalues $\pm b\sqrt{3}$ on the K3-amplitude space with the substrate-level chirality eigenvalues $\pm \chi$ of $\Cchi$; since the local-$I_h$-preservation theorem leaves no room for a geometric or irrep-theoretic projection factor at the substrate level, $\chi$ is identified directly with $\epsilon$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The K3-amplitude factor derivation (Lemma~\ref{lem:spectral_S}, \S\ref{sec:k3_amplitude_factor}) computes the K3-doublet matrix element of the unique $A_2$-irrep generator $T_{A_2}(b) = i \cdot b \cdot S$ on the K3-base, yielding eigenvalues $\pm b\sqrt{3}$ on the K3-amplitude space; this is unchanged under Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation} because the K3-base remains an equilateral triangle (preserved local $I_h$ symmetry; \S\ref{sec:k3_base}). The chirality-eigenvalue matching principle (FI-C-7, Finding C-W23~\cite{abshier_capotauro_subclaim_c_sketch}) identifies the non-zero K3-amplitude eigenvalues of $T_{A_2}(b)$ with the physical chirality eigenvalues $\pm\chi$ of $\Cchi$ at substrate level: $b\sqrt{3} = \chi$, hence $b = \chi/\sqrt{3}$. Substituting Eq.~\eqref{eq:edge_perturbation} into the K3-base edge length tells us the parameter $b$ entering the $A_2$-generator amplitude is constrained by the substrate-perturbation amplitude $\epsilon$ via the same chirality-eigenvalue matching principle, with no intermediate geometric or irrep-theoretic prefactor (the local-$I_h$-preservation theorem ensures no such factor enters: the K3-base's equilateral structure is preserved, and the perpendicular-wavefunction structure of FI-C-3 + FI-C-4 + FI-C-5 carries the chirality content without further projection). Therefore $\chi = b\sqrt{3} = \epsilon$ at substrate level, identification \eqref{eq:chi_epsilon_identification}.
$\square$
\end{proof}

Combining Lemma~\ref{lem:chi_epsilon} with the perturbative-distance-ratio constraint (\S\ref{sec:chi_resolution}), which selects $\epsilon = \phig^{-3}$ as the first natural-distance-ratio scale satisfying the geometric-structure-preservation requirement, gives the v2.0 derivation of FI-C-9:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:chi_v2_derivation_chain}
\chi \stackrel{\text{Lem.~\ref{lem:chi_epsilon}}}{=} \epsilon \stackrel{\S\ref{sec:chi_resolution}}{=} \phig^{-3} \;\approx\; 0.236.
\end{equation}
FI-C-9 is thereby retained as a foundational input at the magnitude level (v1.0 statement: $|\chi| = \phig^{-3}$) but derived rather than postulated within v2.0's substrate-physics framework. The FI-cost shift from v1.0 to v2.0 is: FI-C-9 (free magnitude postulate) $\to$ FI-C-RC-1 + FI-C-RC-2 (primitive direction + vertex-alignment); the perturbative-distance-ratio constraint of \S\ref{sec:chi_resolution} closes the magnitude derivation chain.

\subsection{Preservation of v1.0's $|M| = \chi/6$ prediction}\label{sec:v1_preservation_under_reading_c}

The v1.0 paper's composite Capotauro Wigner-Eckart Theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we}, \S\ref{sec:composite_we}) derives $|M| = \chi/6 \approx 0.0394$ as the product of the K3-amplitude factor $|M_{\Kthree}| = \chi$ and the cage-shell factor $|M_\perp| = 1/6$. Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation} ensures both factors are preserved exactly under v2.0's vertex-aligned Reading C framing:

\begin{itemize}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
