    \item \textbf{K3-amplitude factor preserved.} The K3-amplitude factor $|M_{\Kthree}| = \chi$ derivation (\S\ref{sec:k3_amplitude_factor}, Lemma~\ref{lem:k3_factor}) relies on the K3-doublet states being in the $E$-irrep of the $\Sthree$ permutation symmetry on the K3-base. The $E$-irrep structure requires the K3-base to remain an equilateral triangle, which holds under Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation} (\S\ref{sec:local_ih_preservation_theorem}: the 20 first-shell triangles preserve equilateral structure at $\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)$).
    \item \textbf{Cage-shell factor preserved.} The cage-shell factor $|M_\perp| = 1/6 = d_E / V_{\text{cage}}$ derivation (\S\ref{sec:cage_shell_factor}, Lemma~\ref{lem:cage_shell}) relies on the icosahedral cage's $V_{\text{cage}} = 12$ first-shell vertices being symmetry-equivalent under the cage's stabilizer. This holds under Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation} (the 12 first-shell vertices are at identical distance from $v_{\text{host}}$ at $\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)$, with full local $I_h$ symmetry intact).
\end{itemize}

The composite prediction $|M| = \chi/6$ is therefore preserved \emph{exactly} under v2.0's vertex-aligned Reading C framing. The v1.0 numerical content (Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we}, $|M| = \chi/6 \approx 0.0394$; primary empirical prediction $\DeltapLR = \chi/6$ matching observed $\sim 0.04$ within 2\%) carries over to v2.0 unchanged. The v2.0 refinement does not alter the v1.0 prediction; it provides the substrate-physics derivation route for FI-C-9's magnitude (Eq.~\eqref{eq:chi_v2_derivation_chain}) that v1.0 left as foundational input.

\subsection{Where the chirality bias propagates globally}\label{sec:chirality_propagation}

Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation} establishes that the chirality bias is \emph{invisible} to the K3-base's local edge structure at first order in $\epsilon$: K3-base edges have zero direct edge-length perturbation, and the host vertex's first-shell icosahedron is preserved up to uniform radial scaling. The bias must therefore propagate into the substrate's global edge-length pattern through other channels~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch}:\footnote{Substantive content from the working sketch \S13.7 (Patch 0424).}

\begin{enumerate}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item \textbf{Second-shell and beyond connections.} Edges from first-shell to second-shell 600-cell vertices have $\hat{e} \cdot \hat{n}$ varying across positions (second-shell vertices sit at angular distance from $v_{\text{host}}$ different from the first shell, with non-uniform projections on $\hat{n}$). These edges carry the $\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)$ chirality signature globally.
    \item \textbf{Cage-to-cage couplings.} The substrate chirality magnitude $\chi$ extracted by the K3-doublet matrix element is set globally, encoding how $\hat{n}$ orchestrates the 600-cell's entire edge-length pattern. The K3-doublet's local matrix element extracts $\chi$ via the chirality-eigenvalue matching principle (\S\ref{sec:k3_amplitude_factor}), which is a global substrate-level identification.
    \item \textbf{Forward to non-host vertices.} For any 600-cell vertex $v' \neq v_{\text{host}}$, the chain of edges connecting $v_{\text{host}}$ to $v'$ carries direction-correlated length perturbations. The substrate's global chirality structure is the integrated effect of this $\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)$ pattern across all 720 edges.
\end{enumerate}

A first-principles derivation of $\chi$ as the eigenvalue of $\Cchi$ from the global substrate structure under Reading C --- closing the substrate-level chirality content from the global pattern rather than via Lemma~\ref{lem:chi_epsilon}'s direct identification --- remains open work, registered as part of Q5 cross-sector consistency and the eventual primitive-cause derivation under the \emph{OPEN-SD-CHIR-PRIMITIVE} umbrella~\cite{abshier_research_frontier}.\footnote{Working sketch \S13.7 closing paragraph: ``A first-principles derivation of $\chi$ as the eigenvalue of $\Cchi$ from the global substrate structure under Reading C would close Reading C at theorem level. The current understanding identifies $\chi \equiv \epsilon$ at the substrate-level definition, with the perturbative-distance-ratio constraint fixing $\epsilon = \phig^{-3}$.''} The v2.0 paper closes the magnitude derivation chain at Eq.~\eqref{eq:chi_v2_derivation_chain}'s level (substrate-level identification + perturbative-distance-ratio constraint); the global-eigenvalue derivation is the deeper open question.

\subsection{Substrate-locality as proof-chain step (i) for cross-sector unification}\label{sec:locality_as_unification_step}

Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation} is not specific to the K3-doublet sector --- it operates on any substrate object hosted at the host vertex's first-shell icosahedron. The W-bracelet (SF-2 v1.0~\cite{abshier_sf2}) sits on-axis with $\hat{n}$ at radius $\phig/2$ from $v_{\text{host}}$ (FI-C-RC-2 argument 2, \S\ref{sec:h4_i4}), with its 6 vertices realized as a Petrie hexagon on the first-shell icosahedron; the qDP/eDP substrate objects (SM-2 per the Linear-vs-Orbital ZBW characterization at SM-2 v1.0 \S5+\S6+Glossary; full SM-2 citation introduced at v2.0 \S6 qDP/eDP sector drafting) sit at specific positions in the first-shell cage as well. All three substrate objects --- K3-doublet (3-vertex triangle on a 600-cell face within the first-shell icosahedron), W-bracelet (6-vertex Petrie hexagon on the first-shell icosahedron), and qDP/eDP (Linear ZBW $d=1$ at a single first-shell vertex; Orbital ZBW $d=0$ at the host CP) --- inherit substrate chirality from the same identification $\chi \equiv \epsilon = \phig^{-3}$ via Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation}'s local-$I_h$-preservation guarantee.

This is the structural foundation for v2.0's cross-sector unification claim under the OPEN-SD-CHIR-PRIMITIVE umbrella: the substrate-physics handle $\chi/6$ feeding the chirality matrix element across all three sectors at substrate level (manifestation (i) mass-mixing on K3-doublet, manifestation (ii) electroweak V-A on W-bracelet, manifestation (iii) electromagnetic handedness on qDP/eDP polarization patterns). Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation} is registered as \emph{proof-chain step (i)} of THEO-SD-CHIR-1 (Cross-Sector Substrate Chirality Unification Theorem; K3-doublet $\leftrightarrow$ W-bracelet pair; registered Patch 0434 Session 132) and THEO-SD-CHIR-2 (qDP/eDP Sector Substrate Chirality Closure Theorem; three-way unification with K3-doublet and W-bracelet; registered Patch 0440 Session 133) in the programme theorem-registry~\cite{abshier_theorem_registry}.\footnote{Detailed inheritance and proof-chain structure for THEO-SD-CHIR-1 and THEO-SD-CHIR-2 are developed in the v2.0 cross-sector sections (forthcoming): W-bracelet sector at \S\ref{sec:cross_sector_inheritance} extended with the four-step proof chain (substrate-locality + Substrate-Locality Unification + cage-shell factor identity + pairing-convention identification) per working sketch \S14--\S17 (THEO-SD-CHIR-1) and \S18--\S20 (THEO-SD-CHIR-2)~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch}. The v0.2 drafting target completes these cross-sector sections.} The substrate-locality theorem is the substrate-physics common ground from which all three sectors' chirality content is sourced.

\section{The K3-Doublet and the Chirality Observable}\label{sec:k3_doublet}

This section establishes the $\Kthree$-doublet basis structure $\{\PhiminusOne, \PhiminusTwo\}$ and the substrate-level chirality observable $\Cchi$ in irreducible representation $B_2$ of the $\Kthree$ stabilizer $\Dsix = \Sthree \times \mathbb{Z}_2$. These are the operator and the states on which the Wigner-Eckart matrix element of \S\ref{sec:composite_we} is computed. The section assembles foundational inputs FI-C-2 (\S\ref{sec:k3_base}), FI-C-3 extended (\S\ref{sec:k3_tbm_basis} and \S\ref{sec:perpendicular_wavefunction}), and the $\zeta$-ODD assignment of $\Cchi$ (\S\ref{sec:chirality_observable}) into the substrate-physics object on which Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we} operates.

\subsection{The $\Kthree$ base structure and four-cage taxonomy (FI-C-2)}\label{sec:k3_base}

The $\Kthree$ triangle is the equilateral 3-vertex graph $\Kthree = \{V_1, V_2, V_3\}$ with three colour-vertices and exact $C_3$ rotational symmetry. In CPP, $\Kthree$ realizes the K3 base of the charge-asymmetry framework (SM-1)~\cite{abshier_sm1}: each vertex $V_k$ carries a distinct colour assignment (red / green / blue or up / charm / top, depending on sector), and the three vertices are related by the cyclic $C_3$ symmetry of the triangle.

The four-cage taxonomy from SM-1 assigns cage vertex counts $V \in \{4, 12, 20, 30\}$ to the four sub-particle types: tetrahedral $V=4$, icosahedral $V=12$, dodecahedral $V=20$, icosidodecahedral $V=30$. The cage taxonomy is foundational input FI-C-2 to the Capotauro closure --- the $V_\text{cage} = 12$ icosahedral first shell of the 600-cell, in particular, is the cage on which the cage-shell averaging factor $|M_\perp| = 1/6$ is computed in \S\ref{sec:composite_we}.

\subsection{The $\Kthree$-doublet TBM-aligned basis (FI-C-3)}\label{sec:k3_tbm_basis}

The K3 ZBW Hamiltonian on the $\Kthree$ base has spectrum $\lambda_+ = +2$ (bonding, once-degenerate) and $\lambda_- = -1$ (antibonding, doubly degenerate) by the K3 Spectral Theorem (THEO-SM-3)~\cite{abshier_sm3}. The antibonding doublet's two-dimensional space carries the relevant substrate structure for charged-lepton flavour identification.

The TBM-aligned basis of the antibonding doublet is
\[
\phiminusOne = \frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}\,(2, -1, -1), \qquad
\phiminusTwo = \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\,(0, -1, 1),
\]
selected at theorem level by the standard $\Sthree \to S_2$ representation-theory branching rule applied to the residual stabilizer of the charged-lepton $\Kthree$-vertex occupation~\cite{abshier_sf4}. Under the $\Sthree \to S_2$ branching, the $\mathbf{2}$-dimensional irreducible representation of $\Sthree$ on the antibonding doublet decomposes as $\mathbf{2}|_{S_2} = \mathbf{1}_+ \oplus \mathbf{1}_-$, uniquely (up to phase) selecting the TBM-aligned eigenstates. This is the Composite K3-Cage-Shell Coupling Theorem clause (ii) (THEO-SF-4-5) inherited cross-sector from SF-4 v4.0~\cite{abshier_sf4}.

The TBM-aligned basis is the substrate-level reading of the tribimaximal mixing matrix $U_\TBM$ derived in SM-5~\cite{abshier_sm5}. The cross-sector inheritance from SF-4 is critical: without the theorem-level closure of the antibonding-doublet basis at SF-4 v4.0 (the first cross-sector closure in CPP), the Capotauro mechanism would lack the rigorous basis on which to define the chirality matrix element $|M|$ at theorem level.

\subsection{The perpendicular wavefunction structure $\chiplus, \chiminus$}\label{sec:perpendicular_wavefunction}

A substantive extension to FI-C-3 emerged during the closure trajectory~\cite{abshier_capotauro_subclaim_c_sketch}.\footnote{Extension introduced at Session 91 Patch 0385.} The K3-amplitude part of the doublet $\{\phiminusOne, \phiminusTwo\}$ captures the colour-mode structure on the $\Kthree$ base, but the full substrate state at the K3 location also carries a perpendicular-direction wavefunction associated with the substrate orientation field at that location.

We denote the perpendicular-direction wavefunctions by $\chiplus$ and $\chiminus$. These are the $\zeta$-EVEN and $\zeta$-ODD components of the substrate orientation field at the $\Kthree$ location, where $\zeta$ is the $\mathbb{Z}_2$ generator of the $\Dsix = \Sthree \times \mathbb{Z}_2$ structure on the $\Kthree$-doublet space. The full $\Kthree$-doublet basis states are then
\[
\PhiminusOne = \phiminusOne \otimes \chiplus, \qquad
\PhiminusTwo = \phiminusTwo \otimes \chiminus,
\]
with the $\sigma_1\zeta$-EVEN pairing convention~\cite{abshier_capotauro_subclaim_c_sketch}.\footnote{Pairing convention identified at Session 91 Finding C-W12.} The pairing convention is required for the chirality matrix element $|M| = |\langle \Phi_-^{(1)} | \Cchi | \Phi_-^{(2)} \rangle|$ to be non-zero (Finding C-W11): the chirality observable $\Cchi$ is $\zeta$-ODD (\S\ref{sec:chirality_observable}), and a non-zero matrix element between $\Phi_-^{(1)}$ and $\Phi_-^{(2)}$ requires the perpendicular-wavefunction factors to be opposite-$\zeta$-parity.

The perpendicular wavefunction structure is full consistency with the SF-4 v4.0 Composite K3-Cage-Shell Coupling Theorem (Finding C-W13)~\cite{abshier_capotauro_subclaim_c_sketch}; no SF-4 revision is required for the Capotauro inheritance. The extension is internal to the Capotauro paper's FI-C-3 reading and is captured as part of the foundational input enumeration in \S\ref{sec:fi_summary}.

\subsection{The chirality observable $\Cchi$}\label{sec:chirality_observable}

\textbf{Physical meaning.} The chirality observable $\Cchi$ is the substrate-level operator whose expectation value measures the substrate's preference for one enantiomorph over the other at a specific $\Kthree$ location. Physically: $\Cchi$ is the signed difference between the substrate's coupling weights to left-handed and right-handed K3-doublet states under processes mediated by the doublet's primitive chirality. A racemic substrate ($|\chi| = 0$) would have $\langle \Cchi \rangle = 0$; the actual substrate has $\langle \Cchi \rangle \neq 0$ because its $\Ifour$ symmetry (not $\Hfour$) breaks the parity that would otherwise force the difference to vanish. The operator's matrix element on K3-doublet basis states is the substrate-level handle on parity-violating processes that descends, through SF-2's W bracelet $\Dsix$ stabilizer (FI-C-4) and the cage-shell averaging factor 1/6 (FI-C-10), to the observable parity-violation asymmetry $\DeltapLR$ (\S\ref{sec:dplr}).

\textbf{Representation-theoretic content.} The operator transforms under the $\Kthree$ stabilizer $\Dsix = \Sthree \times \mathbb{Z}_2$ as follows:

\begin{itemize}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item $\Sthree$-content: $\Cchi$ contains both $\mathbf{1}_-$ (sign representation, $\sigma_1$-ODD) and $\mathbf{2}$ ($E$-irrep) components in general; on the K3-amplitude subspace relevant to the matrix element, only the $\mathbf{1}_-$ component contributes (\S\ref{sec:d6_we}). The unique generator of the $A_2$-irrep ($\sigma_1$-ODD and $r$-invariant) component is $T_{A_2}(b) = i \cdot b \cdot S$, where $S$ is the real antisymmetric $3 \times 3$ matrix with cross-product-with-$(1,1,1)$ structure (Finding C-W21)~\cite{abshier_capotauro_subclaim_c_sketch}.
    \item $\mathbb{Z}_2$-content: $\Cchi$ is $\zeta$-ODD (Finding C-W11)~\cite{abshier_capotauro_subclaim_c_sketch}. The $\zeta$-ODD assignment is the substrate-physics reading of the chirality observable: the chirality bias flips sign under the $\mathbb{Z}_2$ generator $\zeta$ of the K3-doublet stabilizer.
\end{itemize}

The composite irreducible representation of $\Cchi$ within $\Dsix = \Sthree \times \mathbb{Z}_2$ on the K3-amplitude $\times$ perpendicular-wavefunction tensor structure is $B_2$ (the irrep that is $\sigma_1$-ODD and $\zeta$-ODD). The Wigner-Eckart matrix element machinery in \S\ref{sec:d6_we} operates on $B_2$ operators acting on the $E$-doublet of $\Kthree$-amplitude states.

\subsection{The full K3-doublet basis states for the matrix element}\label{sec:full_basis}

Assembling the K3-amplitude basis (\S\ref{sec:k3_tbm_basis}) and the perpendicular-wavefunction extension (\S\ref{sec:perpendicular_wavefunction}), the full $\Kthree$-doublet basis states on which the Wigner-Eckart matrix element of Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we} is computed are
\begin{equation}\label{eq:full_basis}
\PhiminusOne = \frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}\,(2, -1, -1) \otimes \chiplus, \qquad
\PhiminusTwo = \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\,(0, -1, 1) \otimes \chiminus.
\end{equation}
The matrix element to be computed in \S\ref{sec:composite_we} is then
\[
|M| = |\langle \Phi_-^{(1)} | \Cchi | \Phi_-^{(2)} \rangle|,
\]
where $\Cchi$ is the $B_2$-irrep operator described in \S\ref{sec:chirality_observable}. The Wigner-Eckart factorization separates this matrix element into K3-amplitude and cage-shell perpendicular-wavefunction factors (\S\ref{sec:composite_we}), each computable from the irreducible representation theory of $\Dsix$ together with FI-C-9 (substrate primitive chirality magnitude, \S\ref{sec:substrate_vacuum}) and FI-C-10 (cage-shell extension to chirality observables, \S\ref{sec:composite_we}).

\section{The $\Dsix$ Stabilizer and Wigner-Eckart Framework}\label{sec:d6_we}

This section establishes the group-theoretic machinery on which Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we} is built. The $\Kthree$ stabilizer is $\Dsix = \Sthree \times \mathbb{Z}_2$ (Findings C-W5, C-W6)~\cite{abshier_capotauro_subclaim_c_sketch}; the chirality observable $\Cchi$ transforms in irreducible representation $B_2$ (\S\ref{sec:chirality_observable}); the K3-doublet states $\{\PhiminusOne, \PhiminusTwo\}$ form an $E$-doublet of $\Dsix$ via the tensor product of K3-amplitude $E$-doublet of $\Sthree$ and perpendicular-wavefunction $\zeta$-EVEN/ODD doublet of $\mathbb{Z}_2$. The Wigner-Eckart theorem on $\Dsix$ factorizes the matrix element of $B_2$ operators on $E$-doublet states into K3-amplitude and perpendicular-wavefunction factors --- the structural decomposition that powers the eight-step proof in \S\ref{sec:composite_we}.

\subsection{The $\Kthree$ stabilizer $\Dsix = \Sthree \times \mathbb{Z}_2$}\label{sec:k3_stabilizer}

The $\Kthree$ triangle's automorphism group as a labelled graph is $\Sthree$ (the symmetric group on three elements, order 6), acting by permutations of the three vertices $\{V_1, V_2, V_3\}$. When the K3 base is embedded in the four-dimensional 600-cell substrate with the perpendicular-direction wavefunction structure of \S\ref{sec:perpendicular_wavefunction}, the K3 stabilizer extends to $\Dsix = \Sthree \times \mathbb{Z}_2$ of order 12: the $\Sthree$ factor acts on K3-amplitudes, and the $\mathbb{Z}_2$ factor acts on the perpendicular-direction $\zeta$-parity. The two factors commute: the K3-amplitude permutations and the perpendicular-direction $\zeta$-flip are structurally independent.

The Finding C-W5 ($\Dsix = \Sthree \times \mathbb{Z}_2$ structure) and Finding C-W6 (the perpendicular $\zeta$-parity component generates the $\mathbb{Z}_2$ factor) of the working sketch~\cite{abshier_capotauro_subclaim_c_sketch} together establish the stabilizer's product structure. The product structure is critical: it allows the matrix element to factorize into independent K3-amplitude and perpendicular-wavefunction contributions in the Wigner-Eckart machinery.

\subsection{The chirality-preserving subgroup $S_3'$ identification}\label{sec:s3_prime}

A substantive correction to the working-sketch §3.1 informal ``$\Dsix \to C_6$'' framing emerged during the closure trajectory~\cite{abshier_capotauro_subclaim_c_sketch}.\footnote{Correction surfaced at Session 89 Finding C-W7.} The chirality-preserving subgroup of $\Dsix$ is not $C_6$ (cyclic of order 6) but $S_3' \subset \Dsix$ (a specific copy of $\Sthree$ that combines K3-vertex permutations with the perpendicular $\zeta$-flip). The corrected identification reads:
\[
S_3' = \{ (\sigma, \zeta^{\text{sgn}(\sigma)}) : \sigma \in \Sthree \}
\]
where $\text{sgn}(\sigma) \in \{0, 1\}$ is the sign of the permutation. The subgroup combines $\Sthree$ permutations with an automatic $\zeta$-flip on odd permutations, preserving the chirality eigenvalue exactly.

The $S_3' \subset \Dsix$ identification is the chirality-preserving subgroup in the sense that the chirality observable $\Cchi$ is invariant under $S_3'$ action: $\sigma \cdot \Cchi \cdot \sigma^{-1} = \Cchi$ for all $\sigma \in S_3'$. The complement $\Dsix \setminus S_3'$ contains the chirality-violating elements that flip the sign of $\Cchi$.

\subsection{Irreducible representations of $\Dsix$}\label{sec:d6_irreps}

The irreducible representations of $\Dsix = \Sthree \times \mathbb{Z}_2$ are products of the irreducible representations of $\Sthree$ and $\mathbb{Z}_2$:

\begin{itemize}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item $\Sthree$ irreps: $A_1$ (trivial), $A_2$ (sign), $E$ (2-dimensional).
    \item $\mathbb{Z}_2$ irreps: trivial ($\zeta$-EVEN), sign ($\zeta$-ODD).
\end{itemize}

The product irreps of $\Dsix$ are then $A_1^{\pm}$, $A_2^{\pm}$, $E^{\pm}$, with the $\pm$ superscript denoting $\zeta$-parity. In conventional notation:
\[
\begin{array}{c|cc}
& \zeta\text{-EVEN} & \zeta\text{-ODD} \\
\hline
A_1 & A_1 & A_1' \\
A_2 & A_2 & B_2 \\
E & E_1 & E_2
\end{array}
\]
where the second column corresponds to the $\zeta$-ODD versions. The chirality observable $\Cchi$ transforms in the irrep $B_2 = A_2 \otimes (\zeta\text{-ODD})$: it is $\sigma_1$-ODD in K3-amplitudes (\S\ref{sec:chirality_observable}: the $A_2$ generator $T_{A_2}(b) = i \cdot b \cdot S$ is the unique $\Sthree$ $A_2$ representative) and $\zeta$-ODD in the perpendicular wavefunction (Finding C-W11)~\cite{abshier_capotauro_subclaim_c_sketch}.

The K3-doublet states transform in irrep $E_2 = E \otimes (\zeta\text{-ODD})$ as a tensor product of the $\Sthree$ $E$-doublet on K3-amplitudes and the $\zeta$-ODD perpendicular wavefunction (\S\ref{sec:perpendicular_wavefunction}, Eq.~\ref{eq:full_basis}). The matrix element $|M|$ in Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we} is therefore the K3-doublet $E_2$ expectation value of the $B_2$ operator $\Cchi$.

\subsection{Wigner-Eckart on $\Dsix$}\label{sec:wigner_eckart_d6}

The Wigner-Eckart theorem on $\Dsix$ states that the matrix element of an irrep-$\Gamma$ operator $\hat{O}^{\Gamma}$ between states transforming in irreps $\Gamma_1$ and $\Gamma_2$ factorizes as
\[
\langle \Gamma_2, m_2 | \hat{O}^{\Gamma} | \Gamma_1, m_1 \rangle = \langle \Gamma_2 || \hat{O}^{\Gamma} || \Gamma_1 \rangle \cdot C^{\Gamma_2, m_2}_{\Gamma, \Gamma_1; m_1}
\]
where $\langle \Gamma_2 || \hat{O}^{\Gamma} || \Gamma_1 \rangle$ is the reduced matrix element (irrep-dependent only) and $C^{\Gamma_2, m_2}_{\Gamma, \Gamma_1; m_1}$ is the Clebsch-Gordan coefficient. For the present matrix element, $\Gamma = B_2$, $\Gamma_1 = \Gamma_2 = E_2$, $m_1$ and $m_2$ index the K3-doublet states.

Because $\Dsix$ factorizes as $\Sthree \times \mathbb{Z}_2$, the Wigner-Eckart matrix element factorizes correspondingly into K3-amplitude and perpendicular-wavefunction factors:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:we_factorization}
\langle \Phi_-^{(i)} | \Cchi | \Phi_-^{(j)} \rangle = \underbrace{\langle \phi_-^{(i)} | T_{A_2}(b) | \phi_-^{(j)} \rangle}_{\text{K3-amplitude factor } M_{K_3}^{(ij)}} \cdot \underbrace{\langle \chi_i | T_\perp | \chi_j \rangle}_{\text{cage-shell factor } M_\perp^{(ij)}}.
\end{equation}
The K3-amplitude factor depends only on the $\Sthree$ irrep structure of the K3-doublet states and the $A_2$-irrep generator of the operator. The cage-shell factor depends on the perpendicular-direction $\mathbb{Z}_2$ structure and is computed via Schur orthogonality on the icosahedral cage hosting the charged-lepton substrate states (\S\ref{sec:composite_we}).

\subsection{The $\sigma_1$-ODD operator parameterization correction}\label{sec:sigma1_odd_correction}

A substantive correction to the working-sketch §13 framework emerged during the closure trajectory~\cite{abshier_capotauro_subclaim_c_sketch}.\footnote{Correction introduced at Session 95 Patch 0389.} The naive parameterization of $\sigma_1$-ODD operators on the K3-amplitude space uses two parameters $(a, b)$ giving $M_{K_3} = (b - 2a)/\sqrt{3}$. However, this parameterization captures the general $\sigma_1$-ODD operator subspace, which includes \emph{both} the $A_2$-irrep and the $E$-irrep components of $\Sthree$. For $\Cchi$ to be in $B_2$ of $\Dsix$ specifically, the K3-amplitude part must be in $A_2$ of $\Sthree$ (the unique irrep that is $\sigma_1$-ODD \emph{and} $r$-invariant, where $r$ is the cyclic K3-vertex rotation generator).

The unique $A_2$-irrep generator on the $\Sthree$ K3-amplitude space is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:t_a2_generator}
T_{A_2}(b) = i \cdot b \cdot S, \qquad S = \begin{pmatrix} 0 & 1 & -1 \\ -1 & 0 & 1 \\ 1 & -1 & 0 \end{pmatrix},
\end{equation}
where $S$ is the real antisymmetric matrix with cross-product-with-$(1,1,1)$ structure: for any K3-amplitude vector $v \in \mathbb{R}^3$, $S v = (1,1,1) \times v$ (Finding C-W21)~\cite{abshier_capotauro_subclaim_c_sketch}. The parameter $b$ is the substrate-physics parameter to be determined in \S\ref{sec:composite_we} via the chirality-eigenvalue matching principle.

\begin{lemma}[Spectral radius of $S$]\label{lem:spectral_S}
The matrix $S$ in Eq.~\eqref{eq:t_a2_generator} has eigenvalues $\{0, +i\sqrt{3}, -i\sqrt{3}\}$ with spectral radius $\sqrt{3}$. The zero eigenvalue corresponds to the eigenvector $(1,1,1)/\sqrt{3}$ (the bonding K3-amplitude $|\phi_+\rangle$); the non-zero imaginary eigenvalues $\pm i\sqrt{3}$ correspond to the K3-doublet antibonding eigenvectors. Consequently, $T_{A_2}(b) = i \cdot b \cdot S$ has eigenvalues $\{0, \pm b\sqrt{3}\}$ on the K3-amplitude space, with the non-zero eigenvalues $\pm b\sqrt{3}$ residing entirely on the K3-doublet subspace $\{\phiminusOne, \phiminusTwo\}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Direct computation: the characteristic polynomial of $S$ is $\lambda^3 + 3\lambda = \lambda(\lambda^2 + 3) = 0$, giving $\lambda \in \{0, \pm i\sqrt{3}\}$. The null vector is found by inspection: $S(1,1,1)^T = (1-1, -1+1, 1-1)^T = (0,0,0)^T$. The cross-product structure $S v = (1,1,1) \times v$ confirms the null direction is parallel to $(1,1,1)$, hence orthogonal to the K3-doublet which is the orthogonal complement of $|\phi_+\rangle = (1,1,1)/\sqrt{3}$. The non-zero eigenvalues live on the K3-doublet subspace.

For $T_{A_2}(b) = i \cdot b \cdot S$, the eigenvalues are $\{i b \cdot 0, i b \cdot (+i\sqrt{3}), i b \cdot (-i\sqrt{3})\} = \{0, -b\sqrt{3}, +b\sqrt{3}\}$ --- real and centered at zero on the K3-doublet, as required for $T_{A_2}(b)$ to be a self-adjoint chirality-like operator on the antibonding doublet.
\end{proof}

Lemma~\ref{lem:spectral_S} is the load-bearing structural input for the chirality-eigenvalue matching principle in \S\ref{sec:k3_amplitude_factor}: identifying the K3-doublet's $T_{A_2}(b)$ eigenvalues $\pm b\sqrt{3}$ with the physical chirality eigenvalues $\pm \chi$ of $\Cchi$ at substrate level determines $b = \chi/\sqrt{3}$ uniquely, fixing the substrate-physics parameter.

\section{The Composite Capotauro Wigner-Eckart Theorem}\label{sec:composite_we}

This section states and proves the paper's flagship result. The theorem and proof are the publication-grade form of Theorem 18.1 of the working sub-sketch~\cite{abshier_capotauro_subclaim_c_sketch}.\footnote{Closure declared at Session 102 Patch 0396; theorem-registry registration as THEO-CAP-1 at Session 103 Patch 0397~\cite{abshier_theorem_registry}.} The eight-step proof gathers the substrate-physics inputs from \S\ref{sec:substrate_vacuum} (FI-C-9, $|\chi| = \phig^{-3}$), the K3-doublet basis structure from \S\ref{sec:k3_doublet} (FI-C-3 + perpendicular wavefunction), and the Wigner-Eckart machinery from \S\ref{sec:d6_we} into the closed-form result $|M| = \chi/6$.

\subsection{Theorem statement}\label{sec:theorem_statement}

\begin{theorem}[Composite Capotauro Wigner-Eckart Theorem]\label{thm:composite_cap_we}
Under foundational inputs FI-C-1 through FI-C-10 (enumerated in \S\ref{sec:fi_summary}) and CPP axioms A1 (DI-bit exchange substrate primitive), A3 (substrate orientation field), A4 (substrate isotropy at vertex level), and A7 (substrate-stress framework), the chirality matrix element of the chirality observable $\Cchi$ on the tribimaximal-aligned $\Kthree$-doublet basis states $\PhiminusOne$ and $\PhiminusTwo$ (Eq.~\eqref{eq:full_basis}) is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:main_result}
\boxed{\;|M| = |\langle \Phi_-^{(1)} | \Cchi | \Phi_-^{(2)} \rangle| = \frac{\chi}{6} = \frac{\phig^{-3}}{6} \approx 0.0394.\;}
\end{equation}
The matrix element decomposes as the product of two structural factors:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:decomposition}
|M| = |M_{\Kthree}| \cdot |M_\perp| = \chi \cdot \frac{1}{6}
\end{equation}
where $|M_{\Kthree}| = \chi$ is the K3-amplitude factor (Lemma~\ref{lem:k3_factor}, chirality-eigenvalue matching principle, \S\ref{sec:k3_amplitude_factor}) and $|M_\perp| = 1/6$ is the cage-shell averaging factor (Lemma~\ref{lem:cage_shell}, cage-shell averaging principle, \S\ref{sec:cage_shell_factor}).
\end{theorem}

The theorem is the publication-grade statement of the eight-step closure trajectory documented in the sub-sketch~\cite{abshier_capotauro_subclaim_c_sketch} across Sessions 87-102. The proof proceeds in eight steps, structurally factoring the matrix element via the Wigner-Eckart machinery of \S\ref{sec:wigner_eckart_d6} and applying two derivation principles (chirality-eigenvalue matching and cage-shell averaging) to determine the two structural factors.

\subsection{Eight-step proof: overview}\label{sec:proof_overview}

The proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we} proceeds in eight steps:

\begin{enumerate}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item[\textbf{S1}] \textbf{Irrep identification}: $\Cchi \in B_2$ of $\Dsix$ via $\sigma_1$-ODD K3-amplitude content (unique $A_2$ generator $T_{A_2}(b) = i \cdot b \cdot S$, \S\ref{sec:sigma1_odd_correction}) and $\zeta$-ODD perpendicular-wavefunction content (Finding C-W11, \S\ref{sec:chirality_observable}).
    \item[\textbf{S2}] \textbf{Wigner-Eckart factorization}: the matrix element decomposes into K3-amplitude $M_{\Kthree}$ and cage-shell $M_\perp$ factors via Eq.~\eqref{eq:we_factorization}.
    \item[\textbf{S3}] \textbf{K3-amplitude part identification}: the K3-amplitude factor depends only on the $A_2$-irrep generator $T_{A_2}(b)$ on the K3-doublet subspace (irrep-uniqueness from \S\ref{sec:sigma1_odd_correction}).
    \item[\textbf{S4}] \textbf{Symbolic K3-amplitude computation}: $M_{\Kthree} = -i \cdot b \cdot \sqrt{3}$ on the TBM-aligned basis (Eq.~\eqref{eq:m_k3_symbolic}, derived from Lemma~\ref{lem:spectral_S} spectral structure).
    \item[\textbf{S5}] \textbf{Chirality-eigenvalue matching}: identifying the K3-doublet eigenvalues $\pm b\sqrt{3}$ of $T_{A_2}(b)$ with the physical chirality eigenvalues $\pm \chi$ of $\Cchi$ at substrate level gives $b = \chi/\sqrt{3}$, hence $|M_{\Kthree}| = \chi$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:k3_factor}).
    \item[\textbf{S6}] \textbf{Cage-shell averaging}: the perpendicular-wavefunction factor on the $V_\text{cage} = 12$ icosahedral cage (FI-C-6 cage-shell extension to chirality observables via FI-C-10) gives $|M_\perp| = d_E/V_\text{cage} = 2/12 = 1/6$ via Schur orthogonality (Lemma~\ref{lem:cage_shell}).
    \item[\textbf{S7}] \textbf{Composite product}: $|M| = |M_{\Kthree}| \cdot |M_\perp| = \chi \cdot (1/6) = \chi/6$.
    \item[\textbf{S8}] \textbf{Substrate-vacuum substitution}: substituting FI-C-9 ($|\chi| = \phig^{-3}$, \S\ref{sec:order_parameter}) gives $|M| = \phig^{-3}/6 \approx 0.0394$, completing Eq.~\eqref{eq:main_result}.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{The K3-amplitude factor: chirality-eigenvalue matching principle}\label{sec:k3_amplitude_factor}

\begin{lemma}[K3-amplitude factor]\label{lem:k3_factor}
Under FI-C-9 (substrate-vacuum chirality magnitude $|\chi| = \phig^{-3}$), the K3-amplitude factor in the Wigner-Eckart factorization of the chirality matrix element on the TBM-aligned $\Kthree$-doublet is
\[
|M_{\Kthree}| = |\langle \phi_-^{(1)} | T_{A_2}(b) | \phi_-^{(2)} \rangle|_{b = \chi/\sqrt{3}} = \chi.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The unique $A_2$-irrep generator on the K3-amplitude space is $T_{A_2}(b) = i \cdot b \cdot S$ (Eq.~\eqref{eq:t_a2_generator}). Direct symbolic computation on the TBM-aligned basis $\phiminusOne = (2,-1,-1)/\sqrt{6}$, $\phiminusTwo = (0,-1,1)/\sqrt{2}$ gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:m_k3_symbolic}
M_{\Kthree} = \langle \phi_-^{(1)} | T_{A_2}(b) | \phi_-^{(2)} \rangle = -i \cdot b \cdot \sqrt{3}.
\end{equation}
The imaginary phase $-i$ is intrinsic to the $A_2$-irrep generator (it reflects the antisymmetric structure of $S$); the magnitude $b\sqrt{3}$ tracks the spectral radius of $S$ on the K3-amplitude subspace (Lemma~\ref{lem:spectral_S}).

To determine the substrate-physics parameter $b$, we apply the \emph{chirality-eigenvalue matching principle} (Finding C-W23)~\cite{abshier_capotauro_subclaim_c_sketch}. The K3-doublet eigenvalues of $T_{A_2}(b)$ are $\pm b\sqrt{3}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:spectral_S}). The physical chirality eigenvalues of $\Cchi$ at substrate level are $\pm \chi$ (the substrate primitive chirality magnitudes, FI-C-9 \S\ref{sec:order_parameter}: the eigenvalues of the chirality observable are $\pm |\chi|$ for the two enantiomorphs distinguished by the substrate's primitive chirality). Identifying:
\[
b\sqrt{3} = \chi \;\Longrightarrow\; b = \frac{\chi}{\sqrt{3}}.
\]
Substituting into Eq.~\eqref{eq:m_k3_symbolic}:
\[
M_{\Kthree}\big|_{b = \chi/\sqrt{3}} = -i \cdot \frac{\chi}{\sqrt{3}} \cdot \sqrt{3} = -i\chi.
\]
The magnitude is $|M_{\Kthree}| = \chi$.
\end{proof}

The chirality-eigenvalue matching principle (Finding C-W23) is the load-bearing structural step that determines the substrate-physics parameter $b$ uniquely from the substrate primitive chirality $\chi$. Without this identification, the parameter $b$ would remain free; with it, the matrix element acquires its $\chi$-dependence at theorem level.

\subsection{The cage-shell averaging factor: cage-shell averaging principle}\label{sec:cage_shell_factor}

The cage-shell averaging factor $|M_\perp| = 1/6$ is the second structural factor in the Wigner-Eckart decomposition. Before formalizing this in Lemma~\ref{lem:cage_shell}, this subsection establishes the physical motivation for why Schur orthogonality on the icosahedral cage is the correct averaging mechanism for chirality observables --- not just a mathematically suggestive coincidence. The motivation rests on two CPP axioms (A4 substrate isotropy at vertex level, A3 substrate orientation field / DI-bit propagation) and shows that the formal Schur-orthogonality result emerges as the structural consequence of these axioms applied to the cage geometry.

\subsubsection{Physical motivation: substrate isotropy + DI-bit propagation}\label{sec:cage_shell_factor_motivation}

The cage-shell extension to chirality observables (FI-C-10) is the load-bearing claim that the cage-shell coupling mechanism for chirality observables follows the same averaging structure as for mass observables (FI-C-6). At v1.0, FI-C-10 is registered as a foundational postulate (\S\ref{sec:fi_summary}); first-principles derivation from CPP axioms is registered as open work (\S\ref{sec:open_fi_c10}). The v1.0 paper does not derive FI-C-10 first-principles, but establishes physical plausibility through three complementary arguments.

\textbf{Intuition for the $1/6$ factor.} Before the formal arguments, the geometric picture: the K3-doublet has $d_E = 2$ basis states (the two members of the doublet); the icosahedral cage hosts $V_\text{cage} = 12$ symmetry-equivalent vertices. Under substrate isotropy, the chirality bias distributes its contribution uniformly across the cage --- two doublet degrees of freedom averaged over twelve cage positions yields the averaging factor $2/12 = 1/6$. The three formal arguments below justify why this geometric picture is the correct one structurally, and not an ansatz.

\textbf{Argument 1: Substrate-isotropy + irrep averaging (A4-based).}
CPP axiom A4 states the substrate is isotropic at the vertex level: no preferred direction emerges from substrate dynamics at individual vertices. The substrate's primitive chirality bias $\chi$ is a global property (\S\ref{sec:substrate_vacuum}), not a vertex-local property. Schur orthogonality is the mathematical expression of ``no preferred direction within an irreducible representation'': for an irrep $\Gamma$ of dimension $d_\Gamma$, summing $\Gamma$-projected quantities over the irrep basis produces a group-invariant average with factor $d_\Gamma / |G|$.

For $\Cchi$ acting on $\Kthree$-doublet $E$-irrep states extended over the icosahedral cage: the K3-doublet is $E$-irrep with $d_E = 2$; the cage hosts $V_\text{cage} = 12$ vertices, invariant under $\Dsix = \Sthree \times \mathbb{Z}_2$ (\S\ref{sec:k3_stabilizer}); A4 isotropy at the cage-vertex level prevents preferred-direction projections; the cage-level chirality observable is therefore the symmetric irrep-average over the $E$-doublet projections with factor $d_E/|\Dsix| = 2/12 = 1/6$. This is the Schur-orthogonality coefficient for the $E$-irrep on a group of order 12.

\textbf{Argument 2: DI-bit propagation paths + path-averaging (A3-based).}
CPP axiom A3 governs the substrate orientation field through DI-bit (Direction Information bit) exchanges between vertices. The chirality observable's value at a cage location is determined by DI-bit propagation paths from the substrate-vacuum reference frame to that location. For the icosahedral cage: propagation paths to any cage vertex traverse the cage's $\Dsix$ symmetry; the total cage-level $\Cchi$ is the symmetric sum over all $|\Dsix| = 12$ symmetry-equivalent paths with the chirality bias entering as the $E$-irrep projection at each endpoint; the $E$-irrep-relevant channels number $d_E = 2$; the averaged contribution per path is $d_E/|\Dsix| = 1/6$. The propagation-path argument and the Schur-orthogonality argument produce the same answer because they express the same structural principle from different starting points: cage symmetry of order 12 and doublet representation of dimension 2.

\textbf{Argument 3: Precedent from cage-shell mass-observable extension (FI-C-6).}
The cage-shell averaging mechanism is not introduced for the first time at FI-C-10. SF-4 v4.0~\cite{abshier_sf4} establishes the same mechanism for the \emph{mass} observable (FI-C-6) at theorem level via the Composite K3-Cage-Shell Coupling Theorem (THEO-SF-4-5), with the same averaging factor $d_E/V_\text{cage} = 1/6$ on the same icosahedral cage and end-to-end numerical verification across the charged-lepton mass hierarchy. FI-C-10 extends this from the mass observable to the broader class of $\Dsix$-equivariant observables on K3-doublet states, in particular $\Cchi$ (which transforms in $B_2$ of $\Dsix$). The extension is the conjecture that cage-shell coupling is observable-class-independent under substrate isotropy --- the load-bearing claim of FI-C-10.

The precedent argument strengthens FI-C-10 plausibility on three grounds: (i) the mechanism is an established theorem-level result extended by observable class, not a new construction invented for the chirality case; (ii) the same numerical $1/6$ emerges from the same cage geometry and irrep structure, providing a consistency check; (iii) the only step requiring justification at FI-C-10 is the observable-class-independence claim, narrower than ``Schur orthogonality on the cage produces a physical averaging factor'' in general. The observable-class-independence claim has registered first-principles closure as open work in \S\ref{sec:open_fi_c10}: a future derivation showing the cage-shell mechanism is genuinely observable-class-independent (e.g., via DI-bit propagation paths not coupling to the irrep content) closes the FI-C-10 gap at theorem level.

\textbf{Why $V_\text{cage} = |\Dsix| = 12$ is not coincidence.}
The structural identity $V_\text{cage} = |\Dsix| = 12$ has physical content: the icosahedral cage hosting the K3-doublet has exactly the order of the K3-doublet's stabilizer group. This is a consequence of the substrate's geometric structure --- the 600-cell's icosahedral first shell hosts the same $\Sthree \times \mathbb{Z}_2$ that stabilizes the K3 base extended over the cage. The two ways of writing the averaging factor ($d_E/V_\text{cage}$ from vertex-count averaging or $d_E/|\Dsix|$ from group-order averaging) reflect the same underlying isotropy structure, not numerical coincidence.

\textbf{What this motivation establishes (and does not establish).}
The three arguments above establish \emph{physical plausibility} for FI-C-10's averaging at $|M_\perp| = 1/6$. They show that the averaging factor is consistent with two independent CPP axioms (A4 + A3); is fixed by cage geometry and irrep structure with no free parameter; agrees with the formal Schur-orthogonality result of Lemma~\ref{lem:cage_shell}; has theorem-level precedent in the mass observable (FI-C-6 via THEO-SF-4-5) extended by observable class.

What the arguments do \emph{not} establish: (a) a first-principles derivation of FI-C-10 from CPP axioms A1--A11 --- the observable-class-independence claim distinguishing FI-C-10 from FI-C-6 is physically motivated but not formally derived from the axiom stack; sub-claim work (\S\ref{sec:open_fi_c10}) is required; (b) the uniqueness of FI-C-10 at theorem level --- an alternative cage-shell mechanism could in principle yield a different chirality-observable factor; ruling out alternatives requires the full substrate-dynamics derivation. The motivation establishes FI-C-10 as the natural reading of cage-shell coupling under substrate isotropy + DI-bit propagation + FI-C-6 precedent, not an ansatz. At v1.0, the formal proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:cage_shell} proceeds from FI-C-10 as foundational input; first-principles closure is future work.

\subsubsection{Formal statement and proof}\label{sec:cage_shell_factor_lemma}

\begin{lemma}[Cage-shell averaging factor]\label{lem:cage_shell}
Under FI-C-10 (cage-shell extension to chirality observables, \S\ref{sec:fi_summary}), the perpendicular-wavefunction factor in the Wigner-Eckart factorization of the chirality matrix element on the icosahedral cage hosting the charged-lepton K3-doublet states is
\[
|M_\perp| = |\langle \chi_+ | T_\perp | \chi_- \rangle| = \frac{d_E}{V_\text{cage}} = \frac{2}{12} = \frac{1}{6}.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The K3-doublet states $\{\PhiminusOne, \PhiminusTwo\}$ are extended over the $V_\text{cage} = 12$ icosahedral cage via FI-C-6 (cage-shell coupling for mass observable). FI-C-10 extends this coupling structure from mass observables to the broader class of $D_6$-equivariant observables on K3-doublet states, in particular to the chirality observable $\Cchi$ (\S\ref{sec:fi_summary}).\footnote{FI-C-10 registered at Session 97 Patch 0391~\cite{abshier_capotauro_subclaim_c_sketch}.}

Under this extension, the perpendicular-wavefunction matrix element on the $V_\text{cage} = 12$ icosahedral cage acquires a Schur-orthogonality averaging factor for the $E$-irrep doublet observable:
\[
|M_\perp| = \frac{d_E}{V_\text{cage}}
\]
where $d_E = 2$ is the dimension of the $E$-irrep of $\Dsix$ on the K3-doublet space and $V_\text{cage} = 12$ is the number of vertices in the icosahedral cage. Direct computation:
\[
|M_\perp| = \frac{2}{12} = \frac{1}{6}.
\]
Equivalently via the structural identity $V_\text{cage} = |\Dsix| = 12$:
\[
|M_\perp| = \frac{d_E}{|\Dsix|} = \frac{2}{12} = \frac{1}{6},
\]
which is the standard Schur orthogonality factor for $E$-irrep doublet observables on a group of order 12.
\end{proof}

The cage-shell averaging principle (Findings C-W25, C-W26)~\cite{abshier_capotauro_subclaim_c_sketch} is the second load-bearing structural step. It converts the icosahedral cage's vertex count $V_\text{cage} = 12$ into the perpendicular-wavefunction factor $1/6$. The structural identity $V_\text{cage} = |\Dsix| = 12$ is a non-trivial coincidence: the cage hosting the charged-lepton K3-doublet states has exactly the order of the K3 stabilizer, allowing the cage-shell averaging factor to be expressed equivalently via vertex-count or group-order formulas.

\subsection{Composite product and substrate-vacuum substitution}\label{sec:composite_product}

Assembling Lemma~\ref{lem:k3_factor} and Lemma~\ref{lem:cage_shell} into the Wigner-Eckart factorization Eq.~\eqref{eq:we_factorization}:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:composite_product}
|M| = |M_{\Kthree}| \cdot |M_\perp| = \chi \cdot \frac{1}{6} = \frac{\chi}{6}.
\end{equation}
Substituting the FI-C-9 primitive chirality magnitude $|\chi| = \phig^{-3}$ (\S\ref{sec:order_parameter}):
\[
|M| = \frac{\phig^{-3}}{6} \approx \frac{0.236}{6} \approx 0.0394.
\]
This completes the eight-step proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we}.

\subsection{End-to-end numerical verification}\label{sec:numerical_verification}

The theorem's predictions can be verified by direct numerical substitution at machine precision. With $\phig = (1+\sqrt{5})/2 \approx 1.6180339887498949$:
\begin{itemize}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item $\chi = \phig^{-3} = 0.2360679774997897 \ldots$
    \item $b = \chi/\sqrt{3} = 0.1362927\ldots$ (substrate-physics parameter)
    \item $|M_{\Kthree}| = \chi = 0.2360679774997897 \ldots$ (K3-amplitude factor)
    \item $|M_\perp| = 1/6 = 0.1666666666666667 \ldots$ (cage-shell averaging factor)
    \item $|M| = \chi/6 = 0.0393446629166316 \ldots$ (composite product)
\end{itemize}
End-to-end consistency: $|M_{\Kthree}| \cdot |M_\perp| = 0.2360679774997897 \cdot 0.1666666666666667 = 0.0393446629166316$, matching the direct computation $\chi/6$ to machine precision $10^{-17}$. The numerical verification matches at every intermediate checkpoint, consistent with the eight-step proof's exact algebraic structure.

\subsection{Foundational inputs and axiom accounting}\label{sec:fi_axiom_accounting}

The theorem's conditional closure rests on ten foundational inputs (FI-C-1 through FI-C-10, enumerated in \S\ref{sec:fi_summary}) and four CPP axioms (A1, A3, A4, A7). The CPP axioms most load-bearing per the Picture B substrate-orientation-field framework~\cite{abshier_capotauro_closure_sketch} are A3 (substrate orientation field) and A7 (substrate-stress framework); A1 (DI-bit exchange) and A4 (substrate isotropy at vertex level) are foundational supporting axioms. The full foundational-input enumeration is presented in \S\ref{sec:fi_summary}; the cross-sector inheritance pattern (FI-C-3 from SF-4 v4.0; FI-C-4, FI-C-5 from SF-2 v1.0; FI-C-6 from SF-4 v3.0; FI-C-2, FI-C-7 from SM-1, SM-2) is discussed in \S\ref{sec:cross_sector_inheritance}.

\subsection{Connection to SF-4 v4.0 cross-sector inheritance}\label{sec:sf4_inheritance_section}

The single most critical cross-sector inheritance for Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we} is FI-C-3: the K3 antibonding-doublet TBM-aligned basis at theorem level, inherited from SF-4 v4.0's Composite K3-Cage-Shell Coupling Theorem (THEO-SF-4-5)~\cite{abshier_sf4}. Without this inheritance, the matrix element $|M|$ would be defined on a basis whose theorem-level rigor is itself open; SF-4 v4.0 closes that openness at conditional theorem closure level (the first cross-sector closure in CPP, simultaneously resolving SM-5's foundational op:nu\_id open problem)~\cite{abshier_sf4}.

The cross-sector inheritance pattern templates future CPP closures: the Capotauro paper provides the chirality matrix element $|M|$ as substrate-physics input to SF-2 v2.0+ (Q11 $\sin^2\theta_{13}$ derivation, \S\ref{sec:sin2theta13}); SF-2 v2.0+ in turn will provide PMNS observable inputs to downstream Capotauro sub-claim work on $\delta_{CP}$ and $\eta_B$. The closure web's increasing density is the structural manifestation of CPP's cross-sector unification thesis.

\section{The W-Bracelet Sector: Cross-Sector Unification with the K3-Doublet}\label{sec:w_bracelet_sector}

The K3-doublet derivation chain of \S\S\ref{sec:k3_doublet}--\ref{sec:composite_we} establishes the chirality matrix element $|M^{\Kthree}| = \chi/6$ on the mass-mixing sector. This section extends the same substrate-physics machinery to a second sector --- the SF-2 W-bracelet substrate object hosting the V--A coupling in $W^\pm$-mediated electroweak interactions --- and derives the chirality matrix element $|M^W| = \chi/6$ on that sector, identical in magnitude to the K3-doublet's. The result is registered programme-level as THEO-SD-CHIR-1 (Cross-Sector Substrate Chirality Unification Theorem; theorem \#63 in the CPP theorem-registry~\cite{abshier_theorem_registry}, registered Patch 0434 Session 132) and constitutes the first full Layer 3 cross-sector unification result under the \emph{OPEN-SD-CHIR-PRIMITIVE} umbrella~\cite{abshier_research_frontier} (umbrella registered Patch 0422). The four-step proof chain ((i) Local-$I_h$-Preservation; (ii) Substrate-Locality Unification; (iii) Cage-shell factor identity; (iv) Pairing-convention identification via icosahedral-center inversion) is developed in working sketch \S14--\S17~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch}.

The structural content of the unification is: the W-bracelet's substrate chirality content is sourced from the \emph{same} substrate parameter $\chi \equiv \epsilon = \phig^{-3}$ that feeds the K3-doublet's chirality matrix element. Both sectors inherit $\chi$ from the local-$I_h$-preservation theorem of \S\ref{sec:substrate_locality} via Substrate-Locality Unification (\S\ref{sec:w_substrate_locality_unification}), and both compute their respective composite matrix elements via the same algebraic structure --- amplitude factor $\chi$ (chirality-eigenvalue matching) times cage-shell factor $1/6$ (Schur orthogonality on a 2D matter-doublet irrep over a 12-element stabilizer) --- on isomorphic-but-distinct $\Dsix \subset H_3 = I_h$ sub-stabilizers.

\subsection{The W-bracelet on the host vertex first-shell icosahedron}\label{sec:w_bracelet_setup}

Per SF-2 v1.0 Theorem~4.2~\cite{abshier_sf2}, the W-bracelet is the $\Hfour$-orbit $\mathcal{O}_\mathcal{B}$ of 1{,}200 induced hexagonal 6-cycles in the 600-cell vertex graph, with stabilizer the dihedral group $\Dsix$ of order 12. Under the vertex-aligned Reading C of \S\ref{sec:h4_i4} (FI-C-RC-2), the geometric realization of the W-bracelet on the substrate is sharpened:

\begin{itemize}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item \textbf{Petrie-hexagon realization.} Each W-bracelet is geometrically a Petrie hexagon of the first-shell icosahedron centered at a 600-cell vertex (sketch \S10, Patch 0418, Finding C-W36)~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch}. There are 10 Petrie-hexagon subgroups of the icosahedral $H_3 = I_h$, corresponding to the 10 distinct 3-fold rotation axes of the icosahedron in dual configuration; equivalently, the 1{,}200 W-bracelets in the substrate distribute as 10 bracelets per 600-cell host vertex $\times$ 120 host vertices.
    \item \textbf{On-axis with $\hat{n}$ at radius $\phig/2$.} Under vertex-aligned Reading C ($\hat{n} = v_{\text{host}}$, FI-C-RC-2), the 10 W-bracelets centered on $v_{\text{host}}$ have centroid $c_W = (\phig/2)\hat{n}$ exactly --- on the $\hat{n}$ axis (Finding C-W36)~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch}. By contrast, the K3-base (\S\ref{sec:k3_base}) sits transversely off-axis at $\hat{c}_K \cdot \hat{n} = \sqrt{3}/2$ ($30°$ off-axis). The two substrate objects are distinguished by transverse centroid placement: K3-base 3-fold off-axis, W-bracelet 6-fold on-axis.
    \item \textbf{6 vertices among the 12 first-shell.} The W-bracelet's 6 hexagonal vertices are 6 of the 12 first-shell vertices of the icosahedron at $v_{\text{host}}$ (the other 6 first-shell vertices participate in the bracelet's antipodal partners; \S\ref{sec:w_zeta_inversion}). All 6 satisfy the first-shell-coordination identity $v_i \cdot v_{\text{host}} = \phig/2$.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Substrate-Locality Unification (Finding C-W40)}\label{sec:w_substrate_locality_unification}

Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation} (\S\ref{sec:local_ih_preservation_theorem}) was proved as a structural statement about any subset of first-shell vertices at $v_{\text{host}}$: under vertex-aligned Reading C, the substrate edge perturbation pattern (Eq.~\eqref{eq:edge_perturbation}) preserves the local $I_h$ symmetry of the first-shell icosahedron at $\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)$, with isotropic radial scaling $(1 - \epsilon/(2\phig))$ on host-to-first-shell edges and zero perturbation on first-shell-to-first-shell edges (Eqs.~\eqref{eq:host_to_first_shell_inner_product}--\eqref{eq:first_shell_to_first_shell_inner_product}). The theorem applies uniformly to the W-bracelet's 6 first-shell vertices.

\begin{corollary}[Substrate-Locality Unification on first-shell substrate objects]\label{cor:substrate_locality_unification}
Under vertex-aligned Reading C (FI-C-RC-1 + FI-C-RC-2, \S\ref{sec:h4_i4}) with Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation} in force, any substrate object built from first-shell vertices of the icosahedron at $v_{\text{host}}$ inherits the substrate-level chirality magnitude $\chi \equiv \epsilon = \phig^{-3}$ from the same identification of \S\ref{sec:chi_epsilon_identification} (Lemma~\ref{lem:chi_epsilon}). In particular:
\begin{enumerate}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item The K3-base (3-vertex triangular face of the first-shell icosahedron; Finding C-W37, \S\ref{sec:h4_i4}) inherits $\chi$.
    \item The W-bracelet (6-vertex Petrie hexagon of the first-shell icosahedron; Finding C-W36, \S\ref{sec:w_bracelet_setup}) inherits $\chi$.
    \item Any other first-shell-vertex substrate object (e.g., qDP/eDP substrate objects at first-shell positions; \S\ref{sec:cross_sector_inheritance}, forthcoming v2.0 sections) inherits $\chi$.
\end{enumerate}
The chirality content at substrate level is sector-independent: a single substrate parameter $\chi$, sourced from FI-C-RC-1's primitive 4D direction $\hat{n}$, feeds each sector's chirality matrix element calculation via cage-shell averaging on the sector's stabilizer.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Direct application of Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation}. The substrate-level identification $\chi \equiv \epsilon$ from Lemma~\ref{lem:chi_epsilon} is global at substrate level --- it is the magnitude of the $\hat{n}$-induced edge-perturbation parameter --- and does not depend on which first-shell substrate object is being considered. Each sector's chirality matrix element calculation accesses the substrate parameter via its sector-specific cage-shell averaging on its sector-specific stabilizer subgroup of the substrate residual $H_3 = I_h$, but the substrate parameter feeding the calculation is the same $\chi$ across all sectors. $\square$
\end{proof}

This is Finding C-W40 of the working sketch (\S14, Patch 0425 Session 130)~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch}, registered programme-level as the Substrate-Locality Unification structural foundation for the OPEN-SD-CHIR-PRIMITIVE umbrella's cross-sector unification claims.

\subsection{The W-bracelet $\Dsix$ stabilizer and matter-doublet $E$-irrep}\label{sec:w_d6_stabilizer}

The W-bracelet stabilizer is the dihedral group $\Dsix$ of order 12 (SF-2 v1.0 Theorem~4.2~\cite{abshier_sf2}). As an abstract group, $\Dsix \cong \Sthree \times \mathbb{Z}_2$ --- isomorphic to the K3-doublet's $\Dsix$ stabilizer (\S\ref{sec:k3_stabilizer}) but in a different conjugacy class within the substrate residual $H_3 = I_h$: the K3-doublet's $\Dsix$ is one of 10 $D_{3d}$ sub-stabilizers (at the icosahedron's 10 3-fold rotation axes), while the W-bracelet's $\Dsix$ is one of 10 Petrie-polygon sub-stabilizers (at the icosahedron's 10 hexagonal Petrie polygons in dual configuration).

The W-bracelet's 6 hexagonal vertices form a single transitive $\Dsix$-orbit with vertex stabilizer $C_2$ of order 2:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:w_orbit_stabilizer}
V_{\text{bracelet}} \;=\; |\Dsix|/|C_2| \;=\; 12/2 \;=\; 6.
\end{equation}
The 6-vertex permutation representation of $\Dsix$, with permutation character $\chi^{\text{perm}} = (6, 0, 0, 0, 2, 0)$ on the six $\Dsix$ conjugacy classes $\{e, r^3, \{r, r^5\}, \{r^2, r^4\}, \sigma_v, \sigma_d\}$ (class sizes $\{1, 1, 2, 2, 3, 3\}$), decomposes as:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:w_perm_decomposition}
\chi^{\text{perm}}_{\text{bracelet}} \;=\; A_1 \;\oplus\; B_1 \;\oplus\; E_1 \;\oplus\; E_2,
\end{equation}
verified by Schur inner-product decomposition against the standard $\Dsix$ character table (dimension check: $1 + 1 + 2 + 2 = 6 \checkmark$)~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch}.\footnote{Working sketch \S15.4 (Patch 0427 Session 131) gives the explicit Schur inner-product computation: $a_{A_1} = (6 + 6)/12 = 1$; $a_{A_2} = (6 - 6)/12 = 0$; $a_{B_1} = (6 + 6)/12 = 1$; $a_{B_2} = (6 - 6)/12 = 0$; $a_{E_1} = (12 + 0)/12 = 1$; $a_{E_2} = (12 + 0)/12 = 1$.}

\textbf{V--A current irrep identification.} The W-bracelet's V--A coupling content arises from the bracelet's $120°/240°$ phase bias under $\Dsix$ symmetry (alternating-polarity placement of $3 \times (+eCP)$, $3 \times (-eCP)$, $3 \times (+qCP)$, $3 \times (-qCP)$; SF-2 v1.0 PROP-SF-2-5~\cite{abshier_sf2}). The $\Dsix$ irrep whose $C_6$ generator has eigenvalues $e^{\pm 2\pi i/3}$ (matching the $120°/240°$ phase bias) is $E_2$; both 2D irreps $E_1, E_2$ are present in the permutation decomposition Eq.~\eqref{eq:w_perm_decomposition} with multiplicity 1 each, and the phase-bias argument selects $E_2$ as the carrier of the W-bracelet's V--A chirality content. For the Schur-orthogonality cage-shell factor computation, the load-bearing input is the matter-doublet's dimension $d_\Gamma = 2$, which both $E_1$ and $E_2$ satisfy.

\subsection{The icosahedral-center inversion $\zeta^W$ (Finding C-W43)}\label{sec:w_zeta_inversion}

The W-bracelet's $\Dsix = \Sthree \times \mathbb{Z}_2$ realization on the substrate has a concrete geometric content: the $\mathbb{Z}_2$ generator $\zeta^W = r^3$ (the half-turn central element of dihedral $\Dsix$) is realized in 4D ambient as the \emph{icosahedral-center inversion}.

\begin{definition}[Icosahedral-center inversion $\zeta^W$]\label{def:zeta_w}
The W-bracelet's $\mathbb{Z}_2$ generator $\zeta^W$ is the affine inversion in $\mathbb{R}^4$ through the W-bracelet centroid $c_W = (\phig/2)\hat{n}$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:w_zeta_definition}
\zeta^W : p \mapsto \phig\hat{n} - p.
\end{equation}
$\zeta^W$ has linear part $-I$ (which flips $\hat{n} \to -\hat{n}$, hence is chirality-flipping at substrate level) plus a translation by $\phig\hat{n}$ that returns the W-bracelet centroid to itself. The linear-part $-I$ realization establishes $\zeta^W$ as an inversion in the standard sense.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[$\zeta^W$ stabilizes the W-bracelet via antipodal-pair structure]\label{lem:zeta_w_antipodal}
For any first-shell vertex $v$ of the 600-cell satisfying $v \cdot \hat{n} = \phig/2$ and $|v|^2 = 1$, the image $\zeta^W v = \phig\hat{n} - v$ satisfies $(\zeta^W v) \cdot \hat{n} = \phig/2$ (still on the first shell of $v_{\text{host}}$) and $|\zeta^W v|^2 = 1$ (still on the unit 3-sphere $S^3$). The 6 W-bracelet vertices factor as 3 antipodal pairs $\{v_i, \zeta^W v_i\}_{i=1,2,3}$ under $\zeta^W$; the bracelet's $\Dsix = \Sthree \times \mathbb{Z}_2$ acts as $\Sthree$ permuting the 3 antipodal pairs and $\mathbb{Z}_2 = \langle \zeta^W \rangle$ swapping the 2 elements within each pair.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For $v \in S^3$ with $v \cdot \hat{n} = \phig/2$: 
\[
(\phig\hat{n} - v) \cdot \hat{n} \;=\; \phig - v \cdot \hat{n} \;=\; \phig - \phig/2 \;=\; \phig/2,
\]
and 
\[
|\phig\hat{n} - v|^2 \;=\; \phig^2 - 2\phig(v \cdot \hat{n}) + |v|^2 \;=\; \phig^2 - \phig^2 + 1 \;=\; 1.
\]
So $\zeta^W v$ remains in the first-shell hyperplane $\{x \in S^3 : x \cdot \hat{n} = \phig/2\}$. Since the first-shell hyperplane intersects $S^3$ in a 2-sphere on which the icosahedron's 12 vertices sit, $\zeta^W$ maps each first-shell vertex to its antipodal partner within the first-shell icosahedron (the diametrically opposite first-shell vertex in the embedding 2-sphere). The W-bracelet's 6 vertices come in 3 such antipodal pairs by Petrie-hexagon symmetry. The $\Dsix = \Sthree \times \mathbb{Z}_2$ realization on 3 pairs $\times$ 2 within-pair follows from the standard dihedral structure decomposition. $\square$
\end{proof}

The antipodal-pair structure is the concrete geometric content of the W-bracelet's chirality-flipping $\mathbb{Z}_2$ generator. Verification via the 6-vertex permutation representation: under the $\Sthree \times \mathbb{Z}_2 \leftrightarrow \Dsix$ irrep identification ($A_1 \otimes \mathbb{1} = A_1$; $A_1 \otimes \sigma = B_1$; $E \otimes \mathbb{1} = E_2$; $E \otimes \sigma = E_1$), the permutation decomposition Eq.~\eqref{eq:w_perm_decomposition} reads $A_1 \oplus B_1 \oplus E_2 \oplus E_1 = (A_1 \otimes \mathbb{1}) \oplus (A_1 \otimes \sigma) \oplus (E \otimes \mathbb{1}) \oplus (E \otimes \sigma)$, consistent with the Lemma~\ref{lem:zeta_w_antipodal} factorization.

\subsection{The W-bracelet doublet basis and $\sigma_1^W \zeta^W$-EVEN pairing}\label{sec:w_doublet_basis}

The W-bracelet's matter-doublet basis is constructed by direct analog of the K3-doublet's pairing convention (\S\ref{sec:k3_tbm_basis}, Finding C-W12)~\cite{abshier_capotauro_subclaim_c_sketch}. The $\Sthree$-side amplitude factors live in the standard 2D $E$-irrep on the 3 antipodal pairs:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:w_amplitude_basis}
\psi_-^{(1)} \;=\; \frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}(2, -1, -1), \qquad \psi_-^{(2)} \;=\; \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(0, -1, 1),
\end{equation}
where the 3 components correspond to the 3 antipodal pairs of bracelet vertices. $\psi_-^{(1)}$ is $\sigma_1^W$-EVEN under the $\Sthree$ reflection $\sigma_1^W$ that fixes pair~1 and swaps pairs~2,~3 (components~2 and~3 equal); $\psi_-^{(2)}$ is $\sigma_1^W$-ODD (components~2 and~3 negate). This is identical algebraic structure to the K3-doublet's amplitude factors (\S\ref{sec:k3_amplitude_factor}); the physical content differs only in that $\Sthree$ acts on 3 antipodal pairs (W-bracelet) rather than 3 K3 vertices.

The $\mathbb{Z}_2$-side perpendicular wavefunctions are the $\zeta^W$-EVEN/ODD combinations on the within-pair structure:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:w_perpendicular_basis}
\chi_+^W \;=\; \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(|v\rangle + |\zeta^W v\rangle), \qquad \chi_-^W \;=\; \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(|v\rangle - |\zeta^W v\rangle),
\end{equation}
with $\chi_+^W$ $\zeta^W$-EVEN (symmetric within-pair combination) and $\chi_-^W$ $\zeta^W$-ODD (antisymmetric). Parallel to K3's $\chi_\pm$ (\S\ref{sec:perpendicular_wavefunction}), the $\chi_\pm^W$ are EVEN/ODD components of the substrate orientation field at the W-bracelet location under the chirality-flipping operation $\zeta^W$. The structural distinction from K3 is concrete: K3's antipodal partners lie \emph{outside} the K3-base (on the antipodal face of the icosahedron), so K3's $\chi_\pm$ are 1-dim abstract degrees of freedom; W-bracelet's antipodal partners lie \emph{within} the bracelet (Lemma~\ref{lem:zeta_w_antipodal}), so $\chi_\pm^W$ are concrete linear combinations of bracelet vertex states. The abstract algebraic structure is identical~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch}.\footnote{Working sketch \S17.6 (Patch 0429 Session 131) develops the concrete-vs-abstract realization distinction.}

Applying the $\sigma_1^W \zeta^W$-EVEN pairing convention (the K3-analog Finding C-W12 pairing) to the W-bracelet:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:w_doublet_basis}
\PsiminusOne^W \;=\; \psi_-^{(1)} \otimes \chi_+^W \;\in\; E_2(\Dsix), \qquad
\PsiminusTwo^W \;=\; \psi_-^{(2)} \otimes \chi_-^W \;\in\; E_1(\Dsix).
\end{equation}
Both basis states satisfy $\sigma_1^W \zeta^W$-EVEN: EVEN $\times$ EVEN $=$ EVEN for $\Psi_-^{(1)}$; ODD $\times$ ODD $=$ EVEN for $\Psi_-^{(2)}$. The W-bracelet doublet basis spans 1-dim in $E_2$ and 1-dim in $E_1$, exactly parallel to the K3-doublet's $E_2 \oplus E_1$ structure --- in neither sector is the doublet a pure $\Dsix$ irrep, but rather a specific 2D projection of $E_2 \oplus E_1$ under the EVEN-pairing convention.

\subsection{The W-bracelet chirality matrix element $|M^W| = \chi/6$}\label{sec:w_matrix_element}

The chirality operator at the W-bracelet, sourced from the substrate primitive direction $\hat{n}$, transforms under $\Dsix = \Sthree \times \mathbb{Z}_2$ as:
\[
\Cchi^W \;\in\; B_2(\Dsix) \;=\; A_2(\Sthree) \otimes \sigma(\mathbb{Z}_2),
\]
with $A_2$ on the $\Sthree$ side (the sign-of-permutation 1D irrep) and $\sigma$ on the $\mathbb{Z}_2$ side ($\zeta^W$-ODD, since $\zeta^W$ flips $\hat{n}$ and hence chirality). This is the same $B_2(\Dsix)$ assignment as the K3-doublet's chirality operator (\S\ref{sec:chirality_observable}), confirmed via Wigner-Eckart bookkeeping:
\begin{itemize}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item \textbf{$\Sthree$ side.} $E \otimes A_2 \otimes E = E \otimes E = A_1 \oplus A_2 \oplus E$ contains $A_1(\Sthree) \checkmark$.
    \item \textbf{$\mathbb{Z}_2$ side.} $\mathbb{1} \otimes \sigma \otimes \sigma = \mathbb{1}$ contains the trivial irrep $\checkmark$.
    \item \textbf{Composite.} $A_1(\Sthree) \otimes \mathbb{1}(\mathbb{Z}_2) = A_1(\Dsix)$: matrix element $\langle E_2 | B_2 | E_1 \rangle$ is non-vanishing.
\end{itemize}
The $\sigma_1^W \zeta^W$-EVEN pairing convention of \S\ref{sec:w_doublet_basis} is \emph{load-bearing} for the non-vanishing: without the opposite-parity within-pair pairing (e.g., if both doublet states were in pure $E_2$), the $\mathbb{Z}_2$-side calculation would give $\mathbb{1} \otimes \sigma \otimes \mathbb{1} = \sigma$ (not trivial), and the matrix element would vanish.

The matrix element factorizes across the $\Sthree$ and $\mathbb{Z}_2$ tensor factors:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:w_matrix_element_factorization}
|M^W| \;=\; \bigl|\langle \PsiminusOne^W \,|\, \Cchi^W \,|\, \PsiminusTwo^W \rangle\bigr| \;=\; \underbrace{\bigl|\langle \psi_-^{(1)} \,|\, T_{A_2}(b^W) \,|\, \psi_-^{(2)} \rangle\bigr|}_{|M_{\text{amp}}^W|} \cdot \underbrace{\bigl|\langle \chi_+^W \,|\, T_\perp \,|\, \chi_-^W \rangle\bigr|}_{|M_\perp^W|}.
\end{equation}

\textbf{Amplitude factor.} The $A_2$-irrep generator $T_{A_2}(b^W) = i b^W S^W$ on the 3-antipodal-pair $E(\Sthree)$ basis has the same spectral structure as K3's $T_{A_2}(b)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:spectral_S}): eigenvalues $\{0, \pm b^W \sqrt{3}\}$ on the $E$-doublet by abstract $\Sthree$ representation theory. Applying the chirality-eigenvalue matching principle (FI-C-7, Finding C-W23~\cite{abshier_capotauro_subclaim_c_sketch}) --- the same principle that fixes $b = \chi/\sqrt{3}$ on the K3-doublet via identification of $T_{A_2}$ eigenvalues with substrate-level chirality eigenvalues $\pm\chi$ of $\Cchi$ --- gives $b^W = \chi/\sqrt{3}$, so
\begin{equation}\label{eq:w_amplitude_factor}
|M_{\text{amp}}^W| \;=\; b^W \sqrt{3} \;=\; \chi.
\end{equation}

\textbf{Cage-shell factor.} The cage-shell averaging on the W-bracelet is via the same icosahedral cage that hosts the K3-doublet --- per Corollary~\ref{cor:substrate_locality_unification}, both K3-doublet and W-bracelet sit in the first-shell icosahedron at $v_{\text{host}}$. The cage-shell factor is therefore $|M_\perp^W| = d_\Gamma / V_{\text{cage}} = 2/12 = 1/6$ where $d_\Gamma = 2$ is the dimension of the W-bracelet matter-doublet's $E(\Sthree)$ irrep and $V_{\text{cage}} = 12$ is the icosahedral cage vertex count shared with the K3-doublet~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch}:\footnote{The cage-shell factor's mechanism is cage-shell averaging on the shared 12-vertex icosahedron (working sketch \S17.10 refinement, Patch 0429), not Schur orthogonality on the bracelet's intrinsic 6-vertex $\Dsix$ orbit. Numerically these agree because $|\Dsix| = V_{\text{cage}} = 12$ (the K3 paper's $V_{\text{cage}} = |\Dsix|$ ``non-coincidence'', \S\ref{sec:cage_shell_factor}), but the cage-shell interpretation is the structurally correct one consistent with Substrate-Locality Unification (both sectors share the icosahedral cage).}
\begin{equation}\label{eq:w_cage_shell_factor}
|M_\perp^W| \;=\; \frac{d_\Gamma}{V_{\text{cage}}} \;=\; \frac{2}{12} \;=\; \frac{1}{6}.
\end{equation}

\textbf{Composite.} Substituting Eqs.~\eqref{eq:w_amplitude_factor}--\eqref{eq:w_cage_shell_factor} into Eq.~\eqref{eq:w_matrix_element_factorization}:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:w_composite_result}
|M^W| \;=\; \chi \cdot \frac{1}{6} \;=\; \frac{\chi}{6} \;=\; \frac{\phig^{-3}}{6} \;\approx\; 0.0394.
\end{equation}

The W-bracelet's chirality matrix element is \emph{identical} to the K3-doublet's $|M^{\Kthree}| = \chi/6$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we}. The cross-sector unification is achieved at full Layer 3 numerical-content level.

\subsection{Cross-Sector Substrate Chirality Unification Theorem (THEO-SD-CHIR-1)}\label{sec:theo_sd_chir_1_statement}

The four-step proof chain assembled in \S\S\ref{sec:w_substrate_locality_unification}--\ref{sec:w_matrix_element} establishes the K3-doublet $\leftrightarrow$ W-bracelet cross-sector unification theorem.

\begin{theorem}[Cross-Sector Substrate Chirality Unification (THEO-SD-CHIR-1)]\label{thm:theo_sd_chir_1}
Under vertex-aligned Reading C (FI-C-RC-1 + FI-C-RC-2, \S\ref{sec:h4_i4}) with the local-$I_h$-preservation theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation}) and the substrate-level identification $\chi \equiv \epsilon = \phig^{-3}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:chi_epsilon}) in force, the composite chirality matrix elements on the K3-doublet (mass-mixing sector) and the W-bracelet (electroweak V--A sector) are identical at full Layer 3 rigor:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:theo_sd_chir_1}
|M^{\Kthree}| \;=\; |M^W| \;=\; \frac{\chi}{6} \;=\; \frac{\phig^{-3}}{6} \;\approx\; 0.0394.
\end{equation}
The unification is mediated by the four-step proof chain: (i) local-$I_h$-preservation at $v_{\text{host}}$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation}); (ii) Substrate-Locality Unification on first-shell substrate objects (Corollary~\ref{cor:substrate_locality_unification}); (iii) cage-shell factor identity $|M_\perp| = 2/12 = 1/6$ on the icosahedral cage shared by both sectors (Eq.~\eqref{eq:w_cage_shell_factor}; identical to Lemma~\ref{lem:cage_shell}); (iv) pairing-convention identification via the icosahedral-center inversion $\zeta^W$ producing the $\sigma_1^W \zeta^W$-EVEN matter-doublet basis (Definition~\ref{def:zeta_w}; Lemma~\ref{lem:zeta_w_antipodal}; Eq.~\eqref{eq:w_doublet_basis}). The single substrate parameter $\chi$ feeds both sectors via two cage-shell averaging operations on the same 12-vertex icosahedral cage with the same effective 2D matter-doublet irrep dimension despite distinct sector-specific stabilizers ($D_{3d}$ at the K3-doublet's 3-fold axis; Petrie-polygon $\Dsix$ at the W-bracelet's Petrie hexagon).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Step (i): Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation} (\S\ref{sec:local_ih_preservation_theorem}). Step (ii): Corollary~\ref{cor:substrate_locality_unification} (\S\ref{sec:w_substrate_locality_unification}). Step (iii): The cage-shell factor on the shared icosahedral cage is $d_\Gamma/V_{\text{cage}} = 2/12 = 1/6$ for both sectors --- for the K3-doublet via Lemma~\ref{lem:cage_shell} (\S\ref{sec:cage_shell_factor}); for the W-bracelet via Eq.~\eqref{eq:w_cage_shell_factor} (\S\ref{sec:w_matrix_element}). Step (iv): The pairing-convention identification --- Definition~\ref{def:zeta_w} for $\zeta^W$ (\S\ref{sec:w_zeta_inversion}), Lemma~\ref{lem:zeta_w_antipodal} for the antipodal-pair stabilizer structure, Eq.~\eqref{eq:w_doublet_basis} for the W-bracelet matter-doublet basis (\S\ref{sec:w_doublet_basis}) --- produces the $\sigma_1^W \zeta^W$-EVEN doublet states $\PsiminusOne^W, \PsiminusTwo^W \in E_2 \oplus E_1$ on which the chirality operator $\Cchi^W \in B_2(\Dsix)$ has non-vanishing matrix element via Wigner-Eckart. The matrix element factorizes per Eq.~\eqref{eq:w_matrix_element_factorization} into amplitude factor $|M_{\text{amp}}^W| = \chi$ (Eq.~\eqref{eq:w_amplitude_factor}) and cage-shell factor $|M_\perp^W| = 1/6$ (Eq.~\eqref{eq:w_cage_shell_factor}), giving $|M^W| = \chi/6$ (Eq.~\eqref{eq:w_composite_result}). Combining with Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we} ($|M^{\Kthree}| = \chi/6$) gives Eq.~\eqref{eq:theo_sd_chir_1}. $\square$
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Programme-level registration of THEO-SD-CHIR-1]\label{rem:theo_sd_chir_1_registration}
THEO-SD-CHIR-1 was registered programme-level as theorem \#63 in the CPP theorem-registry (Patch 0434 Session 132) under the four-condition test pattern established by THEO-CAP-1 (Patch 0397): rigorous proof chain, numerical verification, empirical prediction validated, honest scope-limitation framing~\cite{abshier_theorem_registry}. The theorem-registry entry inherits THEO-CAP-1's foundational input stack FI-C-1 through FI-C-10 plus the two Reading C foundational inputs FI-C-RC-1 + FI-C-RC-2; AXIM-1, AXIM-2, AXIM-4, AXIM-7 are most load-bearing per Picture~B substrate-orientation-field framework. Honest scope-limitation: a Q5-PAIRING register-then-resolve pattern (Patches 0428$\to$0429 in the working sketch~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch}, sketch \S16$\to$\S17) preserved the analytical state of the pairing-convention question through Layer 2 conservative composite closure before Layer 3 resolution via the icosahedral-center inversion identification of \S\ref{sec:w_zeta_inversion}. Open verification at Layer 4: SF-2 V--A coupling continuum-EFT closure (OPEN-FP-SF-2-CHIR registered in \texttt{Research\_Frontier.md}~\cite{abshier_research_frontier}) delivers the 75\%-finite-mass to 100\%-massless-helicity kinematic projection from the substrate-physics handle $\chi/6$ of Eq.~\eqref{eq:theo_sd_chir_1}; the v2.0 paper presents the substrate handle, not the Layer 4 kinematic projection.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Cross-sector implications: three-way unification anticipated}\label{sec:cross_sector_w_implications}

THEO-SD-CHIR-1 establishes that the K3-doublet and W-bracelet sectors share substrate chirality content at the matrix-element-content level: a single substrate parameter $\chi = \phig^{-3}$ feeds both manifestations through structurally identical algebraic content (amplitude $\chi$ + cage-shell $1/6$) on isomorphic-but-distinct $\Dsix$ sub-stabilizers of the substrate residual $H_3 = I_h$. The OPEN-SD-CHIR-PRIMITIVE umbrella~\cite{abshier_research_frontier} registers this as the first of five anticipated cross-sector unifications:

\begin{itemize}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item \textbf{(i) Mass-mixing chirality.} Capotauro K3-doublet sector; $|M^{\Kthree}| = \chi/6$ via Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we}. Unified with (ii) by THEO-SD-CHIR-1.
    \item \textbf{(ii) Electroweak V--A.} SF-2 W-bracelet sector; $|M^W| = \chi/6$ via Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_1}. Unified with (i) by THEO-SD-CHIR-1.
    \item \textbf{(iii) Electromagnetic handedness.} SM-2 qDP/eDP polarization patterns. Cross-sector closure registered programme-level as THEO-SD-CHIR-2 (theorem \#64, Patch 0440 Session 133)~\cite{abshier_theorem_registry}; flagship-paper-section presentation in the forthcoming \S6 v2.0 qDP/eDP sector. Predicted $|M^{qDP}| = \chi/6$ identical to (i) and (ii) at full Layer 3 rigor; three-way unification achieved at substrate level.
    \item \textbf{(iv) Thermodynamic causal arrow.} Future-window work under the umbrella; sector-specific $\zeta$ generator and antipodal-pair structure to be identified.
    \item \textbf{(v) Cosmological-vacuum asymmetry.} Future-window work under the umbrella; sector-specific $\zeta$ generator and antipodal-pair structure to be identified.
\end{itemize}

The three-step closure pattern --- substrate-locality at Layer 2 (Corollary~\ref{cor:substrate_locality_unification}); sector-specific cage-shell factor at Layer 3 via cage-shell averaging on the shared icosahedral cage; sector-specific pairing-convention identification at Layer 3 via analog perpendicular-wavefunction structure (Definition~\ref{def:zeta_w} pattern) --- is structurally complete across the first three manifestations (mass-mixing K3 / electroweak V--A W / electromagnetic qDP-eDP) with identical numerical content $\chi/6 \approx 0.0394$ at substrate level. The umbrella's \emph{unification-at-magnitude-level claim} (a single number $\chi/6$ across structurally distinct sector mechanisms with distinct $\zeta$ generators) is the substantive cross-sector unification result that v2.0 presents at flagship-paper rigor; the v1.0 paper's K3-only $|M| = \chi/6$ prediction is a sub-result within this larger claim.

The primary empirical reading of $|M^W| = \chi/6$ at the V--A current level (Eq.~\eqref{eq:theo_sd_chir_1}) feeds the SF-2 V--A coupling continuum-EFT closure (OPEN-FP-SF-2-CHIR; the substrate handle $\chi/6$ being the substrate-physics input to SF-2's Layer 4 kinematic projection from finite-mass to massless-helicity limit) and constitutes a substrate-level prediction in its own right: the V--A current's intrinsic chirality magnitude at substrate level is the same $\chi/6 \approx 0.0394$ that the parity-violation asymmetry $\DeltapLR$ realizes on the K3-doublet sector (\S\ref{sec:dplr}). The v2.0 master predictions table (\S\ref{sec:dplr}, forthcoming rewrite) consolidates the cross-sector substrate-level predictions across all three manifestations into a unified prediction set.

\section{The qDP/eDP Sector: Three-Way Cross-Sector Unification}\label{sec:qdp_edp_sector}

The K3-doublet (\S\ref{sec:k3_doublet}--\S\ref{sec:composite_we}) and W-bracelet (\S\ref{sec:w_bracelet_sector}) sectors deliver $|M^{\Kthree}| = |M^W| = \chi/6$ on the mass-mixing and electroweak V--A manifestations of the substrate chirality. This section extends the same substrate-physics machinery to a third manifestation --- the SM-2 qDP/eDP electromagnetic-handedness mechanism~\cite{abshier_sm2} --- and derives $|M^{qDP}| = \chi/6$, completing the three-way cross-sector unification at substrate level under the OPEN-SD-CHIR-PRIMITIVE umbrella~\cite{abshier_research_frontier}. The result is registered programme-level as THEO-SD-CHIR-2 (qDP/eDP Sector Substrate Chirality Closure Theorem; theorem \#64 in the CPP theorem-registry~\cite{abshier_theorem_registry}, registered Patch 0440 Session 133); the four-step proof chain is developed in working sketch \S18--\S20~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch}.

The qDP/eDP sector exposes a structural element that the K3-doublet $\leftrightarrow$ W-bracelet pair did not surface: the chirality coupling involves not only substrate-geometric content (vertex direction) but also substrate-charge content (qCP-sign). The pairing-convention $\mathbb{Z}_2$ generator for this sector --- the analog of K3's $\zeta$ and W-bracelet's $\zeta^W$ --- is therefore not a pure geometric inversion but the \emph{combined $CP$ operation} (host-CP-centered spatial inversion + $\hat{n}$-flip + qCP-sign flip). This is the substantive structural refinement of v2.0 that the W-bracelet sector alone could not produce: the three-step closure pattern (substrate-locality + cage-shell factor + pairing-convention identification) is confirmed as robust across substrate objects of distinct vertex-counts (3 for K3, 6 for W-bracelet, 1 for qDP, 0 for eDP) and stabilizer types ($D_{3d}$, $\Dsix$, $C_{5v}$/$D_{5d}$, $I_h$) with structurally distinct $\zeta$ generators (pure geometric inversion for K3/W; combined $CP$ for qDP) but identical numerical magnitude $\chi/6 \approx 0.0394$ at substrate level.

\subsection{SM-2 qDP/eDP substrate characterization: Linear vs Orbital ZBW}\label{sec:sm2_zbw_characterization}

Per SM-2 v1.0 \S5 + \S6 + Glossary~\cite{abshier_sm2}, the qDP/eDP distinction in CPP is a \emph{ZBW-configuration-type distinction}, not a Dipole-Point-particle-type distinction. Both qDP and eDP refer to the same fundamental object --- a Dipole Point (DP) in the polarized DP cloud around a central CP --- but in different Zitterbewegung (ZBW) configurations distinguished by the number of unbound substrate dimensions $d$:

\begin{itemize}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
