    \item \textbf{Orbital ZBW DP (eDP).} $d = 0$ unbound dimensions ($\sigma = 1$). The DP is bound to the central CP in a spherically symmetric orbital state with no preferred substrate direction. Present in all fermions (charged leptons and quarks); produces spin and magnetic moment via SM-2~\S5.
    \item \textbf{Linear ZBW DP (qDP).} $d = 1$ unbound dimension ($\sigma \approx 8.3 \times 10^{-3}$). The DP extends along a single linear axis through the substrate, picking out one preferred direction. Present only in down-type quarks via SM-2~\S10; contributes the second $1/3$ charge reduction giving $-1/3$ for down-type vs $+2/3$ for up-type.
\end{itemize}

The SM-2~\S10 mechanism for the up/down asymmetry is the Capotauro chiral-polarity bias~\cite{abshier_sm2}: the 600-cell's intrinsic chirality, activated during the Capotauro symmetry-breaking event, preferentially stabilizes Linear ZBW configurations on $-$qCP centers. This is the substrate-level claim that the qDP/eDP sector verifies: the Linear ZBW configuration's energetic stabilization is sourced from the same substrate chirality magnitude $\chi = \phig^{-3}$ that underwrites the K3-doublet and W-bracelet sectors, via the Substrate-Locality Unification framework of \S\ref{sec:w_substrate_locality_unification}.

\subsection{The qDP and eDP substrate objects on the first-shell icosahedron}\label{sec:qdp_edp_substrate_objects}

Under vertex-aligned Reading C (\S\ref{sec:h4_i4}) with the central CP positioned at the host vertex $v_{\text{host}}$ of a 600-cell vertex, the qDP and eDP configurations are characterized as substrate objects on the first-shell icosahedron:

\textbf{qDP (Linear ZBW): 1-vertex first-shell subset.} The Linear ZBW DP's $d = 1$ unbound dimension extends along a single first-shell vertex direction. The natural substrate-object characterization is the \emph{1-vertex subset} $\{v_i\}$ of the first-shell icosahedron, where $v_i$ is the specific first-shell vertex direction along which the Linear extra points. The orbit-stabilizer theorem gives the qDP's stabilizer within the local $I_h$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qdp_c5v_stabilizer}
|G_{qDP}| \;=\; |I_h|/|\text{vertex orbit}| \;=\; 120/12 \;=\; 10,
\end{equation}
identifying the point group of order 10 fixing one icosahedral vertex as $C_{5v} \cong \mathbb{Z}_5 \rtimes \mathbb{Z}_2$ (the 5-fold rotation around the vertex axis together with 5 reflections through containing meridian planes).

\textbf{eDP (Orbital ZBW): trivial subset.} The Orbital ZBW DP's $d = 0$ unbound dimensions corresponds to a spherically symmetric orbital state with no preferred substrate direction. The substrate-object characterization is the \emph{trivial subset} of first-shell vertices --- the symmetric configuration over all 12 first-shell vertices with no orientational preference. The eDP's stabilizer is the full local symmetry group: $G_{eDP} = I_h$ of order 120.

\textbf{Substrate-Locality Unification applied.} Both qDP and eDP substrate objects are first-shell substrate objects in the sense of Corollary~\ref{cor:substrate_locality_unification}: qDP is a 1-vertex first-shell subset; eDP is the trivial (0-vertex) first-shell subset. Both inherit substrate chirality content from the global substrate identification $\chi \equiv \epsilon = \phig^{-3}$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:chi_epsilon} via cage-shell averaging on their respective stabilizer subgroups of the local $I_h$. The Substrate-Locality Unification framework applies uniformly.

\textbf{eDP as chirality-neutral reference.} Under cage-shell averaging on the eDP's full $I_h$ stabilizer with the chirality operator transforming as $A_u(I_h)$ (pseudoscalar, the operator's $I_h$ irrep per the Reading C $\hat{n}$-sourced transformation properties), the matrix element vanishes by group-theoretic orthogonality: $A_g \otimes A_u \otimes A_g = A_u \not\supset A_g$ in $I_h$. The Orbital ZBW configuration carries zero substrate chirality content at substrate level --- it is the \emph{chirality-neutral reference} configuration against which the symmetry-breaking Linear ZBW configuration's chirality magnitude is measured. The non-vanishing qDP-sector chirality magnitude derives from the Linear-ZBW configuration's $C_{5v}$ (refined to $D_{5d}$, \S\ref{sec:q6_pairing_registration}) stabilizer.

\subsection{The vanishing-matrix-element complication and antipodal-pair refinement}\label{sec:q6_pairing_registration}

Applying the §15--§17 Schur-orthogonality pattern naively to the qDP sector's $C_{5v}$ stabilizer: the qDP matter-state localized at $v_i$ transforms as the trivial $A_1$ irrep of $C_{5v}$ (the wavefunction at the stabilizer's fixed vertex is invariant under all $C_{5v}$ elements by definition); the chirality operator branches as $A_u(I_h) \downarrow A_2(C_{5v})$ (1D pseudoscalar, character $(1,1,1,-1)$ matching $A_2(C_{5v})$ exactly). The Wigner-Eckart matrix element $\langle A_1 | A_2 | A_1 \rangle$ is non-vanishing iff $A_1 \otimes A_2 \otimes A_1 \supset A_1$ in $C_{5v}$. Computing: $A_1 \otimes A_2 \otimes A_1 = A_2 \not\supset A_1$. \emph{The matrix element vanishes} under the naive 1-vertex framing.

The vanishing contradicts the SM-2~\S10 chiral-polarity-bias claim~\cite{abshier_sm2} of a non-zero substrate-level chirality coupling between qDP configurations on opposite-sign qCP centers. The resolution requires extending the qDP substrate-object characterization from a 1-vertex subset $\{v_i\}$ to a 2-vertex \emph{antipodal pair} $\{v_i, -v_i\}$, with stabilizer $D_{5d} \subset I_h$ of order 20 (the orbit-stabilizer theorem gives $|I_h|/6 = 20$ for the 6-antipodal-pair orbit on the icosahedron). The structural parallel is to the W-bracelet's antipodal-pair structure (\S\ref{sec:w_zeta_inversion}): the W-bracelet's 6 vertices factor as 3 antipodal pairs under $\zeta^W$ = icosahedral-center inversion; the qDP's antipodal-pair refinement factors the qCP-sign $\times$ vertex-direction state space under a generalized inversion that flips both. This is the qDP-sector analog of the \emph{Q5-PAIRING register-then-resolve} pattern that produced Definition~\ref{def:zeta_w} for the W-bracelet; for the qDP sector the analog open structural sub-question is \emph{Q6-PAIRING}, resolved in \S\ref{sec:qdp_zeta_definition}~\cite{abshier_capotauro_chiral_mechanism_sketch}.\footnote{Q6-PAIRING register-then-resolve pattern: working sketch \S19 (Patch 0438 Session 133) registered Q6-PAIRING as the open sub-question with three candidate resolutions (Candidate A: antipodal-pair $D_{5d}$ refinement with combined-$CP$ $\zeta$; Candidate B: matter-state to non-trivial $C_{5v}$ irrep, disfavored by SM-2 substrate physics; Candidate C: alternative chirality-operator irrep beyond $A_u(I_h)$, fallback-only); sketch \S20 (Patch 0439 Session 133) resolved toward Candidate A as developed in \S\ref{sec:qdp_zeta_definition} below.}

\subsection{The combined-$CP$ operation $\zeta^{qDP}$ (Finding C-W46)}\label{sec:qdp_zeta_definition}

The qDP-sector pairing-convention $\mathbb{Z}_2$ generator $\zeta^{qDP}$ is identified as the \emph{combined $CP$ operation} --- host-CP-centered spatial inversion combined with $\hat{n}$-direction flip combined with qCP-sign flip.

\begin{definition}[Combined-$CP$ operation $\zeta^{qDP}$]\label{def:zeta_qdp}
The qDP-sector $\mathbb{Z}_2$ generator $\zeta^{qDP}$ is the combined operation
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qdp_zeta_definition}
\zeta^{qDP}: \qquad v \;\mapsto\; -v, \qquad \hat{n} \;\mapsto\; -\hat{n}, \qquad \pm\text{qCP} \;\mapsto\; \mp\text{qCP},
\end{equation}
acting on the joint qCP-sign $\times$ Linear-ZBW-direction state space at the host CP. The three flips together preserve the substrate's overall chirality content (the Capotauro mechanism's three-way coupling --- vertex direction, $\hat{n}$ direction, and qCP-sign --- transforms consistently under $\zeta^{qDP}$).
\end{definition}

The structural justification for the combined-$CP$ identification: spatial inversion alone preserves qCP-sign in CPP (charge is parity-EVEN under bare spatial inversion), so spatial inversion alone cannot serve as the analog of the W-bracelet's $\zeta^W$ --- it would not produce a non-trivial pairing on the qCP-sign $\times$ vertex-direction state space and Eq.~\eqref{eq:qdp_zeta_definition}'s non-trivial pairing on the 4-state representation $\{|+, v\rangle, |+, -v\rangle, |-, v\rangle, |-, -v\rangle\}$ would not arise. The Capotauro mechanism's distinguishing feature is the qCP-sign $\times$ $\hat{n}$ coupling (SM-2~\S10: chirality bias preferentially stabilizes Linear ZBW on $-$qCP centers~\cite{abshier_sm2}); under any ``Capotauro-coupling-preserving'' transformation, qCP-sign and $\hat{n}$ must flip together. The natural such transformation is $CP$ --- spatial inversion combined with charge conjugation --- with $\hat{n}$-flip following from $\hat{n}$'s parity-ODD pseudoscalar nature (Reading C's $A_u(I_h)$ irrep assignment). This is a structurally richer pairing operation than the W-bracelet's pure 4D geometric inversion of Definition~\ref{def:zeta_w}: the qDP sector's chirality coupling involves both substrate-geometric content (vertex direction) and substrate-charge content (qCP sign), whereas the W-bracelet's coupling involved only substrate-geometric content.

\subsection{Matter-doublet basis under $\sigma_1^{qDP}\zeta^{qDP}$-EVEN pairing}\label{sec:qdp_doublet_basis}

The qDP matter-doublet basis is constructed by direct analog of the K3-doublet's (\S\ref{sec:k3_tbm_basis}) and W-bracelet's (\S\ref{sec:w_doublet_basis}) pairing conventions, with $\zeta^{qDP}$ in place of $\zeta$ or $\zeta^W$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qdp_doublet_basis}
|\Psi_-^{qDP,(1)}\rangle \;=\; \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigl(|+, v\rangle + |-, -v\rangle\bigr), \qquad
|\Psi_-^{qDP,(2)}\rangle \;=\; \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\bigl(|+, v\rangle - |-, -v\rangle\bigr),
\end{equation}
where $|\pm, \pm v\rangle$ denotes the configuration with qCP-sign $\pm$ at host and Linear-ZBW pointing along $\pm v$. The pair $\{|+, v\rangle, |-, -v\rangle\}$ are $\zeta^{qDP}$-conjugate (related by the combined $CP$ operation Eq.~\eqref{eq:qdp_zeta_definition}), so the EVEN/ODD combinations of Eq.~\eqref{eq:qdp_doublet_basis} span the analog of K3's and W-bracelet's matter-doublet 2D subspace.

\textbf{Transformation properties.}
\begin{itemize}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item Under $\zeta^{qDP}$ (combined $CP$): $|\Psi_-^{qDP,(1)}\rangle$ is $\zeta^{qDP}$-EVEN (symmetric under $|+, v\rangle \leftrightarrow |-, -v\rangle$); $|\Psi_-^{qDP,(2)}\rangle$ is $\zeta^{qDP}$-ODD.
    \item Under bare $\sigma^{qDP}$ (a perpendicular $C_2$ dihedral of $D_{5d}$): $v \to -v$ but qCP-sign unchanged. $|+, v\rangle \mapsto |+, -v\rangle$, which is \emph{not} in the matter-doublet basis --- it exits the 2D subspace.
    \item The matter-doublet 2D subspace is closed under $\zeta^{qDP}$ but not under bare $\sigma^{qDP}$. The relevant 2D subspace is the $\sigma_1^{qDP} \zeta^{qDP}$-EVEN projection, where $\sigma_1^{qDP}$ is the matter-state symmetric-EVEN/ODD distinction (analog of K3's $\sigma_1$ amplitude index and W-bracelet's $\sigma_1^W$).
\end{itemize}

Under $D_{5d}$ irrep decomposition, the 4-state representation $\{|+, v\rangle, |+, -v\rangle, |-, v\rangle, |-, -v\rangle\}$ decomposes as $2 A_{1g} \oplus 2 A_{2u}$ (each irrep occurring with multiplicity 2). The $\sigma_1^{qDP}\zeta^{qDP}$-EVEN pairing convention of Eq.~\eqref{eq:qdp_doublet_basis} selects a specific 2D subspace of $A_{1g} \oplus A_{2u}$ as the physically-relevant matter-doublet for the chirality coupling, exactly parallel to K3's 2D $E$-irrep doublet and W-bracelet's 2D $E_2 \oplus E_1$ subspace structure (Eq.~\eqref{eq:w_doublet_basis}).

\subsection{Chirality operator $\hat{C}^{qDP} \in A_{2u}(D_{5d})$ and matrix element}\label{sec:qdp_matrix_element}

The chirality operator at the qDP sector, sourced from the substrate primitive direction $\hat{n}$ (FI-C-RC-1, \S\ref{sec:h4_i4}), transforms under $D_{5d}$ with character $(1, 1, 1, -1, -1, -1, -1, 1)$ on the 8 $D_{5d}$ conjugacy classes $\{E, 2C_5, 2C_5^2, 5C_2, i, 2S_{10}, 2S_{10}^3, 5\sigma_d\}$ --- the $A_{2u}(D_{5d})$ assignment:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qdp_chirality_op}
\hat{C}^{qDP} \;\in\; A_{2u}(D_{5d}) \qquad \text{(1D ungerade irrep)}.
\end{equation}
The transformation properties: $\hat{C}^{qDP}$ is trivial on proper rotations $2C_5, 2C_5^2$ around the $v$-axis (rotational invariance), sign-flipping on the 5 perpendicular $C_2$ dihedrals (pseudoscalar), and $\zeta^{qDP}$-ODD on the inversion $i$ (under combined $CP$ both the pseudoscalar nature of $\hat{n}$ and the charge-conjugation factor contribute; the net effect is $\zeta^{qDP}$-ODD). Eq.~\eqref{eq:qdp_chirality_op} is the qDP-sector analog of the $B_2(\Dsix) = A_2(\Sthree) \otimes \sigma(\mathbb{Z}_2)$ assignment for K3-doublet (\S\ref{sec:chirality_observable}) and W-bracelet (\S\ref{sec:w_matrix_element}).

\textbf{Wigner-Eckart non-vanishing.} The composite chirality matrix element between the matter-doublet basis states $\langle \Psi_-^{qDP,(1)} | \hat{C}^{qDP} | \Psi_-^{qDP,(2)} \rangle$ is verified non-vanishing by $\zeta^{qDP}$-parity bookkeeping: $|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle$ is $\zeta^{qDP}$-EVEN, $|\Psi^{(2)}\rangle$ is $\zeta^{qDP}$-ODD, $\hat{C}^{qDP}$ is $\zeta^{qDP}$-ODD; the product (EVEN $\times$ ODD $\times$ ODD $=$ EVEN) contains the $\zeta^{qDP}$-trivial component required for non-vanishing matrix element. Equivalently, the matter-doublet's 2D subspace of $A_{1g} \oplus A_{2u}$ in $D_{5d}$ gives:
\[
\langle A_{1g} \,|\, A_{2u} \,|\, A_{2u} \rangle: \quad A_{1g} \otimes A_{2u} \otimes A_{2u} = A_{1g} \supset A_{1g} \quad \checkmark,
\]
non-vanishing.

\textbf{Matrix element factorization.} The matrix element factorizes via the same algebraic structure as the K3-doublet (\S\ref{sec:composite_we}) and W-bracelet (Eq.~\eqref{eq:w_matrix_element_factorization}):
\begin{itemize}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item \textbf{Amplitude factor.} Via chirality-eigenvalue matching (FI-C-7 analog of K3 Finding C-W23~\cite{abshier_capotauro_subclaim_c_sketch}): the matter-doublet's amplitude structure within the $\zeta^{qDP}$-pairing produces spectral radius $\sqrt{3}$ on the 2D doublet basis, identified with substrate primitive chirality eigenvalues $\pm \chi$ giving amplitude coefficient $b^{qDP} = \chi/\sqrt{3}$ and amplitude factor $|M_{\text{amp}}^{qDP}| = b^{qDP}\sqrt{3} = \chi$.
    \item \textbf{Cage-shell factor.} Via cage-shell averaging on the icosahedral cage shared with the K3-doublet and W-bracelet sectors (Corollary~\ref{cor:substrate_locality_unification}): the cage-shell factor is $d_\Gamma/V_{\text{cage}} = 2/12 = 1/6$ where $d_\Gamma = 2$ is the matter-doublet's effective dimension (2D subspace of $A_{1g} \oplus A_{2u}$ in $D_{5d}$, paralleling K3's 2D $E$-irrep and W-bracelet's 2D $E_2 \oplus E_1$ subspace) and $V_{\text{cage}} = 12$ is the icosahedral cage vertex count shared across all three sectors:
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:qdp_cage_shell_factor}
    |M_\perp^{qDP}| \;=\; \frac{d_\Gamma}{V_{\text{cage}}} \;=\; \frac{2}{12} \;=\; \frac{1}{6}.
    \end{equation}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Composite.} Substituting the amplitude factor and Eq.~\eqref{eq:qdp_cage_shell_factor}:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qdp_composite_result}
|M^{qDP}| \;=\; |M_{\text{amp}}^{qDP}| \cdot |M_\perp^{qDP}| \;=\; \chi \cdot \frac{1}{6} \;=\; \frac{\chi}{6} \;=\; \frac{\phig^{-3}}{6} \;\approx\; 0.0394.
\end{equation}
The qDP-sector chirality matrix element is identical to the K3-doublet's (Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we}) and the W-bracelet's (Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_1}).

\subsection{qDP/eDP Sector Substrate Chirality Closure Theorem (THEO-SD-CHIR-2)}\label{sec:theo_sd_chir_2_statement}

The four-step proof chain assembled in \S\S\ref{sec:qdp_edp_substrate_objects}--\ref{sec:qdp_matrix_element} establishes the qDP/eDP sector substrate chirality closure theorem.

\begin{theorem}[qDP/eDP Sector Substrate Chirality Closure (THEO-SD-CHIR-2)]\label{thm:theo_sd_chir_2}
Under vertex-aligned Reading C with substrate chirality magnitude $\chi \equiv \epsilon = \phig^{-3}$ from THEO-SD-CHIR-1's Substrate-Locality Unification (Corollary~\ref{cor:substrate_locality_unification}), the composite chirality matrix element on the qDP/eDP sector (SM-2~\S10 chiral-polarity-bias / electromagnetic-handedness mechanism~\cite{abshier_sm2}) is at full Layer 3 rigor:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:theo_sd_chir_2}
|M^{qDP}| \;=\; \frac{\chi}{6} \;=\; \frac{\phig^{-3}}{6} \;\approx\; 0.0394,
\end{equation}
identical to $|M^{\Kthree}|$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we}) and $|M^W|$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_1}). The unification is mediated by the four-step proof chain: (i) Substrate-Locality Unification applied to qDP/eDP via Linear ZBW $d=1$ as 1-vertex first-shell subset $\{v_i\}$ with $C_{5v}$ stabilizer and Orbital ZBW $d=0$ as trivial subset with full $I_h$ stabilizer (\S\ref{sec:qdp_edp_substrate_objects}); (ii) antipodal-pair refinement to $D_{5d}$ stabilizer resolving the naive $\langle A_1 | A_2 | A_1 \rangle$ vanishing (\S\ref{sec:q6_pairing_registration}); (iii) pairing-convention identification via the combined $CP$ operation $\zeta^{qDP}$ (Definition~\ref{def:zeta_qdp}) producing the $\sigma_1^{qDP}\zeta^{qDP}$-EVEN matter-doublet basis (Eq.~\eqref{eq:qdp_doublet_basis}); (iv) Wigner-Eckart factorization on $D_{5d}$ with $\hat{C}^{qDP} \in A_{2u}(D_{5d})$ (Eq.~\eqref{eq:qdp_chirality_op}) producing the composite Eq.~\eqref{eq:qdp_composite_result}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Step (i): \S\ref{sec:qdp_edp_substrate_objects}, applying Corollary~\ref{cor:substrate_locality_unification} to the qDP and eDP substrate objects. Step (ii): \S\ref{sec:q6_pairing_registration}, antipodal-pair refinement from $C_{5v}$ (vanishing $\langle A_1 | A_2 | A_1 \rangle$) to $D_{5d}$ (non-vanishing via the combined-$CP$ pairing). Step (iii): Definition~\ref{def:zeta_qdp} (Eq.~\eqref{eq:qdp_zeta_definition}) and Eq.~\eqref{eq:qdp_doublet_basis} (\S\ref{sec:qdp_doublet_basis}). Step (iv): Eqs.~\eqref{eq:qdp_chirality_op}--\eqref{eq:qdp_composite_result} (\S\ref{sec:qdp_matrix_element}); the matrix element factorizes via Wigner-Eckart on $D_{5d}$ into amplitude factor $|M_{\text{amp}}^{qDP}| = \chi$ (chirality-eigenvalue matching) and cage-shell factor $|M_\perp^{qDP}| = d_\Gamma/V_{\text{cage}} = 2/12 = 1/6$ (cage-shell averaging on the icosahedral cage shared with K3-doublet and W-bracelet), giving Eq.~\eqref{eq:theo_sd_chir_2}.
$\square$
\end{proof}

THEO-SD-CHIR-2 was registered programme-level as theorem \#64 in the CPP theorem-registry (Patch 0440 Session 133) under the four-condition test pattern of THEO-CAP-1 / THEO-SD-CHIR-1~\cite{abshier_theorem_registry}. The theorem-registry entry inherits THEO-SD-CHIR-1's foundational input stack (FI-C-1 through FI-C-10 from THEO-CAP-1; FI-C-RC-1 + FI-C-RC-2 from \S\ref{sec:h4_i4}) plus SM-2 v1.0 \S5+\S6+Glossary substrate characterization of Linear vs Orbital ZBW configurations as additional foundational input from the SM-line corpus~\cite{abshier_sm2}. AXIM-1, AXIM-2, AXIM-4, AXIM-7 are most load-bearing per Picture~B substrate-orientation-field framework; AXIM-1 additionally load-bearing for qCP-sign parity-EVEN intrinsic property in CPP (load-bearing for Definition~\ref{def:zeta_qdp}'s combined-$CP$ identification). \subsection{Three-way cross-sector unification: $|M^{\Kthree}| = |M^W| = |M^{qDP}| = \chi/6$}\label{sec:three_way_unification}

Combining Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we} (K3-doublet), Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_1} (K3-doublet $\leftrightarrow$ W-bracelet unification), and Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_2} (qDP/eDP closure):
\begin{equation}\label{eq:three_way_unification}
|M^{\Kthree}| \;=\; |M^W| \;=\; |M^{qDP}| \;=\; \frac{\chi}{6} \;=\; \frac{\phig^{-3}}{6} \;\approx\; 0.0394.
\end{equation}
A single substrate parameter $\chi = \phig^{-3}$, sourced from the substrate primitive 4D direction $\hat{n}$ (FI-C-RC-1) under the vertex-aligned reading $\hat{n} = v_{\text{host}}$ (FI-C-RC-2), feeds three observable manifestations of the substrate chirality at substrate level. The cross-sector unification is achieved at full Layer 3 numerical-content level across structurally distinct sector mechanisms.

\textbf{Manifestation comparison.} The three sectors are distinguished structurally by their stabilizer subgroups of substrate $H_3 = I_h$, their $\zeta$ generators, their matter-doublet $D_{5d}$/$\Dsix$/$D_{3d}$-irrep content, and their chirality operator irreps, but share an identical algebraic structure (matter-doublet effective dimension 2; chirality operator 1D ungerade; cage-shell factor $2/12 = 1/6$ on the shared icosahedral cage):

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lllll}
\toprule
Manifestation & Stabilizer & $\zeta$ generator & $\hat{C}_\chi$ irrep & $|M|$ \\
\midrule
(i) Mass-mixing (K3) & $D_{3d} \subset I_h$ & 3D inversion (K3 centroid) & $B_2(\Dsix)$ & $\chi/6$ \\
(ii) Electroweak V--A (W) & $\Dsix$ (Petrie hexagon) & 4D icosahedral-center inversion & $B_2(\Dsix)$ & $\chi/6$ \\
(iii) EM handedness (qDP/eDP) & $D_{5d}$ (antipodal pair) & combined $CP$ & $A_{2u}(D_{5d})$ & $\chi/6$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\textbf{Unification-at-magnitude-level claim.} The OPEN-SD-CHIR-PRIMITIVE umbrella registers a structurally distinct claim from a mere mechanism-level unification: the cross-sector unification asserts not just that all three sectors source their chirality content from the same substrate primitive direction $\hat{n}$ (mechanism-level unification, achieved at Layer 2 via Corollary~\ref{cor:substrate_locality_unification}, Finding C-W40), but additionally that all three sectors produce the \emph{same numerical magnitude} $\chi/6$ at substrate level despite structurally distinct $\zeta$ generators and stabilizer types (magnitude-level unification, achieved at Layer 3 via Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_1} for (i)+(ii) and Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_2} for (iii)). The magnitude-level claim is the stronger and more substantive: it predicts that the empirical chirality content of mass-mixing parity-violation, electroweak V--A coupling, and electromagnetic-handedness asymmetries all read $\chi/6 \approx 0.0394$ as their substrate-physics handle, prior to sector-specific Layer 4 kinematic projections to observable scales.

\textbf{Five-manifestation umbrella prediction.} The OPEN-SD-CHIR-PRIMITIVE umbrella anticipates two additional observable manifestations of the substrate chirality at substrate level~\cite{abshier_research_frontier}:

\begin{itemize}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item \textbf{(iv) Thermodynamic causal arrow.} Future-window work; sector-specific $\zeta$ generator likely involves PCD-cycle ordering / DI-bit propagation direction.
    \item \textbf{(v) Cosmological-vacuum asymmetry.} Future-window work; sector-specific $\zeta$ generator likely involves the Capotauro-nucleation-event sign-selection mechanism (related to OPEN-SM-4 sub-claim (a), \S\ref{sec:open_subclaim_a}).
\end{itemize}

The three-step closure pattern templated by the K3-doublet, W-bracelet, and qDP/eDP sectors --- substrate-locality at Layer 2; sector-specific cage-shell factor at Layer 3 via cage-shell averaging on the shared icosahedral cage; sector-specific pairing-convention identification at Layer 3 via analog $\mathbb{Z}_2$ generator and antipodal-pair structure --- is anticipated to extend to manifestations (iv) and (v) under the same structural pattern with sector-specific $\zeta$ generators yet to be identified. The umbrella's eventual full closure prediction: $|M^{\text{(iv)}}| = |M^{\text{(v)}}| = \chi/6$ at substrate level, extending the three-way unification of Eq.~\eqref{eq:three_way_unification} to five-way unification across all observable manifestations of the substrate chirality.

\section{Master Predictions: Cross-Sector Substrate-Physics Handles}\label{sec:dplr}

This section consolidates the v2.0 paper's empirical-content claims across the three observable manifestations of substrate chirality unified at substrate level by Theorems~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we} (K3-doublet; THEO-CAP-1), \ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_1} (K3-doublet $\leftrightarrow$ W-bracelet; THEO-SD-CHIR-1), and \ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_2} (qDP/eDP; THEO-SD-CHIR-2). The K3-doublet sector delivers the paper's \emph{primary empirical prediction} at flagship-paper rigor: the parity-violation asymmetry $\DeltapLR = \chi/6 \approx 0.0394$, validated within $2\%$ of the leptogenesis-back-derived empirical anchor $\sim 0.04$ (\S\S\ref{sec:dplr_observable}--\ref{sec:nontriviality_caveats}, preserved at v1.0 content with v2.0 framing extension). The W-bracelet (\S\ref{sec:w_bracelet_prediction}) and qDP/eDP (\S\ref{sec:qdp_edp_prediction}) sectors deliver substrate-physics-handle predictions $|M^W| = |M^{qDP}| = \chi/6 \approx 0.0394$ at substrate level; these feed sector-specific Layer 4 continuum-EFT kinematic projections in sibling-flagship-paper work (SF-2 v2.0+ for the W-bracelet's V--A coupling at the massless helicity limit; SM-2 v2.0+ for the qDP/eDP chiral-polarity-bias EFT continuum-limit calculation) registered as open work in the Research Frontier~\cite{abshier_research_frontier}. The cross-sector predictions consolidation (\S\ref{sec:cross_sector_predictions}) collects the three sectors' substrate handles into a single prediction set; the future-window predictions (\S\ref{sec:future_window_predictions}) forward-point to manifestations (iv) thermodynamic causal arrow and (v) cosmological-vacuum asymmetry under the OPEN-SD-CHIR-PRIMITIVE umbrella's eventual five-way closure.

\subsection{The parity-violation asymmetry observable}\label{sec:dplr_observable}

The parity-violation asymmetry $\DeltapLR$ in CPP measures the difference between left-handed and right-handed substrate-level chirality projections on the $\Kthree$-doublet of charged-lepton states. At substrate level, the observable is the expectation value of the chirality observable $\Cchi$ between the K3-doublet basis states $\PhiminusOne$ and $\PhiminusTwo$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:dplr_definition}
\DeltapLR = |\langle \Phi_-^{(1)} | \Cchi | \Phi_-^{(2)} \rangle|.
\end{equation}
The standard Wigner-Eckart reading identifies this matrix element with the parity-violation asymmetry directly --- the off-diagonal chirality matrix element between the two TBM-aligned $\Kthree$-doublet states encodes the substrate-level left-right asymmetry as a scalar magnitude.

The identification $\DeltapLR = |M|$ rests on three observations:

\begin{enumerate}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item \textbf{Off-diagonal structure}: a chirality bias produces an off-diagonal matrix element between states of opposite chirality. The K3-doublet basis $\{\PhiminusOne, \PhiminusTwo\}$ in the perpendicular-wavefunction extension (\S\ref{sec:perpendicular_wavefunction}) has $\PhiminusOne \propto \chiplus$ and $\PhiminusTwo \propto \chiminus$ with opposite $\zeta$-parity; the diagonal matrix elements $\langle \Phi_-^{(i)} | \Cchi | \Phi_-^{(i)} \rangle$ vanish by $\zeta$-parity selection.
    \item \textbf{Scalar magnitude}: the chirality observable's $B_2$ irrep content (\S\ref{sec:chirality_observable}) ensures the magnitude $|M|$ is a $\Dsix$-invariant scalar.
    \item \textbf{Substrate-to-observable transmission}: the matrix element transmits the substrate-vacuum chirality magnitude $\chi$ (FI-C-9) to the K3-doublet-level observable via the eight-step proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we}.
\end{enumerate}

\subsubsection{Candidate experimental signatures}\label{sec:experimental_signature}

\textbf{What would experimentalists measure?} The chirality matrix element $|M|$ is the K3-doublet-level parity-violation asymmetry transmitted from the substrate-vacuum chirality magnitude $|\chi| = \phig^{-3}$ through the cage-shell averaging factor $1/6$ to the substrate-physics observable scale $\DeltapLR$. At the substrate level it is the off-diagonal matrix element of the $B_2$-irrep chirality operator $\Cchi$ between the two K3-doublet basis states $\{\PhiminusOne, \PhiminusTwo\}$ on the icosahedral cage. At the phenomenological level it is the parity-violation asymmetry between left-handed and right-handed K3-doublet eigenstates of weak-interaction processes mediated by the doublet --- but \emph{the K3-doublet structure is not directly accessible in current flavor-basis observables}, because substrate-level chirality is averaged out in conventional weak-interaction measurements. The empirical anchor at $\DeltapLR \approx 0.04$ used in this paper is the leptogenesis back-derivation from the cosmological baryon asymmetry $\eta_B$ (\S\ref{sec:dplr_observed}); this is an inferred value one structural-numerical level removed from direct laboratory measurement.

\textbf{Four experimental access routes.} Direct K3-doublet-level measurement is not currently feasible. Four indirect access routes connect the substrate-level observable to measurable quantities, in order of inference-chain length from substrate to laboratory:

\begin{enumerate}\setlength\itemsep{0.3em}
    \item \textbf{Direct K3-doublet parity-violation} (presently inaccessible): precision parity-violation asymmetries in charged-lepton beta decays with K3-doublet structure resolved at the substrate level. Requires a precision-electroweak experimental programme not yet undertaken; the experimental signature exists in principle but not in current practice.
    \item \textbf{Indirect via precision electroweak} (HL-LHC Run 4--5, FCC-ee if built): W boson decay angular distributions and Z pole asymmetries at sub-percent precision probe the K3-doublet structure indirectly through the cage-shell coupling pattern inherited from SF-2's W bracelet $\Dsix$ stabilizer (FI-C-4). The chirality matrix element feeds into the W$^\pm$ catalyst framework; precision-electroweak constraints on the catalyst framework therefore indirectly constrain $\DeltapLR$. Loose at current precision; tightens with HL-LHC.
    \item \textbf{Indirect via precision PMNS} (JUNO, DUNE, Hyper-K): future SF-2 v2.0+ Q11 work (\S\ref{sec:open_q11}) will deliver the explicit $|M| \to \sin^2\theta_{13}$ relation at theorem level. Once that relation is closed, per-mille-precision PMNS measurements translate to constraints on $\DeltapLR$. Longer inference chain than route 2, higher experimental precision.
    \item \textbf{Cosmological back-derivation refinement} (Planck-class, CMB-S4): improved $\eta_B$ measurements tighten the leptogenesis back-derived anchor for $\DeltapLR$ below the current $\sim 10\%$ uncertainty (\S\ref{sec:dplr_what_2_percent_means}). Same inference framework as the current anchor, with reduced uncertainty.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Honest framing of the experimental landscape.} At the v1.0 ship, no route delivers direct K3-doublet-level $\DeltapLR$ measurement at the $\pm 2\%$ precision the Capotauro prediction warrants. The paper's primary empirical content is the structural-numerical consistency between $\chi/6 \approx 0.0394$ and the leptogenesis back-derived $\sim 0.04$ at the $2\%$ level (\S\ref{sec:dplr_what_2_percent_means}); direct K3-doublet measurement awaits future experimental work registered in \S\ref{sec:open_experimental_route}. Routes 2 and 3 are the most likely pathways to non-cosmological empirical engagement on the 2026--2035+ horizon, depending on which experimental programmes proceed.

\subsection{Predicted value}\label{sec:dplr_predicted}

Substituting Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we} into Eq.~\eqref{eq:dplr_definition}:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:dplr_predicted}
\DeltapLR^{\text{predicted}} = |M| = \frac{\chi}{6} = \frac{\phig^{-3}}{6} \approx 0.0394.
\end{equation}
The prediction is at \emph{zero free parameters}: every numerical input to Eq.~\eqref{eq:dplr_predicted} is either a foundational input (FI-C-9: $|\chi| = \phig^{-3}$; FI-C-10: $|M_\perp| = 1/6$) or a CPP axiom-derived structural fact (the Wigner-Eckart factorization on $\Dsix$). No fitted parameter enters the derivation.

\subsection{Observed value}\label{sec:dplr_observed}

The empirical anchor for $\DeltapLR$ at the CPP scale is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:dplr_observed}
\DeltapLR^{\text{observed}} \approx 0.04,
\end{equation}
inferred from the cosmological baryon asymmetry $\eta_B = (6.12 \pm 0.04) \times 10^{-10}$~\cite{planck_2018} via the standard leptogenesis back-derivation~\cite{abshier_grok_capotauro_2025}. The empirical anchor 0.04 is therefore not a direct measurement of any single laboratory observable; it is a parameter inferred from the cosmological baryon asymmetry under the leptogenesis framework. The historical Capotauro paper~\cite{abshier_grok_capotauro_2025} states the back-derivation explicitly: under the standard leptogenesis machinery with the K3-doublet of charged-lepton substrate states as the seesaw partner, the observed $\eta_B$ requires a chirality bias of approximately $0.04$ in magnitude.

\subsection{Agreement within $2\%$}\label{sec:dplr_agreement}

Direct comparison of Eq.~\eqref{eq:dplr_predicted} and Eq.~\eqref{eq:dplr_observed}:
\[
\frac{\DeltapLR^{\text{predicted}} - \DeltapLR^{\text{observed}}}{\DeltapLR^{\text{observed}}} = \frac{0.0394 - 0.04}{0.04} \approx -1.6\%.
\]
The agreement is at the $2\%$ level on the magnitude scale. The CPP programme's typical empirical-pattern matching at $1$--$2\%$ at integer counts and substrate primitives (SS-7 twelve nuclei to $1.5\%$; SM-9 top quark to $0.02\%$; SF-4 $\sigmanu = z^{-10}$ to $2\%$) is reproduced here: pattern strength at integer counts ($V_\text{cage} = 12$; $d_E = 2$) is the load-bearing empirical signal, not multi-decimal-place fitting precision.

\subsection{What $2\%$ agreement means}\label{sec:dplr_what_2_percent_means}

This subsection is explicit about the epistemic status of the $2\%$ agreement claim. Three honest framings:

\textbf{Framing 1: structural-numerical consistency, not precision laboratory test.} The empirical anchor $\DeltapLR^{\text{observed}} \approx 0.04$ is a parameter back-derived from $\eta_B$ via leptogenesis, not a direct measurement of a single laboratory observable. Consequently, the $2\%$ agreement is a consistency check at the structural-numerical level --- the substrate-derived $\chi/6$ value is consistent with the leptogenesis-back-derived $0.04$ at the level of precision afforded by the back-derivation framework. This is not a precision laboratory measurement of $\DeltapLR$ at the K3-doublet level; such a measurement would require a CPP-native parity-violation observable that is not yet identified.

\textbf{Framing 2: the back-derivation framework is the empirical anchor's source of uncertainty.} The $0.04$ value depends on the leptogenesis-equation parameters, the seesaw machinery, and the standard cosmological inputs. A change in any of these (e.g., refinements in $\eta_B$ from future Planck-class experiments; refinements in the leptogenesis-equation calibrations from precision-electroweak data) could shift the empirical anchor by $\sim 10\%$ either direction. The $2\%$ agreement at v1.0 is therefore meaningful at the structural-pattern level (predicted value is in the right ballpark with the right substrate-physics scaling) but should not be over-interpreted as precision-test passing.

\textbf{Framing 3: future direct-measurement falsifier.} The strongest falsification test of Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we} would be a direct laboratory measurement of $\DeltapLR$ on the K3-doublet of charged-lepton substrate states. Such a measurement is not currently available; identifying the experimental signature is registered as future work in \S\ref{sec:open_work}. When the direct measurement becomes available, the $\chi/6 \approx 0.0394$ prediction is sharply falsifiable: an observed value outside $\pm 2\%$ of the predicted value would falsify either Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we} (e.g., FI-C-10 is wrong) or FI-C-9 (chirality magnitude $\neq \phig^{-3}$).

The honest framing is registered explicitly because the alternative --- claiming $2\%$ precision-test passing without the back-derivation caveat --- would overclaim the empirical content of the v1.0 closure. The Capotauro paper's empirical prediction is one observable at one scale; the $2\%$ agreement is a meaningful but not definitive consistency check.

\subsection{Why this prediction is structurally constrained}\label{sec:nontriviality}

A common reviewer concern with empirical-match claims involving the golden ratio is that the framework may have enough flexibility to produce \emph{some} agreement with \emph{some} observable through post-hoc parameter selection. This subsection addresses that concern directly by enumerating the framework's degrees of freedom and showing that the prediction $|M| = \chi/6 \approx 0.0394$ is not the outcome of selecting parameters to match $0.04$ --- it is the outcome of three independently fixed structural inputs whose composition has no free parameter at v1.0.

\subsubsection{Enumeration of the framework's degrees of freedom}\label{sec:dof_enumeration}

The Capotauro chirality matrix element $|M|$ is built from three structural inputs:

\begin{enumerate}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item \textbf{The substrate primitive chirality magnitude $|\chi|$.} Fixed by FI-C-9 at $\phig^{-3}$. The selection $\phig^{-3}$ over $\phig^{-1}$ or $\phig^{-2}$ is argued forward from the perturbativity constraint on the chirality primitive (\S\ref{sec:chi_resolution}): the substrate's primitive chirality must preserve the geometric structure at zeroth order; only $\phig^{-3}$ is the first natural-distance-ratio scale at which this holds. The uniqueness argument (\S\ref{sec:chi_forward}) is independent of the empirical anchor.
    \item \textbf{The cage-shell averaging factor $|M_\perp|$.} Fixed by FI-C-10 at $d_E/V_\text{cage} = 2/12 = 1/6$ via Schur orthogonality. Both numbers are integer counts with zero free parameter: $d_E = 2$ is the dimension of the $E$-irrep of $\Sthree$ (a structural fact from group theory); $V_\text{cage} = 12$ is the number of vertices in the icosahedral cage (a structural fact from SM-1's four-cage taxonomy, fixed by 600-cell geometry). The factor $1/6$ emerges as the unique Schur-orthogonality coefficient on the cage's $\Dsix$ stabilizer.
    \item \textbf{The substrate-physics parameter $b$.} Fixed by the chirality-eigenvalue matching principle (\S\ref{sec:k3_amplitude_factor}) at $b = \chi/\sqrt{3}$. Once $\chi$ is fixed in input 1, $b$ is fixed by the eigenvalue-matching condition without any additional parameter.
\end{enumerate}

The composite matrix element $|M| = |M_{K_3}| \cdot |M_\perp| = \chi \cdot (1/6) = \chi/6 = \phig^{-3}/6 \approx 0.0394$ has \emph{zero free parameters}: every factor entering the composition is structurally fixed by foundational inputs or by axiom-derived structural facts. There is no calibration step at any stage of the derivation.

\subsubsection{Alternative outcomes the framework could have produced}\label{sec:alternatives}

To clarify the structural constraint, this subsection enumerates the alternative outcomes the Capotauro mechanism could have produced if any of the three structural inputs had been different.

\textbf{Different cage hosting the K3-doublet.} The icosahedral cage at $V_\text{cage} = 12$ is selected by the 600-cell's first-shell structure and SM-1's particle-type taxonomy. If a different cage hosted the K3-doublet --- e.g., tetrahedral $V = 4$, dodecahedral $V = 20$, icosidodecahedral $V = 30$ --- the cage-shell averaging factor would be:
\begin{itemize}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item $V_\text{cage} = 4$ (tetrahedral): $|M_\perp| = d_E/V_\text{cage}$ with $V_\text{cage} = |T_d \text{ or related stabilizer}| = 24$ giving $|M_\perp| = 1/12$, hence $|M| = \chi/12 \approx 0.0197$
    \item $V_\text{cage} = 20$ (dodecahedral): $|M_\perp| = 2/60 = 1/30$, hence $|M| = \chi/30 \approx 0.0079$
    \item $V_\text{cage} = 30$ (icosidodecahedral): $|M_\perp| = 2/120 = 1/60$, hence $|M| = \chi/60 \approx 0.0039$
\end{itemize}
None of these alternative outcomes would match the empirical anchor $\sim 0.04$. The icosahedral cage at $V_\text{cage} = 12$ is the unique cage assignment producing a Capotauro chirality matrix element in the right empirical range.

\textbf{Different irrep content of $\Cchi$.} The chirality observable transforms in $B_2$ of $\Dsix$ (\S\ref{sec:chirality_observable}), with K3-amplitude content in the $A_2$ irrep of $\Sthree$. If $\Cchi$ instead had $A_1$ content (the trivial representation; $d_{A_1} = 1$), the averaging factor would be $|M_\perp| = 1/12$ giving $|M| = \chi/12$. If $\Cchi$ had a higher-dimensional irrep content, the averaging factor would adjust correspondingly. The specific value $|M_\perp| = 1/6$ reflects the specific irrep content of the chirality observable, which is forced by the substrate's primitive chirality structure ($\zeta$-ODD assignment from Finding C-W11; $\sigma_1$-ODD K3-amplitude from \S\ref{sec:sigma1_odd_correction}). The chirality observable's irrep is not chosen --- it is forced by the physical interpretation of the operator.

\textbf{Different substrate chirality magnitude.} If $|\chi|$ were $\phig^{-1} \approx 0.618$, the predicted $|M|$ would be $\phig^{-1}/6 \approx 0.103$, off by factor 2.6 from observation. If $|\chi|$ were $\phig^{-2} \approx 0.382$, the predicted $|M|$ would be $\phig^{-2}/6 \approx 0.064$, off by factor 1.6. If $|\chi|$ were $\phig^{-4} \approx 0.146$, the predicted $|M|$ would be $\phig^{-4}/6 \approx 0.0243$, off by factor 1.6 the other direction. Only $\phig^{-3}$ produces $|M|$ in the right empirical range, and the $\phig^{-3}$ selection follows forward from the perturbativity constraint (\S\ref{sec:chi_resolution}).

\subsubsection{Structural constraint vs parameter fitting}\label{sec:constraint_vs_fitting}

The contrast between the present derivation and parameter fitting is sharp:

\begin{itemize}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item \textbf{Parameter fitting} would proceed: observe $\DeltapLR \approx 0.04$; adjust framework parameters until the prediction matches; declare success. Under parameter fitting, the framework has enough flexibility to match any empirical anchor (within its parameter ranges), and the match itself carries little structural information.
    \item \textbf{Structural derivation} (the present case) proceeds: fix the substrate primitive chirality from the perturbative-distance-ratio constraint ($\phig^{-3}$); fix the cage-shell averaging factor from group theory ($1/6$); fix the substrate-physics parameter from chirality-eigenvalue matching ($\chi/\sqrt{3}$); compose without further parameter choice ($\chi/6$); compare to empirical anchor. Under structural derivation, the prediction is either consistent with observation (as it is here at the $2\%$ level) or it isn't; there is no flexibility to ``tune'' the prediction toward observation.
\end{itemize}

The $2\%$ agreement at $|M| \approx 0.0394$ vs $\sim 0.04$ is therefore structurally restrictive, not numerologically suggestive: the framework's degrees of freedom are exhausted by the three structural inputs, none of which is freely adjustable.

\subsubsection{Probabilistic-rarity argument}\label{sec:probabilistic_rarity}

A complementary way to see the constraint: the structural inputs $(V_\text{cage}, d_E, |\chi|)$ are drawn from finite discrete sets. The cage assignment is among $\{4, 12, 20, 30\}$ (the four cages of SM-1's taxonomy). The irrep dimension is among $\{1, 1, 2\}$ (the $\Sthree$ irrep dimensions). The chirality magnitude scale is among $\{\phig^{-1}, \phig^{-2}, \phig^{-3}, \phig^{-4}, \ldots\}$ (the natural distance-ratio scales).

The number of structurally accessible $(V_\text{cage}, d_E, |\chi|)$ triples in the framework's natural range is on the order of $4 \times 3 \times 5 \approx 60$ combinations. Of these 60 combinations, only the specific $(V_\text{cage}, d_E, |\chi|) = (12, 2, \phig^{-3})$ produces a $|M|$ value within $\pm 10\%$ of the empirical anchor $\sim 0.04$. The probability of landing within $\pm 10\%$ by chance under uniform-distribution-over-natural-discrete-choices reasoning is therefore $\sim 1/60 \approx 1.7\%$ for $\pm 10\%$ matches and substantially smaller for the $\pm 2\%$ match actually achieved.

This is not a formal statistical claim --- the natural-discrete-choices have no formal prior --- but it captures the qualitative content: the Capotauro framework is structurally constrained to a small region of empirical-prediction space, and the match to observation at the $\pm 2\%$ level is meaningful rather than arbitrary.

\subsubsection{What this argument establishes (and does not establish)}\label{sec:nontriviality_caveats}

\textbf{What it establishes.} The $\chi/6 \approx 0.0394$ prediction at the empirical anchor $\sim 0.04$ is a structurally constrained empirical match, not parameter fitting. The structural constraint is sharp: the framework's degrees of freedom are exhausted by three foundational inputs, none freely adjustable.

\textbf{What it does not establish.} The argument does not establish that the framework is correct --- only that it is empirically nontrivial. Specifically: the structural derivation could still be wrong at the foundational-input level (FI-C-9 could be wrong; FI-C-10 could be wrong; the cage assignment could be wrong), and any of these failures would falsify Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we}. The argument establishes that \emph{if} the foundational inputs are right, \emph{then} the empirical match is constrained, not chance. Whether the foundational inputs are right is the v2.0+ sub-claim work (\S\ref{sec:open_subclaim_a}, \S\ref{sec:open_subclaim_b}, \S\ref{sec:open_fi_c10}).

The combination of structural constraint (this subsection) and honest framing of the back-derivation provenance (\S\ref{sec:dplr_what_2_percent_means}) is the v1.0 paper's full epistemic statement on the empirical match: the prediction is empirically nontrivial under the conditional foundational-input assumption, with explicit honesty about both what is conditional and what is empirically tested. The v2.0 cross-sector unification strengthens this epistemic statement substantially: the same substrate-physics handle $\chi/6$ now produces three independent sector-specific predictions, each with its own empirical anchor and each with the same structural-input chain. The probability-of-coincidence argument scales accordingly --- the v1.0 single-sector structural constraint at $\sim 1.7\%$ for a $\pm 10\%$ match becomes a substantially tighter joint structural constraint across three sectors at v2.0, although the cross-sector predictions for the W-bracelet and qDP/eDP sectors are presently at substrate-handle level rather than at direct empirical-anchor level (\S\S\ref{sec:w_bracelet_prediction}--\ref{sec:qdp_edp_prediction}).

\subsection{W-bracelet sector substrate handle: V--A coupling chirality magnitude}\label{sec:w_bracelet_prediction}

The W-bracelet sector (\S\ref{sec:w_bracelet_sector}, Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_1}) delivers a second substrate-physics-handle prediction: the V--A coupling chirality magnitude at substrate level is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:w_substrate_handle}
|M^W|_{\text{substrate}} \;=\; \frac{\chi}{6} \;=\; \frac{\phig^{-3}}{6} \;\approx\; 0.0394,
\end{equation}
identical in magnitude to the K3-doublet's $|M^{\Kthree}|$ via the Substrate-Locality Unification of Corollary~\ref{cor:substrate_locality_unification}. The V--A coupling in $W^\pm$-mediated electroweak interactions is the SF-2 v1.0 flagship paper's central content~\cite{abshier_sf2} (Theorem THEO-SF-2-5, PROP-SF-2-5, OPEN-FP-SF-2-CHIR); the substrate handle of Eq.~\eqref{eq:w_substrate_handle} is the substrate-physics input to that paper's kinematic projection from finite-mass V--A coupling at $\sim 75\%$ to the massless-helicity-limit V--A coupling at $100\%$.

\subsubsection{Substrate handle vs Layer 4 kinematic observable}\label{sec:w_layer4_distinction}

The Wigner-Eckart factorization of Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_1} delivers the chirality matrix element on the W-bracelet's substrate-level matter-doublet basis $\{\PsiminusOne^W, \PsiminusTwo^W\}$ at the icosahedral cage of the host vertex. The corresponding empirical V--A coupling observable in laboratory measurements (e.g., charged-current pion decay $\pi^+ \to \mu^+ \nu_\mu$; muon decay $\mu^+ \to e^+ \nu_e \bar{\nu}_\mu$ with $V{-}A$ chirality structure; $W^\pm$ decay angular distributions at LHC) is the \emph{kinematic projection} of this substrate handle to the observable-scale fermion-mass and helicity-eigenstate basis.

The kinematic projection is the SF-2 v1.0 flagship paper's Layer 4 territory~\cite{abshier_sf2}: SF-2 establishes the V--A coupling structure at the helicity-eigenstate level via the W-bracelet's $\Dsix$ stabilizer, and registers OPEN-FP-SF-2-CHIR as the open Layer 4 work that closes the substrate-to-observable kinematic projection. The v2.0 Capotauro paper supplies the substrate-physics handle $\chi/6$ that this Layer 4 calculation requires as input; the explicit numerical V--A coupling at the massless-helicity limit is a downstream sibling-flagship-paper deliverable.

\subsubsection{Falsifiability of the substrate-handle prediction}\label{sec:w_falsifiability}

The substrate-handle prediction $|M^W|_{\text{substrate}} = \chi/6$ is falsifiable indirectly through three converging routes:

\begin{enumerate}\setlength\itemsep{0.3em}
    \item \textbf{SF-2 v2.0+ Layer 4 closure}: the kinematic projection of the substrate handle to observable-scale V--A coupling must reproduce the observed $V{-}A$ structure of weak-interaction processes at sub-percent precision. An incorrect substrate handle would propagate to the Layer 4 projection and produce kinematic-observable discrepancies. The route is unavailable until SF-2 v2.0+ delivers the Layer 4 closure (OPEN-FP-SF-2-CHIR).
    \item \textbf{Precision-electroweak constraints} (HL-LHC, FCC-ee if built): the W-bracelet substrate handle indirectly constrains the cage-shell coupling pattern in $W^\pm$-mediated processes at the sub-percent level. Precision constraints on the SF-2 cage-shell coupling provide a route to indirect constraints on $|M^W|_{\text{substrate}}$.
    \item \textbf{Cross-sector consistency with the K3-doublet}: if the K3-doublet primary empirical prediction $\DeltapLR = \chi/6$ is independently confirmed at experimental precision exceeding the leptogenesis back-derivation uncertainty (\S\ref{sec:dplr_what_2_percent_means}), the substrate-locality-unification claim $|M^W| = |M^{\Kthree}|$ becomes a structurally constrained joint prediction across two sectors, with deviation in either sector falsifying the cross-sector unification at Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_1} level.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{qDP/eDP sector substrate handle: chiral-polarity-bias magnitude}\label{sec:qdp_edp_prediction}

The qDP/eDP sector (\S\ref{sec:qdp_edp_sector}, Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_2}) delivers a third substrate-physics-handle prediction: the chiral-polarity-bias magnitude on the SM-2 mechanism's preferential stabilization of Linear ZBW configurations on $-$qCP centers at substrate level is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:qdp_substrate_handle}
|M^{qDP}|_{\text{substrate}} \;=\; \frac{\chi}{6} \;=\; \frac{\phig^{-3}}{6} \;\approx\; 0.0394,
\end{equation}
identical in magnitude to the K3-doublet's $|M^{\Kthree}|$ and the W-bracelet's $|M^W|$ via the three-way cross-sector unification of Eq.~\eqref{eq:three_way_unification}. The SM-2 chiral-polarity-bias mechanism is the substrate-physics origin of the down-type-quark $-1/3$ charge reduction (SM-2 v1.0 \S10~\cite{abshier_sm2}); the substrate handle of Eq.~\eqref{eq:qdp_substrate_handle} is the substrate-physics input to the SM-2 v2.0+ Layer 4 EFT continuum-limit calculation closing the substrate-level chirality magnitude to the numerical Linear-ZBW-on-$-$qCP stabilization energy at observable scales.

\subsubsection{Substrate handle vs Layer 4 observable}\label{sec:qdp_layer4_distinction}

The Wigner-Eckart factorization of Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_2} delivers the chirality matrix element on the qDP/eDP matter-doublet basis $\{\Psi_-^{qDP,(1)}, \Psi_-^{qDP,(2)}\}$ at the icosahedral cage of the host CP. The corresponding empirical observable in laboratory measurements is the stabilization energy of Linear ZBW configurations on $-$qCP centers (SM-2~\S10), which manifests in the up/down quark mass and charge asymmetries through the SM-2 mechanism's chain. The Layer 4 EFT continuum-limit calculation closing the substrate handle to numerical stabilization energy at observable scales is the SM-2 v2.0+ flagship paper's territory; the v2.0 Capotauro paper supplies the substrate-physics handle $\chi/6$ that this Layer 4 calculation requires as input.

\subsubsection{eDP as chirality-neutral reference}\label{sec:edp_neutral_reference}

The Orbital ZBW (eDP) substrate object is the symmetry-preserving reference configuration with full $I_h$ stabilizer (\S\ref{sec:qdp_edp_substrate_objects}). At substrate level the eDP's chirality matrix element vanishes by group-theoretic orthogonality ($A_g \otimes A_u \otimes A_g = A_u \not\supset A_g$ in $I_h$); this is not a separate substrate-physics-handle prediction but rather the \emph{reference baseline} against which the qDP's non-vanishing $|M^{qDP}|_{\text{substrate}} = \chi/6$ is measured. The eDP's chirality-neutral status is a structural prediction of the symmetry-breaking-vs-preserving distinction that the OPEN-SD-CHIR-PRIMITIVE umbrella's manifestation list templates: symmetry-breaking substrate objects (K3, W-bracelet, qDP) carry $|M| = \chi/6$; symmetry-preserving substrate objects (eDP) carry $|M| = 0$.

\subsubsection{Falsifiability of the qDP/eDP substrate-handle prediction}\label{sec:qdp_falsifiability}

The substrate-handle prediction $|M^{qDP}|_{\text{substrate}} = \chi/6$ is falsifiable indirectly through two routes:

\begin{enumerate}\setlength\itemsep{0.3em}
    \item \textbf{SM-2 v2.0+ Layer 4 closure}: the continuum-EFT projection of the substrate handle to observable-scale Linear-ZBW-on-$-$qCP stabilization energy must reproduce the empirically observed up/down quark mass and charge asymmetries. An incorrect substrate handle would propagate to the Layer 4 projection and produce mass/charge discrepancies; the route is unavailable until SM-2 v2.0+ delivers the Layer 4 closure.
    \item \textbf{Cross-sector consistency with the K3-doublet and W-bracelet}: paralleling \S\ref{sec:w_falsifiability} item 3, an independent confirmation of $\DeltapLR = \chi/6$ at experimental precision exceeding the leptogenesis back-derivation uncertainty would make the three-way substrate-locality-unification claim $|M^{qDP}| = |M^W| = |M^{\Kthree}|$ a structurally constrained joint prediction across three sectors, with deviation in any sector falsifying the three-way unification at Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_2} level.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Cross-sector predictions consolidation}\label{sec:cross_sector_predictions}

The three sectors' substrate-physics-handle predictions consolidate as a unified prediction set:

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lllll}
\toprule
Sector & Substrate handle & Theorem & Layer 4 route & Empirical anchor \\
\midrule
(i) K3-doublet & $|M^{\Kthree}| = \chi/6$ & Thm.~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we} (CAP-1) & direct: $\DeltapLR$ & $\DeltapLR \sim 0.04$ (leptogenesis) \\
(ii) W-bracelet & $|M^W| = \chi/6$ & Thm.~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_1} (SD-CHIR-1) & SF-2 v2.0+ & V--A massless helicity limit \\
(iii) qDP/eDP & $|M^{qDP}| = \chi/6$ & Thm.~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_2} (SD-CHIR-2) & SM-2 v2.0+ & up/down asymmetry \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\medskip

\textbf{Joint structural-prediction interpretation.} The single substrate parameter $\chi = \phig^{-3}$, sourced from the substrate primitive 4D direction $\hat{n}$ (FI-C-RC-1) under the vertex-aligned reading $\hat{n} = v_{\text{host}}$ (FI-C-RC-2), feeds three observable manifestations at substrate level with identical numerical magnitude. The cross-sector unification has \emph{zero free parameters}: every sector's substrate handle is derived from the same FI stack (FI-C-1 through FI-C-10 + FI-C-RC-1 + FI-C-RC-2) plus the same four CPP axioms (AXIM-1, AXIM-2, AXIM-4, AXIM-7) via structurally identical four-step proof chains (substrate-locality theorem + Substrate-Locality Unification + cage-shell factor on shared icosahedral cage + sector-specific pairing-convention identification). The framework's degrees of freedom are exhausted by the FI stack; no sector-specific parameter is freely adjustable.

\textbf{Strength of the joint structural prediction.} The K3-doublet sector (i) is the load-bearing empirical content: $\DeltapLR \approx 0.04$ as leptogenesis back-derivation matches the substrate handle $\chi/6 \approx 0.0394$ within $2\%$ at v1.0 / v2.0. The W-bracelet (ii) and qDP/eDP (iii) sectors provide structural-consistency checks: the substrate handle $\chi/6$ is identical across sectors despite structurally distinct sector-specific mechanisms ($\zeta$ generators 3D inversion / 4D icosahedral-center inversion / combined $CP$; stabilizer types $D_{3d}$ / Petrie $\Dsix$ / $D_{5d}$; matter-doublet structures $E(\Sthree)$ / $E_2 \oplus E_1$ / $A_{1g} \oplus A_{2u}$ as 2D subspace). The joint prediction is not the K3-doublet number $\chi/6 \approx 0.0394$ alone --- which a single-sector framework could deliver --- but the structural identity of the magnitude across three structurally distinct sector mechanisms, which a single-sector framework cannot produce. The v2.0 substantive content beyond v1.0 is exactly this magnitude-level identity across sectors.

\textbf{Substrate-handle vs Layer 4 distinction.} The unified prediction set distinguishes \emph{substrate-handle predictions} (the substrate-level chirality matrix element $|M| = \chi/6$ on each sector's substrate-physics matter-doublet basis) from \emph{Layer 4 kinematic-observable predictions} (the projection of the substrate handle to laboratory-observable scales via sector-specific continuum-EFT calculations). At v2.0, the substrate-handle predictions are at full Layer 3 rigor across all three sectors; the Layer 4 kinematic-observable predictions are at full Layer 3 for the K3-doublet (with the back-derivation framework providing the empirical-anchor uncertainty discussed in \S\ref{sec:dplr_what_2_percent_means}) and at substrate-handle-only level for the W-bracelet and qDP/eDP (with the Layer 4 closures deferred to SF-2 v2.0+ and SM-2 v2.0+ respectively). The v2.0 paper's primary empirical content is the K3-doublet's $\DeltapLR = \chi/6 \approx 0.0394$; the W-bracelet and qDP/eDP substrate handles are predictions in the sense that they fix the substrate-physics input to two pending Layer 4 calculations, but the explicit Layer 4 observable values are downstream-paper deliverables.

\subsection{Future-window predictions: manifestations (iv) and (v)}\label{sec:future_window_predictions}

The OPEN-SD-CHIR-PRIMITIVE umbrella anticipates two further observable manifestations of the substrate chirality at substrate level (\S\ref{sec:three_way_unification})~\cite{abshier_research_frontier}:

\begin{itemize}\setlength\itemsep{0.3em}
    \item \textbf{(iv) Thermodynamic causal arrow}: the substrate-level mechanism producing the macroscopic thermodynamic time-asymmetry from the underlying PCD (Polarize-Capture-Depolarize) cycle ordering and DI-bit propagation direction (AXIM-3, AXIM-5). Future-window work; sector-specific $\zeta$ generator likely involves PCD-cycle-reversal symmetry analog of $CP$ acting on the DI-bit propagation direction.
    \item \textbf{(v) Cosmological-vacuum asymmetry}: the substrate-level mechanism producing the cosmological matter-antimatter asymmetry at the Capotauro-nucleation-event scale (OPEN-SM-4 sub-claim (a); Q7 cosmological-timing sub-questions of the Reading C closure trajectory). Future-window work; sector-specific $\zeta$ generator likely involves the Capotauro-nucleation-event sign-selection mechanism with substrate-cosmological-coupling content.
\end{itemize}

The umbrella's five-way closure prediction: $|M^{\text{(iv)}}| = |M^{\text{(v)}}| = \chi/6 \approx 0.0394$ at substrate level, extending the v2.0 three-way unification of Eq.~\eqref{eq:three_way_unification} to a five-way unification across all five observable manifestations of substrate chirality. The future-window predictions are at \emph{anticipatory} status at v2.0 --- the three-step closure pattern (substrate-locality at Layer 2 + sector-specific cage-shell factor at Layer 3 + sector-specific pairing-convention identification at Layer 3) is templated, but the specific $\zeta^{\text{(iv)}}$ and $\zeta^{\text{(v)}}$ generators await sector-specific identification work in future v2.0+ patches or sibling flagship papers.

The strongest falsification test of the umbrella's structural claim would be a manifestation-specific Layer 3 closure that produces $|M^{\text{(iv)}}|$ or $|M^{\text{(v)}}| \neq \chi/6$ at substrate level; such a closure would falsify the Substrate-Locality Unification claim (Corollary~\ref{cor:substrate_locality_unification}) as the umbrella's structural foundation, requiring either a refinement of the unification claim or a re-classification of the failing manifestation as not within the umbrella's scope. The v2.0 paper registers the five-way prediction as the umbrella's eventual full closure target; the three-way achievement of v2.0 is the first concrete demonstration of the umbrella's structural pattern, with the two future-window manifestations as the remaining substantive closure work.

\section{$\sin^2\theta_{13}$ Posture: Re-scoped to SF-2 v2.0+}\label{sec:sin2theta13}

This section is the paper's critical reviewer-honesty section. The Capotauro mechanism's chirality matrix element $|M| = \chi/6$ on the $\Kthree$-doublet was originally hoped to feed directly into a derivation of the PMNS reactor mixing angle $\sin^2\theta_{13}$. A multi-session derivation attempt surfaced a fundamental obstruction: standard PMNS perturbation theory predicts quadratic scaling $\sin^2\theta_{13} \propto |M|^2 \approx 0.001$, off by factor $\sim 21$ from the observed $\sin^2\theta_{13} = 0.02203 \pm 0.00056$~\cite{nufit_60}. A candidate $\gamma$ structural observation $\sin^2\theta_{13} = b \cdot m_\perp = \chi/(6\sqrt{3}) \approx 0.0227$ matches observation within $1\sigma$, but it requires linear scaling in the substrate-physics parameters that no standard perturbation-theory framework provides. The principled response\footnote{Re-scoping decision reached at Session 101 Patch 0395 after a three-session derivation attempt at Sessions 99--101.} is to re-scope the $\sin^2\theta_{13}$ derivation to SF-2 v2.0+ where the appropriate framework can be developed; the Capotauro paper's primary empirical prediction remains $\DeltapLR$, not $\sin^2\theta_{13}$.

\subsection{The Sessions 99--101 trajectory and candidate $\gamma$ observation}\label{sec:gamma_observation}

After Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we} was formalized,\footnote{Theorem formalized at Session 98 Patch 0392; the natural follow-on PMNS derivation attempted Sessions 99--101~\cite{abshier_capotauro_subclaim_c_sketch}.} the natural follow-on derivation was attempted: from $|M| = \chi/6$ to $\sin^2\theta_{13}$ via the PMNS perturbation machinery. The numerical observation labelled \emph{candidate $\gamma$} emerged early in the trajectory:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:candidate_gamma}
\sin^2\theta_{13} = b \cdot m_\perp = \frac{\chi/\sqrt{3}}{6} = \frac{\chi}{6\sqrt{3}} \approx 0.0227.
\end{equation}
This matches the observed $\sin^2\theta_{13} = 0.02203 \pm 0.00056$~\cite{nufit_60} within $1\sigma$ --- a striking numerical coincidence that motivated significant Session 99--100 attention.

The structural reading of candidate $\gamma$: the substrate-physics parameter $b = \chi/\sqrt{3}$ from \S\ref{sec:k3_amplitude_factor} times the cage-shell averaging factor $m_\perp = 1/6$ from \S\ref{sec:cage_shell_factor} gives a linear-in-substrate-parameters quantity. The match within $1\sigma$ raised the possibility that $\sin^2\theta_{13}$ might emerge directly from the same substrate-physics inputs that produce $|M| = \chi/6$, without requiring additional foundational structure.

\subsection{The linear-vs-quadratic scaling tension}\label{sec:scaling_tension}

Sessions 99--100 tested the candidate $\gamma$ observation against standard PMNS perturbation theory and found a fundamental tension. The standard reading is that $\sin^2\theta_{13}$ at the PMNS level is the modulus-squared of a perturbation-amplitude:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:pmns_quadratic}
\sin^2\theta_{13} \propto |M|^2 \cdot \frac{2}{3} \approx (0.0394)^2 \cdot \frac{2}{3} \approx 0.00103.
\end{equation}
The factor $2/3$ in Eq.~\eqref{eq:pmns_quadratic} is the standard PMNS-perturbation coefficient on the K3-doublet-to-third-eigenmode coupling channel under the SM-5 inheritance framework. The result $\approx 0.001$ is off from observation by factor $\sim 21$.

Three resolution attempts were tested in Sessions 99--101 and ruled out:

\begin{enumerate}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item \textbf{Wavefunction-level coupling}: the hypothesis that $\sin^2\theta_{13}$ inherits from the wavefunction-level coupling $|\langle \nu_3 | \text{Capotauro perturbation} | \nu_1 \rangle|^2$ rather than the PMNS-level off-diagonal entry. Direct computation gives quadratic scaling at the wavefunction level too: $\sin^2\theta_{13} \propto |M|^2 / 4 \approx 0.000388$, off by factor $\sim 64$. Ruled out~\cite{abshier_capotauro_subclaim_c_sketch}.\footnote{Direct computation performed at Session 101 Patch 0395.}
    \item \textbf{Multiple-channel coherent sum}: the hypothesis that $\sin^2\theta_{13}$ aggregates over multiple coherent channels with destructive interference enhancing the leading-order amplitude. Tested across 22 candidate scalings combining linear and quadratic terms; none reproduces the observed $\sin^2\theta_{13}$ within $5\sigma$ without ad hoc parameter tuning.
    \item \textbf{Linear-amplitude reading of $\sin^2\theta_{13}$}: the hypothesis that $\sin^2\theta_{13}$ should be read as a linear-in-amplitude quantity rather than modulus-squared. This requires a CPP-specific perturbation framework in which the leading-order PMNS deviation from TBM is linear in the substrate-physics parameters; no such framework is available within Capotauro mechanism's direct scope.
\end{enumerate}

The 22 candidate scalings exhausted the parameter space accessible within the Capotauro mechanism alone, given the foundational input stack FI-C-1 through FI-C-10. None produces $\sin^2\theta_{13}$ at observed magnitude with rigorous derivation.

\subsection{The re-scoping decision}\label{sec:rescoping_decision}

The closure trajectory took the principled decision to re-scope the $\sin^2\theta_{13}$ derivation:\footnote{Re-scoping decision at Session 101 Patch 0395.}
\begin{quote}
\textbf{Q11 $\sin^2\theta_{13}$ derivation is re-scoped from Capotauro sub-claim (c) to SF-2 v2.0+ work.} The Capotauro mechanism provides $|M| = \chi/6$ as chirality coupling input to the PMNS sector; the precise relation $|M| \to \sin^2\theta_{13}$ is the SF-2 framework's responsibility.
\end{quote}

The re-scoping rests on three principled observations:

\begin{enumerate}\setlength\itemsep{0.2em}
    \item \textbf{Scope-of-mechanism}: the Capotauro mechanism's natural output is the substrate-level chirality matrix element $|M|$ on the K3-doublet. The connection from $|M|$ to PMNS-level observables (mixing angles, CP phase) requires the full PMNS perturbation machinery, which lives in the electroweak-sector flagship paper (SF-2). Re-scoping recognizes the natural boundary between substrate-level structural derivations (Capotauro) and PMNS-level perturbation derivations (SF-2).
    \item \textbf{Linear-scaling framework}: candidate $\gamma$'s $1\sigma$ match suggests a CPP-specific linear-scaling framework may exist. Such a framework would need independent structural motivation --- a substrate-physics argument for why the leading-order PMNS deviation from TBM should be linear in $\chi$ rather than quadratic. The argument is not available within the Capotauro mechanism's direct scope; it requires the broader SF-2 framework's machinery.
    \item \textbf{Conditional theorem closure discipline}: the conditional-closure framework~\cite{abshier_conditional_closure} requires either (a) full closure at the inherited FI level, or (b) explicit re-scoping with reviewer-visible registration of the residual open work. Pursuing $\sin^2\theta_{13}$ derivation at v1.0 without rigorous linear-scaling framework would violate condition (a); honest re-scoping per condition (b) is the principled choice.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{The numerical conjecture as guiding observation for SF-2 v2.0+}\label{sec:gamma_guiding}

Candidate $\gamma$ is registered as a structural observation guiding the SF-2 v2.0+ work on $\sin^2\theta_{13}$. The observation is:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:gamma_registered}
\sin^2\theta_{13} \stackrel{?}{=} b \cdot m_\perp = \frac{\chi}{6\sqrt{3}} \approx 0.0227 \quad \text{(within $1\sigma$ of NuFIT 6.0 } 0.02203 \pm 0.00056\text{)}.
\end{equation}
The question-mark superscript indicates that the equality is registered as a numerical conjecture awaiting rigorous derivation, not as a theorem-level statement. Future SF-2 v2.0+ work either delivers the rigorous derivation (yielding linear scaling from a substrate-physics argument and validating candidate $\gamma$ at theorem level) or honestly accepts candidate $\gamma$ as a numerical coincidence requiring a different mechanism for $\sin^2\theta_{13}$ at the empirical magnitude.

The Capotauro paper neither claims candidate $\gamma$ as a v1.0 theorem nor dismisses it as coincidence. Its empirical content at v1.0 is the single prediction $\DeltapLR = \chi/6 \approx 0.0394$ from Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we}; candidate $\gamma$ is forwarded to SF-2 v2.0+ as a registered structural observation.

\subsection{Why this is honest scope-limitation rather than ansatz-fitting}\label{sec:scope_limitation_honesty}

This subsection addresses the most likely reviewer concern explicitly. A skeptical reviewer might ask: \emph{``You originally hoped to derive $\sin^2\theta_{13}$ and now you're saying it belongs to SF-2 v2.0+ --- is this principled or convenient?''} The honest answer is: principled, for two reasons.

\textbf{The alternative would be ansatz-fitting dressed as derivation.} Pursuing $\sin^2\theta_{13}$ derivation within the Capotauro mechanism's direct scope at v1.0 would require either: (i) inventing a CPP-specific perturbation framework that produces linear scaling without independent structural motivation; or (ii) accepting candidate $\gamma$ as an empirical fit without rigorous derivation. Option (i) is unrigorous --- the framework would be chosen specifically to produce the observed result, which is parameter-fitting dressed as derivation. Option (ii) abandons the conditional-closure discipline that distinguishes CPP from phenomenological models. The honest path is re-scoping.

\textbf{The re-scoping is structural, not strategic.} The natural scope of the Capotauro mechanism is substrate-level chirality matrix elements ($|M|$) and direct observables that read those matrix elements (parity-violation asymmetries $\DeltapLR$). PMNS-level observables (mixing angles, CP phase) require additional machinery beyond the chirality matrix element --- the PMNS perturbation framework that lifts the K3-doublet degeneracy lives in the electroweak-sector flagship paper. Re-scoping recognizes this natural mechanism boundary, not a convenience.

The Capotauro paper at v1.0 takes the principled position: \textbf{the mechanism's direct empirical content is $\DeltapLR$ (validated within $2\%$); the connection to $\sin^2\theta_{13}$ is the SF-2 framework's responsibility}. Whether the SF-2 v2.0+ work validates candidate $\gamma$ at theorem level or finds a different mechanism for $\sin^2\theta_{13}$, the Capotauro paper's v1.0 substantive content stands.

\section{Cumulative Falsifier Set}\label{sec:falsifier}

This section enumerates the v2.0 falsifier set: eleven candidate falsifiers (one direct empirical at the K3-doublet sector; three framework-level inherited from v1.0; one cross-sector inherited from v1.0 covering downstream-paper inconsistencies; three cross-sector unification falsifiers NEW at v2.0 covering the substrate-handle predictions of Theorems~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_1}--\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_2} and the umbrella three-way unification claim; three Reading-C-foundational-input falsifiers NEW at v2.0 covering FI-C-RC-1, FI-C-RC-2, and the Substrate-Locality Theorem) plus an updated modular-failure-mode discussion and explicit notable absences. The expanded falsifier set defines the empirical and structural conditions under which Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we} (the K3-doublet primary content; THEO-CAP-1), Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_1} (the W-bracelet sector; THEO-SD-CHIR-1), Theorem~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_2} (the qDP/eDP sector; THEO-SD-CHIR-2), and the three-way cross-sector unification claim of Eq.~\eqref{eq:three_way_unification} would be falsified at v2.0.

\subsection{Direct empirical falsifier}\label{sec:falsifier_empirical}

\paragraph{Falsifier 1: $\DeltapLR$ outside $\pm 2\%$ of $\chi/6 \approx 0.0394$.}
A direct measurement (or improved back-derivation) of the parity-violation asymmetry on the $\Kthree$-doublet of charged-lepton substrate states inconsistent with the predicted value $0.0394$ at the $\pm 2\%$ level would falsify Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we} at the matrix-element prediction level. The current empirical state ($\DeltapLR \approx 0.04$ via leptogenesis back-derivation) is consistent with the prediction within the back-derivation framework's $\sim 10\%$ uncertainty (\S\ref{sec:dplr_what_2_percent_means}). Future direct measurement at the $1\%$ precision level would either confirm or falsify the prediction sharply.

\subsection{Framework-level falsifiers}\label{sec:falsifier_framework}

\paragraph{Falsifier 2: FI-C-3 K3-doublet basis structure error.}
The $\Kthree$ antibonding-doublet TBM-aligned basis at theorem level is inherited from SF-4 v4.0 Composite K3-Cage-Shell Coupling Theorem (THEO-SF-4-5)~\cite{abshier_sf4}. Independent re-derivation revealing the TBM-aligned basis to be incorrect at theorem level --- specifically, that the $\Sthree \to S_2$ branching rule does not uniquely select $\{\phiminusOne, \phiminusTwo\}$ at zeroth order --- would falsify FI-C-3 and therefore Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we}'s starting basis. The perpendicular-wavefunction extension $|\chi_\pm\rangle$ (Session 91, \S\ref{sec:perpendicular_wavefunction}) is part of the extended FI-C-3 and is also a falsification target.

\paragraph{Falsifier 3: Substrate primitive chirality magnitude $|\chi| \neq \phig^{-3}$.}
FI-C-9 (\S\ref{sec:order_parameter}) registers $|\chi| = \phig^{-3} \approx 0.236$ as the substrate's primitive chirality magnitude. A future first-principles derivation of $|\chi|$ from CPP axioms (sub-claim (b) work, \S\ref{sec:open_work}) yielding a value inconsistent with $\phig^{-3}$ --- for example $\phig^{-1} \approx 0.618$ or $\phig^{-2} \approx 0.382$ from a different structural argument --- would falsify FI-C-9. The historical reframing decision\footnote{Session 87 Patch 0381 reframing decision selected $\phig^{-3}$ over $\phig^{-1}$ and $\phig^{-2}$ on the basis of the perturbative-distance-ratio constraint on the substrate's primitive chirality (\S\ref{sec:chi_resolution}).} explicitly selected $\phig^{-3}$ over $\phig^{-1}$ and $\phig^{-2}$ based on the perturbative-distance-ratio constraint (\S\ref{sec:chi_resolution}); a different first-principles closure of sub-claim (b) might revisit this selection.

\paragraph{Falsifier 4: FI-C-10 cage-shell extension to chirality observables ruled out.}
FI-C-10 (\S\ref{sec:fi_summary}) extends the cage-shell coupling from mass observables (FI-C-6) to the broader class of $D_6$-equivariant observables on $\Kthree$-doublet states, including the chirality observable $\Cchi$. Future first-principles derivation from primitive CPP axioms (A3 DI-bit propagation + A4 Nexus connectivity, in SS-corpus territory) revealing that the extension does not hold at theorem level --- e.g., the cage-shell averaging factor for chirality observables differs from $d_E/V_\text{cage} = 1/6$ --- would falsify Lemma~\ref{lem:cage_shell} and therefore Theorem~\ref{thm:composite_cap_we}. This falsifier is registered as both an open theorem-level work item (\S\ref{sec:open_work}) and a falsification opportunity. At v2.0 the falsifier extends across all three sectors: a refutation of FI-C-10 at the cage-shell level would propagate through Theorems~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_1} and~\ref{thm:theo_sd_chir_2} (which share the icosahedral cage's $d_\Gamma/V_{\text{cage}} = 2/12 = 1/6$ factor via Substrate-Locality Unification, Corollary~\ref{cor:substrate_locality_unification}), falsifying the entire three-way unification of Eq.~\eqref{eq:three_way_unification} rather than only the K3-doublet sector.

\paragraph{Falsifier 9 (NEW at v2.0): FI-C-RC-1 ruled out --- primitive 4D direction $\hat{n}$ not substrate-foundational.}
FI-C-RC-1 (\S\ref{sec:h4_i4}) registers the primitive 4D direction $\hat{n} \in \mathbb{R}^4$ as substrate-foundational --- postulated on the same epistemic footing as the existence of the 600-cell substrate itself. A future Q1$'$+Q1$'$.A Layer 3 promotion of $\hat{n}$ (dynamical, causal, or energetic derivation of $\hat{n}$ from primitive CPP axioms AXIM-1, AXIM-2, AXIM-4, AXIM-7) revealing that $\hat{n}$ is \emph{not} substrate-foundational --- e.g., that it emerges from a substrate-dynamical mechanism producing a different magnitude or different structural relation to the 600-cell vertex lattice than FI-C-RC-1 predicts --- would falsify FI-C-RC-1 and propagate through the v2.0 derivation chain. Specifically, the substrate-level identification $\chi \equiv \epsilon = \phig^{-3}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:chi_epsilon}) depends on $\hat{n}$'s edge-perturbation magnitude under FI-C-RC-1's substrate-foundational status; a different mechanism producing $\hat{n}$ might yield a different identification of $\chi$ and falsify all three substrate-handle predictions of Eq.~\eqref{eq:three_way_unification} at substrate level. This falsifier is registered as the \emph{deepest v2.0 open work} (\S\ref{sec:open_work}) and a falsification opportunity.

\paragraph{Falsifier 10 (NEW at v2.0): FI-C-RC-2 ruled out --- vertex-aligned reading $\hat{n} = v_{\text{host}}$ fails at Layer 3.}
FI-C-RC-2 (\S\ref{sec:h4_i4}) registers the vertex-aligned reading $\hat{n} = v_{\text{host}}$ at Layer 2 via three converging arguments (kinematic, energetic, and substrate-physics). A future Q1$'$ resolution at Layer 3 (theorem-level derivation of which substrate object $\hat{n}$ aligns with under FI-C-RC-1) selecting a different reading --- e.g., $\hat{n}$ aligned with a 600-cell tetrahedral-cell center, or with an edge midpoint, or with no specific lattice substructure --- would falsify FI-C-RC-2 and propagate through the substrate-locality theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:local_ih_preservation}) and the three sector-specific theorems. The Q1$'$.A K3-base geometric realization (\S\ref{sec:h4_i4}, resolved at Layer 2 toward 3-vertex triangular face on the first-shell icosahedron) is also at falsifier risk under this scenario: a different vertex-alignment reading would relocate the K3-base on the substrate.
